# FreeCAD Migration — Implementation Plan

**Scope:** Roadmap Phases 3–7 — porting the generators to FreeCAD and the capabilities that
port enables. Plan abbreviation `FC`.

**Design authority:**
[doc/architecture/freecad_migration.md](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) for the system
shape and the nine use cases; [corner.md](../design/corner.md),
[bulkhead.md](../design/bulkhead.md) and [cowl.md](../design/cowl.md) for the geometry being
ported. All three parts now have one — `cowl.md` was written as IP-FC-7, immediately
unblocked IP-FC-16, and raised seven open questions about the cowl. **Five of those were
answered on 2026-08-09**, closing the last blocking cowl question (OQ-DES-CW6) and adding
IP-FC-42, IP-FC-43 and IP-FC-44.

**Last updated:** 2026-08-09

---

> **The hand drivers are not a source of truth about parameters.** `fuselage_corner.scad` and
> `fuselage_bulkhead.scad` each exercise one hand-written configuration, and their constants
> disagree with what the sweep derives — `extrusion_width` 0.4 against 0.6, `panel_offset` 0
> against 2.5, `panel_overlap` 4 against 4.7625. Several are not free at all: `panel.offset`
> and `panel.overlap` are computed by `derived_parameters()`, so setting one and not the
> others produces a combination the sweep would never generate, which renders without
> complaint and is wrong. Read design questions against derived values, and render variants
> with [`render_variant.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/render_variant.py) rather than `-D`
> overrides. Comparisons in this plan that use driver values are valid as *port equivalence*
> tests — identical inputs on both sides — and say nothing about design intent.

## Reading this plan

Items are ordered by dependency, not by phase. The first six are **unblocked and can start
in any order, today** — they were chosen deliberately: each one either answers an open
question that shapes later work, or delivers value that does not depend on the port at all.

Three things are worth noticing about the shape of the plan:

- **IP-FC-4 (OML as a surface) is on the critical path and depends on nothing.** Until the
  OML is a surface rather than a 36 MB tessellated mesh, the cowls cannot be solid models,
  and Phases 4–6 would deliver B-rep export, assemblies and drawings with the cowls
  silently excluded.
- **IP-FC-1 decides an architecture, not a setting.** If `freecadcmd` startup is small next
  to a part's build time, the entire existing sweep driver survives with one call swapped.
  If it is not, the queue, the worker budget, the atomic writes and the retry path all need
  rework. Measure before building.
- **Four items are blocked only by open questions**, not by other work. Those OQs are
  answerable by design decisions rather than by code, so they are the cheapest way to widen
  the front.

---

| IP-FC-32 | done | **They do, and the tier is built.** Measured 2026-08-18 across five builds in five separate `freecadcmd` processes and two part kinds: the bulkhead's **150 `.brp` members hash identically across three runs**, the corner's **84 across two**, and the exported STL is byte-identical in both kinds. So this tier is **exact — it needs no tolerance at all**, which no other comparison in the project can say. [`brep_snapshot.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/brep_snapshot.py) has `digest`, `capture` and `compare` beside `params_snapshot.py`. **The trap, and it is the whole reason the tool exists rather than a `diff`:** 73 of the bulkhead's 224 `.FCStd` members differ between two runs that produced *identical* geometry — `Document.xml` (timestamps, a fresh UUID, and the document name, which follows the output filename), `StringHasher.Table.txt` (topological-naming hash IDs assigned per session, so one face is `H1323` in one run and `Ha75` in the next), and the 71 `*.Shape.Map.txt` element maps referencing them. Comparing whole documents or their sizes reports differences that are not differences — the same failure `mesh_stats` had with triangle counts (OQ-ARCH-16), reached by a different route — so the tool compares `.brp` members only. **Tested both ways on the real exit code, not through a pipe:** two runs of the same part exit 0, a bulkhead against a corner names the 234 differing members and exits 1. **Note this does not contradict the recompute floor** — a *fresh build* from identical parameters is bit-identical; a *recompute* after a parameter round-trip is not, at ~1.5e-8 relative on volume (IP-FC-56). Different operations, different answers | — | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-2](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-33 | done | **All three trialled 2026-08-18 on `U_1.0 end_bolt 3/16in`, each scored on a pair that should agree *and* a pair that should not, so a method is judged on false alarms as well as sensitivity.** **(1) Mass properties — kept.** Free, and the cross-engine agreement is the headline: OpenSCAD 6922.2727 mm³ against FreeCAD's B-rep 6922.2805, **1.13×10⁻⁶ relative**. Identical shapes give exactly 0 on volume, area and centre of mass; different parts give 1.044 relative volume and 47.4 mm of centre-of-mass separation. A signature, not a proof — distinct shapes can share all three in principle. **(2) Mesh-to-B-rep deviation — kept and promoted to [`mesh_to_brep.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/mesh_to_brep.py).** The best of the three and now the strongest cross-engine tier: **max 0.000158 mm, mean 0.000025 mm** over 300 points, against 57.310369 mm for the same mesh on a *corner's* B-rep. Five orders of magnitude between signal and noise. It beats `compare_backends.py` for this purpose because it never tessellates the B-rep — that tool meshes the FreeCAD shape and compares mesh to mesh, leaving FreeCAD's mesher inside the comparison as a third variable. Costs ~24 ms/point, so sample deliberately. **(3) Section-curve compare — trialled, works, not adopted, and explicitly not deprecated.** Detected the wrong part on all 8 sections via differing wire counts, and gave 0 on matching sections. But it is the slowest per unit of information (~1 s/section against 24 ms/point), and everything it caught here was caught more cheaply by (1) and (2). It also reports badly: with wire counts mismatched, its length and area figures both read `0.000e+00`, so two completely different parts look identical unless the wire-count column is read. Recorded rather than deprecated — the failure is my comparator's reporting, not the method. **One trap worth carrying forward:** the first trial selected the document's *largest solid* as "the part" and got a construction blank — 5,538,767 mm³ against a real part of 6,922 — so every method compared bounding boxes while looking like it compared parts. Caught only by sanity-checking against a known volume. The tool now takes the tip node (empty `InList`) and refuses outright when `--expect` disagrees | — | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-2](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |

| IP-FC-8 | done | **The SI↔mm conversion layer, one named module: [`freecad/units.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/units.py), 2026-08-21.** Holds `MM_PER_M`, `M_PER_MM` and `PA_PER_MPA` with `m_to_mm`/`mm_to_m`/`mpa_to_pa`/`pa_to_mpa`, re-exports the standard library's angle pair so "the project's conversions" is one import rather than a rule with an exception, and carries `bbox_m_matches_mm` — the bounding-box-÷1000 test the roadmap names — plus `describe_bbox_mismatch`, which says *"a value in millimeters is being read as meters"* rather than leaving a reader to derive that from a table of numbers. **It lives in `freecad/` and not `tools/` because that is the only directory the ported modules can import from**: they run under `freecadcmd` with just their own directory on `sys.path`. It imports nothing from FreeCAD, so any interpreter can read it. **What the boundary actually is, which is narrower than "port the code to SI":** SI stops at the parameters, and everything from the FreeCAD API inward is millimeters permanently, because that is the API's unit and STL/3MF carry no unit metadata. So `fillets.py` carrying bare millimeter rows is **correct**, and converting it would be the defect — the module says so, because the opposite reading is the natural one. **The SI side has no members yet and the file says that too**: the port's parameters still come from `derived_parameters()`, which is the sweep's millimeters under a permanent exemption, so nothing crossing into the port today needs converting. Writing the factor and the check before the first caller is deliberate — the alternative is introducing them alongside the code that needs them, when a wrong sign arrives with them and nothing independent is left to catch it. **Verified** against a real swept corner box `[-6.15, -6.15, 0, 5, 5, 25]`: a correct SI box passes, mm-read-as-m and m-written-as-mm are both caught and correctly named, a 1 µm error on a 25 mm part is caught, a face sitting exactly on the origin does not false-positive — the tolerance is relative to the part's own extent for near-zero components, the same rule IP-FC-56's absolute tolerance got wrong — and a malformed box is refused. `tools/oml_export.py` keeps its own `MODEL_UNIT_MM = 1000.0`, which is **not** a duplicate: it composes the convention *one OpenVSP model unit is one metre* with this factor, and it runs under OpenVSP's Python. Both files now point at each other so a third copy reads as the duplicate it would be | — | [general.md §Units](../guidelines/general.md), [freecad_migration.md §The unit boundary](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |

| IP-FC-12 | in progress | **Unblocked 2026-08-10** by IP-FC-4 and IP-FC-10, re-blocked the same day on [OQ-DES-B11](../design/bulkhead.md), and unblocked again when that was decided: **split by intent** — true fillets on the key, the morphological chain for the region-wide remainder. The key's direct construction landed in the OpenSCAD path first and is verified across all 24 distinct swept key geometries at a worst 0.00522% symmetric difference, with `boom_key_validity_check()` enforcing the domain. [`boom_key.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/boom_key.py) then ports it — the first module built **in the plane**, which established that coplanar unions must be built from cuts rather than fuses or every offset downstream is wrong by hundreds of percent (§IP-FC-12 progress). Port the boom bulkhead and the cowls. Preserve the OML transform algebra verbatim, including `offset_x_m` preceding the scale. Note what IP-FC-46 through IP-FC-49 cost the two kinds already ported: run `compare_backends.py` against each new kind across the swept space, not at one point. **The boom bulkhead is built almost entirely from 2D offsets** — `fillet_inner`, `fillet_outer` and plain `offset(r=±)` strokes — and three of its four `fillet_inner` uses wrap a whole compound region rather than a named corner, so the route is not the one the frame bulkhead's four true fillets settled. **Done pending that answer:** [`ref_boom_bulkhead.scad`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/ref_boom_bulkhead.scad) isolates the part and twelve sub-shapes at swept parameters taken from `derived_parameters()` rather than typed, and the swept space is enumerated at 132 valid variants, none of them on the offset degeneracy (§IP-FC-12 progress). **The part itself is ported and agrees to 0.00039%** as of 2026-08-11 — [`boom_bulkhead.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/boom_bulkhead.py), one profile and one `Part::Extrusion`. `boom_make_lower_web` followed the same day, so all three swept boom types are covered — `center_single` is checked against its own reference at 0.00040%. **The harness wiring landed 2026-08-11**: the two bulkhead sweeps are now described by one `BULKHEAD_FAMILIES` table that `render_variant.py`, `export_parameters.py` and `compare_backends.py` all read, the export carries a named `boom_parameters` table, and the kind is swept and compared like any other (§IP-FC-12 progress). Sweeping it immediately found two defects the two-point check could not: IP-FC-52, a +0.167% error at one variant in eight, and IP-FC-54, a null offset that stopped three variants building. and IP-FC-57, two more null offsets that came down to how a shape had been built rather than what it was. **All 132 valid boom variants now build and agree with OpenSCAD — worst deviation 0.00110% against a 0.010% tolerance, across U = 0.5 to 4.0, all nine panels and all three boom types, every bounding box exact.** **The cowls' geometry landed 2026-08-31** — [`oml_blank.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/oml_blank.py) imports the OML as a B-rep and [`cowl.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/cowl.py) builds all four parts, wired into the sweep as the kinds `nose_cowl`, `nose_nose`, `nose_plate` and `tail`. **Verified by holding the OML constant**, which this comparison must do and no other kind has to: the OpenSCAD path cuts the committed *mesh* and the port cuts the *surface*, and those two exports of the same loft differ by 0.253% over the nose cowl's own extent while agreeing to 0.005% over the whole nose ([cowl.md §6.1](../design/cowl.md)) — so a direct volume comparison measures the two exports rather than the port. Built on the same mesh: nose cowl **−0.0000%**, tail **−0.0000%**, nose tip **+0.0018%**, plate **−0.0096%**. It also confirmed `buttress_cut_thickness = 0.1` against the pre-rename reference, 0.05 being wrong by +0.0088% and +0.0513% (IP-FC-43). Three things the comparison found on the way. (i) `Shape.Volume` is unreliable on freeform surfaces — a NURBS sphere reports −0.0437% against a known answer, the tail blank +6.79% — so the acceptance measure is the tessellated volume, [OQ-DES-CW12](../design/cowl.md). (ii) That measure's resolution floor is set by the **smallest** part, not the largest: at 0.02 mm deflection the nose tip read +0.118% for geometry that is exact, a tessellation inscribing its 30 mm plate bore and so under-removing; 0.002 mm is the coarsest value at which every part converges inside tolerance. (iii) `build_part.py`'s export deflection of 1.0e-3, measured on a bulkhead, produced a **94 MB tail of 1 879 224 facets** against the OpenSCAD path's 18 MB — planes and cylinders mesh cheaply at any deflection and a wholly freeform surface does not — so the cowls export at 0.02 mm. **What remains is that the cowl documents are not yet parametric.** Measured 2026-08-31: a corner document carries dozens of expression bindings and every feature reads the sheet; a cowl document carries a sheet and one baked `Part::Feature` with **zero** bindings — editable in name only, which is the failure `build_part.py`'s own docstring names when it says an STL-only mode would be a way to spend hours producing the wrong artifact. The route is established and its one hard part is answered: `corner_tree._sketch` already drives constrained polygons from the sheet, and `Draft::Clone` was verified headless on 2026-08-31 to carry an expression-bound `Scale`, so even the imported blank can track `U`. **It is gated on nothing.** Both questions raised against it on 2026-08-31 were withdrawn the same day: what the sheet contains was already settled by [OQ-DES-CW4](../design/cowl.md) — buttress placement becomes a list, so the eleven tail placements and the nose's one are sheet rows — and the second asked what should happen to a cowl document if the airframe were reshaped, which is a case that does not exist. The lip is derived from the outer surface the model has. **The nose cowl and the tail are parametric documents as of 2026-09-01** — [`cowl_tree.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/cowl_tree.py), 17 objects and 38 expression bindings for the nose, 37 and 248 for the tail, each **geometrically identical to the baked reference: zero solids and exactly zero volume in the difference, taken both ways**, with matching face counts. Four separate causes had to be cleared and three of them were self-inflicted, each traced by measurement after an assertion made without one. (i) **A diagnostic harness had seeded `OmlSurface` from the 80 000-facet STL instead of the 12-face STEP surface**, so it measured the mesh and reported it as the surface; that is where "a lone 0.1 mm slab cannot cut this blank" came from, and it is false — one side buttress cuts in 0.4 s and the intersection volume is exactly linear in tool thickness from 0.1 mm to 5 mm. It is also where a 4.4 GB build came from: seven document cuts against an 80 000-face B-rep, every intermediate held live. (ii) The six-connected-group cutter built on that false premise, removed. (iii) The diagonal plate had been widened from ±`unit_width`/2 to ±`unit_width` on a bad tangency diagnosis — with the widening the cut returns an **empty** shape, without it the same cut is valid. (iv) **A `Draft::Clone` scales a shape's tolerance along with its geometry.** The protected core, cloned up by `unit_width`/2 = 50, went from OCC's 1e-7 to 5e-6, which inflated the cutter's edges to 2.6e-4 and emptied the final cut; it is now a `Part::Wedge` whose top face degenerates to a point — same pyramid, 1e-7, every dimension still on the sheet. That same mechanism, not the "rebuilt B-spline" first blamed, is why the blank had been frozen at build size; it is now a scripted `_ScaledSurface` reading `Factor` from the sheet, so the blank tracks `U` (100 → 200 mm at U = 2) instead of staying put. **The finding that outlasts the port is that FreeCAD's document booleans return the wrong solid for this part** — `Part::Cut` gives 69 faces, invalid, 68 mm³ of material short and 22% smaller tessellated, where `Part.Shape.cut` on byte-identical operands gives 39 faces and a valid solid; `PartDesign::Boolean` and `PartDesign::Pocket` reproduce the same wrong answer, all three sharing FreeCAD 1.1's `TopoShape` layer and logging `hasher mismatch` throughout. **Tolerance is the whole cause**, isolated 2026-09-01 on 1.1.3 — an earlier note here said it was not, on the grounds that a cutter cleaned to 1.5e-07 changes nothing, which is true but only because `Part::Cut` re-applies its own automatic fuzz whatever the tool carries. The pair that isolates it: force the *stock* cutter's recorded tolerance down and the cut returns 39 faces and valid; force the *clean* cutter's up to the stock value and it returns the 69-face invalid solid, the two cutters being the same geometry to ±0.000000 mm³. The recorded number runs at 7.0e-07 per mm of bounding-box diagonal (170.3 mm → 1.20e-04, 371.2 mm → 2.63e-04) while the cut's requirement is absolute — 1.5e-07 exact, 1.0e-06 already 68 faces and +1565 mm³, nothing in between — so a stock-built tool is clean enough only under about 0.2 mm across. So every boolean in both cowls runs through one small scripted object, `_ShapeBoolean`, and the deployment consequence — FreeCAD will not restore a scripted class whose module is not an addon, so a handed-out `.FCStd` displays correctly but does not re-solve — is [OQ-DES-CW16](../design/cowl.md). **The nose tip's persistence defect is fixed**: `makeOffset2D` turned the section's 4 B-spline edges into 540 `OffsetCurve` segments, 98 under a micron and the shortest 2.2e-08 mm, which survived in memory but not a save and reload — the part built valid and reloaded invalid at 548 faces carrying 0.099 mm of edge tolerance. Re-fitting the offset as one periodic B-spline (`cowl._clean_offset`, 480 samples, 8.3e-04 mm from the exact offset) takes it to **9 faces and 19 edges, valid through the document, a BRep round-trip and back**, at a volume change of 1.1e-06. **What remains**: `nose_nose` and `nose_plate` are still baked single-solid documents with zero bindings, and **the tail cowl is only correct at `U` = 1** — measured 2026-09-01, and it supersedes the earlier note here that "editing `U` gives the wrong size, the nose reads 7.66 where 8 is right and the tail empties, so something besides the blank does not scale." Both halves of that were wrong. **The nose's 7.66 is correct, not a defect**: every bounding box doubles exactly from `U` = 1 to 2, and `buttress_cut_thickness` is deliberately unscaled (§6.3), so the part is not self-similar and its volume ratio is 1.971³ = 7.657 rather than 8. Comparing a volume ratio against 8 was the wrong test. **And nothing fails to scale in the tail either**: `Half` tracks `U`³ to four decimals, `Core`, `Cutter` and `Safe` all track exactly, the cutter's volume scaling `U`² to four figures (10464.28 → 16353.48 → 23551.93 at `U` = 1, 1.25, 1.5) exactly as fixed-thickness plates must. What fails is the cut itself. Since the slots keep their 0.1 mm while the body grows, the material removed must scale as `U`²; and it must be measured **tessellated**: the slots take 231.46 mm³ out of a 297 242.57 mm³ half, 0.078%, while `Shape.Volume` overstates that half by 5.5%, so the raw-volume version of this test reports the removal as 5923.93 and is measuring its own error. A first pass at this used those raw figures and read the damage as "+16.7% at `U` = 1.1"; **those are withdrawn**, and the tessellated measure shows it is far worse. At `U` = 1.1 the cut removes **84 890.02 mm³ where 280.07 is right** — 21% of the part — while reporting 38 faces and `valid = True`. The face count wanders 39 → 38 → 68 → 69 → 68 → 7 across the range. Above about 1.5 it collapses: `U` = 1.6 gives a **cut volume of −11.05** and `U` = 3.0 a seven-face solid of −339.51, both reported `isValid = True`, and `U` = 1.75 and 2.0 return an empty shape. **A fresh build fails identically to editing the sheet** (`U` = 2.0 empty either way, `U` = 1.25 agreeing to three decimals), so the sweep is affected exactly as the interactive path is, and it is not a recompute-order defect. Fusing the crossing group and cutting the rest singly gives byte-identical results at every `U`, so it is not a construction-order defect either. The nose does **not** share it: 10 faces on the cut and 64 on the part at every `U` from 0.5 to 3.0, always valid, never empty, and its removal normalised by `U`² holds to ±1.3% over that whole range (8.19 to 8.32, with one 6.2% outlier at `U` = 2.0 that is plausibly measurement resolution, the slot taking 35 mm³ out of 372 550). That last figure has to be measured tessellated: the nose's single slot removes 18 mm³ from a 46 570 mm³ octant, 0.04%, while `Shape.Volume` on that octant misses `U`³ by 0.5 to 0.8% — so the raw-volume version of this test returns *negative* removals for the nose and means nothing. The tail's raw volumes are usable only because `Half` tracks `U`³ to four decimals and its slots take 1.9% of the body. **The cut inflates the tail's tolerance and it does not matter** (measured 2026-09-01, raised and closed the same day). `Half`, `Cutter` and `Safe` are all clean at about 1e-07; `Cut` comes out at 2.428e-01 — larger than the 0.1 mm slots themselves. It is confined to vertices and edges, 22 of 83 vertices over 1e-5 and 5 over 0.1, with **every face still exact at 3.281e-07**, and every one of the ten loosest vertices sits on a diagonal slot, most where two or three tools cross. It is load-bearing rather than sloppy bookkeeping: `fixTolerance(1e-07)` leaves the volume identical but makes the solid **invalid**, so the kernel needs that slack to certify the topology at the crossings, and it survives a BRep round trip unchanged. It does not reach the artifact: tessellated at the export's 0.02 mm the tail gives 89 644 triangles with **zero degenerate facets and every edge shared by exactly two triangles** — watertight and manifold — and the neighbourhood of each loose vertex holds 9 to 14 ordinary triangles whose smallest area is 0.11 mm². Same root cause as the sequential-versus-union divergence: thin slots crossing inside a curved body. **FreeCAD 1.1.3 was installed and tested 2026-09-01 and does not lift OQ-DES-CW16.** Its new `PartDesign::Boolean.FuzzyTolerance` fixes the *cut* outright — at 0 it reproduces the reference solid with zero symmetric difference, where auto (−1) still gives the 69-face invalid one, and the property is stored so it survives a save — but a cowl is not one cut. PartDesign's `Fuse` returns a compound rather than a union at every fuzzy value tried (`Top1` fused with `Diag11`, overlapping by 1.1 mm³, comes back as two solids at exactly the sum of their volumes), and its `Common` collapses the mask intersection against the NURBS blank to 5 faces, +4067.96 mm³ and a tolerance of 19.7 mm — a fully stock nose ends at 4 solids and 1.84e+05 symmetric difference. `Part::Cut` gains no such property. Nothing stock re-records a tolerance either (`Part::Refine`, `Part::Mirroring` twice about one plane, and `Draft::Clone` at scale 1 all return the identical shape at the identical 2.63e-04). **But the fuzz is a preference, and turning it off resolves the whole thing** — this corrects a claim made earlier the same day that no such preference existed. It is `BooleanFuzzy` under `BaseApp/Preferences/Mod/Part/Boolean`, default 10.0, read once at startup in `AppPart.cpp` and applied in `FCBRepAlgoAPIHelper::setAutoFuzzy()` as `BooleanFuzzy × bounding-box diagonal × Precision::Confusion()` — 1.0e-06 per mm at the default, which is exactly the 7.0e-07 per mm of recorded tolerance measured above. Two things hid it: the binary search looked only for 7-bit ASCII, and the value is read at module load, so setting it from a running session does nothing. With `BooleanFuzzy = 0` set before FreeCAD starts, `Part::MultiFuse` records 2.12e-07 with geometry unchanged to ±0.000000 mm³, `Part::Cut` returns 39 faces valid at zero symmetric difference, and **both whole cowls build from stock objects alone at exactly zero symmetric difference against the scripted construction** (74 faces tail, 64 nose), with `Draft::Clone` replacing `_ScaledSurface` for the blank at zero difference and still tracking `U` (200.000 mm at U = 2). That is alternative 6 of OQ-DES-CW16 and it is a decision, not a defect: the setting is per-install, so a file that depends on it fails by producing a wrong part rather than an error. One positive result to keep: **only one fuse in the tail is load-bearing** — the eleven tools form six disjoint solids of which one group of six overlaps (`Top1`, `Top2`, the four diagonals), and cutting by that group's union then by the five remaining slabs separately reproduces the reference exactly, so the scripted surface could shrink from seven nodes to one; not worth doing alone, since one blocked import stops a restore as completely as seven and the two cowls would stop sharing a construction — **and that result is now withdrawn (2026-09-01): the reference it reproduced is itself wrong.** Fusing that group is the tail's second defect, below **The tail's scale failure has a cause, found 2026-09-01, and it is not tolerance: the OML blank is not a legal boolean argument.** `Shape.check(True)` runs `BOPAlgo_ArgumentAnalyzer`, and on `Blank`, `Lower` and `Half` it raises `BOPAlgo GeomAbs_C0` on both edges and faces, at `U` = 1 and `U` = 2 alike, while `Core`, `Cutter` and `Safe` all pass clean. OCC's boolean requires arguments to be at least C1 and the blank has never been one — which is why this behaves like a cliff rather than a defect: at `U` = 1 the induced error stays under tolerance and the right part falls out anyway, and as `U` grows the error grows with the model while the deliberately-absolute 0.1 mm slot does not, until it swamps the feature. It also explains the non-repeatability and why every tool measured perfect. The C0 is written by OpenVSP's STEP exporter, whose internal surface representation is a grid of Bézier patches joined at interior knot multiplicity equal to the degree: each of the blank's eight B-spline faces is degree 5 × 3 with 45 interior V knots at multiplicity 3, 360 C0 seams, V circumferential and U a single quintic span axially. The surface is not creased, only written that way — the worst normal break across all 45 knots of face 0 is **0.000029°** — which is what makes it fixable without touching geometry, and also why it went unnoticed. **Fuzz is not the cause.** `Part.Shape.cut()` takes an explicit fuzz; at `U` = 1.5 the cut is already wrong at **zero** fuzz (68 faces, invalid, 10004.97 removed where 520.79 is right) and no value recovers it, while `U` = 2 is empty at every value. Setting `BooleanFuzzy = 0` changes nothing for a second reason: this port builds through `Part.Shape`, which does not read that preference. The `U` = 1 row does bound the margin — correct only while the fuzz stays at or below 1e-07 mm, OCC's own `Precision::Confusion`. **Refitting is ruled out by the airframe.** The section is a rounded rectangle, C1 but not C2, and 10 of the 45 interior knots on face 0 carry real curvature discontinuities up to a 99.3% jump (κ = 0.0687 → 9.4528 at knot 15, a 0.106 mm corner radius, the same order as the cut). A C1 smoothing approximation flattened all ten, blew a 1e-04 mm budget at 4.2e-03 mm, and would not sew closed. **Splitting the faces at their C0 seams is the candidate cure** — 46 single-Bézier strips per face, 369 faces for the blank, no approximation anywhere — and a first spike returns one valid solid at `U` = 1, 1.5, 2 and 4 with a *constant* error factor instead of a growing one, against 19× at 1.5 and empty at 2 and 4 before. Measured 2026-09-01: **the split is exact to 8 parts per million** tessellated (16833.772147 against 16833.911873), the 6.35% loss first reported for it was `Shape.Volume`'s own 6.8% error on this blank and is withdrawn, sewing 369 faces costs 1.0 s, and the 24-minute stall was `Shape.check(True)` rather than the construction. Recompute costs about 6× — 75 s per `U` against 12 s. **It is necessary and not sufficient, and the second defect is the fuse.** With the split blank the error localises completely: five of the six disjoint `Safe` solids scale as `U`² to 0.07% (213.290 at `U` = 1 against 3410.144 at `U` = 4), and everything wrong sits in the sixth, the fused group of `Top1`, `Top2` and the four diagonals. Cut individually those six all behave — factors 1.000, 1.000, 1.125, 0.999, 0.990, 1.106 — summing to 91.320 at `U` = 1 and 1486.304 at `U` = 4. **Fused, the group removes −0.252 at `U` = 1 and 1461.713 at `U` = 4**: 98.3% of the singles' sum at `U` = 4, and zero at `U` = 1. The fuse fails at `U` = 1, not at large `U`, which is the reverse of what every earlier note here assumed and is why `U` = 1 looked like a knife edge — it agreed with OpenSCAD while silently omitting a cut. **With all three fixes the tail is correct across the whole swept range** — blank converted to C1, subdivided at every third knot to 129 faces, eleven tools cut individually, whole mirrored part against the pre-migration OpenSCAD sweep: **+0.0040% at `U` = 0.5, +0.0038% at 1.0, +0.0036% at 1.5, and +0.0035% at 2.0, 3.0 and 4.0**, one valid solid and **154 faces at every scale** so the topology is stable and not just the volume, flat across 512× in volume so no scale term survives, with removal tracking `U`² to **0.05%** and recompute at **10.2–12.3 s** against about 12 s for the 9-face blank — no penalty, and faster than the same subdivision without the conversion. **The C1 conversion is exact and nearly free**: reparameterise the V knot intervals to match parametric speeds, then reduce interior multiplicity 3 → 2, 360 reductions across the eight faces, **5.8e-10 mm deviation, 0.5 s, once at import**. It does not fix the cut alone — a CLEAN 9-face C1 blank still returns −3573.933 mm³ at `U` = 4 — but it **doubles the subdivision margin**, moving the cliff from between `k` = 3 and 6 to between 6 and 12: measured with controls in one harness, C1 `k` = 6 gives 0.999 where plain `k` = 6 gives −9.569. Multiplicity 2 at degree 3 is exactly C1, the continuity the rounded-rectangle section actually has, so the ten corner curvature breaks survive. The conversion relies on a property of this export — the speed ratio at each joint is independent of position along that joint, and the ratios are exact powers of two, OpenVSP subdividing dyadically — so it verifies that precondition per direction and skips the reparameterisation where it does not hold, rather than assuming it. **Both cowls import through `oml_blank.surface()` and so share the conditioning**, and the nose exercises a case the tail does not: its faces 0–3 carry interior **U** multiplicities of 5 at degree 5, making it C0 in both parametric directions where the tail is C0 only in V. The general function handles both — nose 8 faces in, 96 out, 180 reductions, 9.5e-10 mm, 0.2 s, precondition spread 2.7e-11 in U and 5.7e-08 in V against a 1e-04 tolerance — and the tail through that same general function reproduces the inline result exactly (129 faces, 360 reductions, 5.5e-10 mm, +0.0040/+0.0038/+0.0035/+0.0035%, 154 faces at every scale). **The nose improves too**, which had not been measured: unconditioned it drifts +0.0110% at `U` = 0.5, +0.0111% at 1.0, **−0.0046%** at 2.0 and **−0.0156%** at 4.0 — 0.027% across the range, crossing zero, a small but real scale term — and conditioned it is flat at +0.0134–0.0136%, one valid solid and 88 faces at every scale, 1.3 s per recompute. This supersedes the earlier note that the nose is unaffected: it never failed, but it was not clean either. **The C0 finding is not the cause, and that is settled by measurement, not by argument.** The condition can be removed exactly — reparameterising the V knot intervals to match parametric speeds (the ratios are u-independent to 3.5e-07 and are exact powers of two) makes the surface genuinely C1 at 2.9e-14 mm, after which multiplicity reduces 3 → 2 at all 45 joints at a further 3.1e-10 mm and `check(True)` returns CLEAN on a 9-face blank — **and the cut still fails, returning −3573.933 mm³ at `U` = 4**. Conversely a 129-face blank carrying **256 C0 errors** builds the part to +0.0036%. Subdivision is what the cut needs; continuity is neither sufficient nor necessary — though, as above, it buys a factor of two in how coarse the subdivision may be, so both contribute to conditioning. The depth is sharp and was measured at `U` = 4 by splitting at every `k`-th knot: `k` = 46 (9 faces) is 815× out, `k` = 23 (17) 474× out, `k` = 12 (33) and `k` = 6 (65) both return negative volumes, `k` = 3 (129) is correct at +0.0036%, and `k` = 1 (369) adds nothing at +0.0034%. The earlier 6× recompute cost quoted here was an artifact of splitting to 369 faces and is **withdrawn**. Neither fix alone suffices: without subdivision the cut fails at scale whatever the continuity, and without unfusing, `U` = 1 loses 91.5 mm³. **Implemented and verified through the real code path 2026-09-01.** `oml_blank.condition()` carries the reparameterisation and the subdivision and is called from `surface()`, so both cowls and every other OML consumer get it from one place; it reports what it did, and *declines* per direction rather than approximating where the speed-ratio precondition fails. `cowl_tree.tail_cowl()` drops the fused `Cutter` for one `Safe` node per tool — `_ShapeBoolean.execute` applies its operation over `Tools` in order, so the single `Cut` node is eleven successive cuts and not a multi-argument one — taking the tail document from 37 objects to 46. Built by that code alone, against the pre-migration sweep: **nose cowl +0.0136/+0.0136/+0.0134/+0.0134/+0.0134/+0.0134% and tail +0.0040/+0.0038/+0.0036/+0.0035/+0.0035/+0.0035% at `U` = 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0**, one valid solid throughout, 88 and 306 faces at every scale, 1.3–1.8 s and 12.6–14.4 s per recompute. **The change reaches `nose_tip` too**, which builds through `cowl.py` rather than `cowl_tree.py`: it still builds, and it is *better* — 9 faces at 9.916e-02 mm of edge tolerance become 37 faces at **5.000e-06**, a factor of 20 000, with the BRep round trip that IP-FC's persistence fix established still exact both ways. Its volume moves +0.02978% (9474.5564 → 9477.3775), which sits inside the mesh-against-surface export band. `nose_plate` does not touch the OML and is unaffected | IP-FC-10, IP-FC-4, OQ-DES-B11, OQ-DES-CW4, OQ-DES-CW16, OQ-DES-CW17, OQ-DES-CW18 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B11](../design/bulkhead.md), [cowl.md §2](../design/cowl.md), [cowl.md §6.1](../design/cowl.md), [cowl.md §6.3](../design/cowl.md), [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW12](../design/cowl.md), [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW16](../design/cowl.md), [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW17](../design/cowl.md) |
| IP-FC-13 | in progress | [`compare_backends.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/compare_backends.py) renders the same variants through both engines and compares measured geometry, with the two tiers below and a guard that refuses to count a part FreeCAD never built. Usable now for the corner, the frame bulkhead and — since 2026-08-14 — the boom bulkhead, and `--kinds` sweeps one kind exhaustively without paying for the others. **Sampling is not enough and this item now has the evidence**: eight boom bulkheads spread across the range found IP-FC-52 at one of them, 0.167% against 0.0006% for its seven neighbours, and the exhaustive run then found IP-FC-54 and IP-FC-57 at five variants the sample never visited — four of which did not build at all, so no tolerance would have caught them. The boom bulkhead is the first kind swept exhaustively: **132 of 132, worst 0.00110%**. Whole-corpus coverage is still blocked on IP-FC-12, which needs the cowls. Full-sweep equivalence against the OpenSCAD corpus by volume, bounding box and hole positions — **not** triangle count. **Two tiers, per OQ-DES-B9:** parts whose geometry is exactly reproducible (the corner) stay strict; parts carrying real fillets need a stated deviation tolerance and a comparison that measures deviation rather than volume equality. Interface dimensions are strict in both tiers — no interface is set by a fillet. **The boom bulkhead lands in the strict tier**, settled by [OQ-DES-B11](../design/bulkhead.md): its one true-fillet feature is the key, and replacing the key's morphological rounding moved the whole part by 0.00008% — two orders inside the strict tolerance — so the split does not cost a tier. **Two cost controls added 2026-08-15, both learned the expensive way.** (i) `--build-only` renders only the FreeCAD side and reports each variant as built / not ported / did not build. The build question and the agreement question are separable and priced completely differently: an `--all` comparison renders the space twice, and the OpenSCAD half measured 2.5 hours at ~38 s/part against the FreeCAD half's ~1.3 s/part — *and it cannot produce a build failure of its own*, OpenSCAD being the authority that builds everything. Running the expensive half to answer "what fails to build" was hours spent on the wrong artifact. (ii) `--reference DIR` reuses an existing OpenSCAD tree instead of rendering one, with `--resume`, so a part whose definition still matches byte for byte costs nothing and only stale or missing ones rebuild — the same check `--resume` already trusts, which is what makes reuse safe rather than merely fast. A killed run leaves its reference intact, so the expensive half survives and a full comparison afterwards costs only the FreeCAD pass; measured, the 544-part reference re-attached with `resume: skipped 544 unchanged part(s)`. A supplied reference is never wiped going in and never deleted coming out, including when it sits inside the scratch tree, which is the natural place for it | IP-FC-12 | [freecad_migration.md §Equivalence between toolchains](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B9](../design/bulkhead.md), [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B11](../design/bulkhead.md) |

| IP-FC-14 | done | **UC-2 — every FreeCAD build now writes its `.FCStd`, and it is not optional.** `build_part.py` could always save the document, but only when handed `--fcstd`, and nothing ever handed it one — so every invocation built the parametric document in memory and threw it away, shipping only the mesh. The default is now the mesh's own path with the extension swapped (`--out=x.partial.stl` → `x.partial.FCStd`), which is exactly what the sweep's partial-then-rename write wants: `render_definition` gained a `sidecars` parameter so the document goes through the *same* atomic write and the same resume check as the STL, because a half-written or stale `.FCStd` is as misleading as a truncated mesh, and a resume that looked only at the STL would skip every part rendered before the document existed and never backfill it. A missing sidecar hard-fails the part. `--fcstd` now only relocates; there is deliberately no way to suppress it, an STL-only mode being a way to spend hours producing the wrong artifact. Verified: default path, explicit relocation, and end-to-end through the sweep with the rename; the saved document is genuinely parametric — 160 objects, a `Params` spreadsheet carrying 374 aliased cells, and a constrained sketch — not a baked shape. Found while testing: IP-FC-69, a silent `saveAs` failure above a 222-character path | IP-FC-13 | [freecad_migration.md §Use cases](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |

| IP-FC-16 | done | **Written 2026-08-21: [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md).** Carries the construction stage by stage — adaptive stations, the 2-D erosion, contour correspondence, the fitted surface, closing the solid — plus the refinement criterion, the verification table and the failure modes. **Three things in it are worth naming because they are derived rather than restated.** (1) **Feature stations are patch boundaries, not interior knots**: the exterior has a genuine crease where a buttress ramp starts or ends, so fitting one G2 surface across it would smooth away a feature the part really has — matching the exterior's continuity class is what the G1/G2 requirement is a statement *of*, which makes the split a consequence of OQ-ARCH-5 rather than an exception to it. (2) **The rib is not modelled separately.** Eroding the notched section by `t` removes a `t`-neighbourhood of the 0.1 mm notch from each side, leaving `t_cut + 2·n_p·w` = 1.3 mm — exactly the rib thickness OQ-DES-CW3 states. The wall follows the notch in and back out and the material between the passes *is* the rib, so reproducing it for free is the confirmation that a 2-D erosion is the right operation. (3) **The refinement tolerance is absolute, 0.05 mm, and that is deliberately the opposite of `bbox_tol`'s `U`-scaling**: that one measures two engines agreeing on a whole part, which grows with `U`, while this one measures whether a **wall** is in the right place, and the wall is `n_p·w` at every `U` because extrusion width is a machine property. A `U`-scaled tolerance would be 2 % of the wall at U = 0.5 and 67 % of it at U = 4. **The erosion uses the morphological identity from IP-FC-54**, `erode(A−B,t) == erode(A,t) − dilate(B,t)`, because the notched section is exactly the `A−B` shape that returned a null shape there — and that failure was not a tangency a nudge would clear, so a workaround that finds a working value would leave the defect. Deliberately not specified: the OCC entry point for the fit, since §4.4 states the requirement and §6 the acceptance test, and naming an API without measuring it would read as decided. `cowl.md` §6.2 was corrected where it still said the nose tip and tail cap have vanishing wall | IP-FC-7 | [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-17](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-28 | done | OQ-DES-CW2 resolved 2026-08-09: `cone_angle` is the **overhang angle measured from the print bed**, 35°, and both call sites are correct — the complementary spellings meet the same face because the radius lies in the bed plane and the axis is normal to it. **Done 2026-08-21: renamed to `overhang_angle_from_bed` and moved from four places to one entry in `design_constants.json`.** This row originally said `PrinterSettings`, and that was wrong by the time it came to be done — that group's own `_about` reads *"properties of the machine — the same airframe printed on a different nozzle wants different numbers here and no other change"*, and an overhang angle does not move with the nozzle. The row was written 2026-08-09, before the `slicing` group existed; **OQ-DES-CW11 put it in `slicing`**, where OQ-DES-CW9 had already placed a quantity that is neither machine nor design. **Two schema consequences.** `slicing` no longer holds one kind of quantity, so `_check_slicing` dispatches **per key** — a perimeter count is a whole number of loops, an angle is continuous, and one rule cannot serve both without admitting half a perimeter or refusing 35.5° — and a name with no rule is refused rather than passed through; the angle must lie strictly between 0 and 90, since 0 is a flat ceiling the geometry would divide by the tangent of. And `slicing` is not uniformly per part: a perimeter count is, an overhang angle is a limit of the process. **The value is NOT freely tunable and the constant says so**: it has to agree with where the nose/cowl break line falls, changing it means adjusting the buttresses that build every ramp from `r_inset*tan(angle)`, and **nothing in the code enforces either coupling** — editing it alone gives a part that builds, renders, passes every check here, and is wrong. **The dependency on IP-FC-12 did not apply**, checked rather than assumed: nothing under `freecad/` referenced `cone_angle`, so the cowl port neither gated this nor was gated by it, and landing the rename first means the port inherits the right name. **Verified geometry-neutral**: all 7 drivers render the same 131,506 triangles compared as sorted facet sets, `audit_call_args.py` finds no positional mismatch across the renamed signatures, and the sampled sweep agrees on every cowl | — | [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW2](../design/cowl.md), [§OQ-DES-CW11](../design/cowl.md) |

| IP-FC-42 | done | **Adopt `n_perimeters`. Decided by OQ-DES-CW9 2026-08-18: a per-part `slicing` group** — a perimeter count is neither a machine property (`PrinterSettings` says so of itself) nor a part property (the same cowl prints vase or conventional), and parts are sliced independently, so a corner's count is unrelated to a cowl's. **Landed:** a `slicing` group in `design_constants.json` carrying `cowl_n_perimeters = 1`, with its own validator refusing anything but a whole number ≥ 1 — zero perimeters is not a thin wall, it is no wall, and a "positive number" rule would let 0.5 through; plus `COWL_N_PERIMETERS`, a `CowlFlangeParameters.cowl_n_perimeters` field, and the **cowling bulkhead's flange radius now reading `corner_radius - cowl_n_perimeters*extrusion_width - cowl_flange_tolerance`**, threaded through three SCAD signatures and both hand drivers. Named for *whose* count it is: the flange is sized around the **cowl's** wall and the bulkhead's own count is irrelevant to it. At `n = 1` the arithmetic is identical and all 7 drivers render warning-free at unchanged triangle counts. **The third consumer — the nose cowl's base offset — landed 2026-08-21 and moved nothing.** OQ-DES-CW10 confirmed it *is* a function of the perimeter count: the nose shape seats on top of the cowl's perimeter shell and is bonded there, and the inset gives that joint its alignment and its bonding surface. So the offset is `cowl_n_perimeters * extrusion_width + nose_flange_tolerance` with `nose_flange_tolerance = -0.1`, returning exactly the 0.5 mm at the sweep's `extrusion_width = 0.6` that every nose cowl has been built with. The `flange_inset` key is deleted from both cowl parameter files so no stale copy survives, and `_check_tolerance` no longer refuses negative values — that rule rested on an assumption nobody had checked. **Verified across all 576 variants: zero parameters changed value**, the only difference being `cowl_n_perimeters` newly appearing on the 148 bulkheads. **Two false leads are recorded in OQ-DES-CW10 because both read as evidence and neither was.** `0.5 = 0.4 + 0.1` looked like provenance and twice produced the conclusion that the sweep's offset "should" be 0.7 mm, leaving the flown parts 0.2 mm tight — but **the hand drivers' `extrusion_width = 0.4` is a development test value that was never a tuning**, so the decomposition carries no information. And `cowl_flange_tolerance = 0.2` cannot be borrowed for this joint: it is the same *kind* of bonded lap and a different *fit*, which is why they are two parameters. Both `design_constants.json` entries were corrected where they had recorded these as fact. Consumer 1, the cowl's rib thickness, is by design not this item's work: it waits for the rib to be modelled at all, under IP-FC-17 and IP-FC-23 | — | [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW9](../design/cowl.md), [§OQ-DES-CW10](../design/cowl.md) |

Verified geometry-neutral: `verify_sweep_change.py` against `variant_output_baseline` returns both cowls identical, and a control run with the change stashed returns byte-identical output, so the three non-cowl differences it also reports are pre-existing drift and not this change. `verify_drivers.py` renders all 7 drivers warning-free | IP-FC-29 | [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW8](../design/cowl.md) |

| IP-FC-48 | done | **The seed did not supply everything the expression rows read, and nothing checked.** `seeded()` replaces literal rows only, so a row the port states as a relationship — `corner_radius` = `=U * 10`, `longeron_radius` = `=U * 2`, `unit_length` = `=U * FX * 100` — keeps its expression and evaluates from whatever the sheet holds. Neither `U` (bulkhead) nor `FX` (corner) was in the seed, so both stayed at their literal 1.0: **every FreeCAD corner was built at FX=1.0 and every FreeCAD bulkhead at U=1.0**, with the authority's correct values sitting unused in the parameter file. Exact at U=1/FX=1 — the one part IP-FC-10 measured — and wrong by up to 115% elsewhere.
