| IP-FC-49 | done | The bulkhead could not be tiled at U ≥ 2.5: the octant and its mirror were each valid and only their **fuse** was not. Cause was the `eps` overlap that makes the octant interpenetrate its mirror — 0.01 mm absolute, 4e-5 of a 250 mm part, under what OCCT's booleans resolve. **The overlap exists for OpenSCAD's union and OCCT does not need it at all**: a solid fused with its own mirror about the touching plane is valid and volume-exact at 10, 100, 250 and 400 mm with no overlap. Now `mask_eps = 0`, separate from `eps` so cut overshoot is untouched. Every U from 0.5 to 4.0 tiles into one valid solid, the part is dimensionally unchanged, and `full == 8 × octant` becomes exact — a stronger tiling check than the one it replaces | IP-FC-48 | [freecad_migration.md §IP-FC-49](../implementation/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-50 | done | **Audited every `eps` in the port and measured what each one is for.** IP-FC-49 had zeroed the *union overlap*; this asks the same of the other two jobs the constant was doing. [`spike_eps.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/spike_eps.py) measures flush-versus-padded cuts and abutting-versus-overlapped fuses at 10, 100, 250 and 400 mm. **Cut overshoot buys nothing** — a tool cap coplanar with the face it exits through is exact to machine precision and yields the same or *fewer* faces — so the ±eps pad on every section cut tool in `corner_tree._section` is gone, verified inert by the four section volumes and the 52-face count coming back byte-identical. `boom_oml` was still carrying the old mask overlap and now uses `mask_eps` like `bulkhead_cuts`. **The exception is tangency**: safe when incidental, but where a boolean *depends* on a tangent face OCCT under-removes — the greeble wedge's flanks lose 0.0199 mm³ without their eps, so that one stays. Forcing all corner `eps` to zero moves the assembled part by −9e-6% and drops 4 faces, but that near-cancellation hides two errors of opposite sign, which is why the audit is per-site rather than global. **Found OQ-DES-B12 on the way** | IP-FC-49 | [freecad_migration.md §IP-FC-50](../implementation/freecad_migration.md), [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B12](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-51 | done | **The OQ-DES-B12 fix, decided and implemented 2026-08-11 as alternative 1.** `corner_end` now takes an explicit `overshoot` argument, defaulting to 0, that moves the extrusion and the greeble bore and sizes nothing; `bulkhead_section` calls it with the true thickness and an overshoot of `eps` instead of inflating the thickness. **The overshoot had to reach the bore, not just the extrusion** — the bore's z extent is dimensioned from the same argument, so extending only the extrusion would have silently un-overshot the post. The z extent of the tool is unchanged and only the rib moved: **2.00667 → 2.000, equal to the corner post's**, now asserted rather than printed. Regenerated: greeble tool 557.746362 → 557.804925, octant 865.3140969 → 865.2850690, part 6922.5048968 → 6922.2726731. Two checks say the fix landed the same way on both kernels: `ref_end` — the corner's own end section — is **bit-identical** across the change, and OpenSCAD and FreeCAD agree on the *size* of the octant's change (−0.0290279 against −0.0290299) to 2e-6 mm³. The part lost exactly 8× the octant's change, so the tiling is untouched. Found on the way: five scripts read freecadcmd's `--pass` token as their first argument, now filtered once in `corner_common.script_args()` | IP-FC-50, OQ-DES-B12 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B12](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-52 | done | **An outward `Part::Offset2D` does not merge faces that grow into overlap, and the overlap is then counted twice.** FreeCAD offsets each face of a multi-face source independently — correct while the faces stay apart, which is why `plane2d.fragmented()` deliberately allows several faces — but a *positive* offset can grow two of them into each other, and the result keeps both. Found by IP-FC-13 at U=0.75 / 1/8 in panel / `dual`, the one sampled boom bulkhead where the inner web's erosion pinches into pieces closer together than twice the fillet radius: `offset(−r)` split it into four faces, `offset(+2r)` regrew them overlapping at 467.4780 mm² against OpenSCAD's 458.4831, the inner web came out 1.56% small, and the finished part came out **+0.167% — sixteen times the tolerance**, while the other seven sampled variants agreed to 0.0006%. Fixed by merging the dilation through the same `R − (R − X)` complement `plane2d.union` already uses, at both fillet functions' dilation steps — the two places an offset is applied to a shape the same function just produced. An inward offset cannot create an overlap, so only the outward direction needs it. The failing variant went **+0.16731% → +0.00086%**, and every previously verified reference number is bit-identical, the merge being a no-op on a single face. `compare_backends.py` gained `--kinds` so a newly ported kind can be swept exhaustively without paying for the ones already covered | IP-FC-12, IP-FC-13 | |
| IP-FC-53 | done | **A seed was only checked in one direction.** `check_seed` walks the values the *authority supplied* and confirms the sheet reproduces each, so a literal row the parameter file failed to supply was never looked at — and a literal row is configuration by the port's own rule (`corner_common.is_literal`), so the part was built with the module's own reference value silently substituted for the variant's. `check_unseeded` is the other half: every literal row must be supplied or be named in `corner_common.UNSEEDED` with a reason. Run from `build_sheet`, so it covers every route to a seeded sheet rather than just `build_part.py`, and skipped when there is no seed at all, that being the reference check where the literals are the measurement. Verified by removing two rows from a parameter file: refused by name, no part written | IP-FC-41, IP-FC-48 | |
| IP-FC-54 | done | **`Part::Offset2D` returns a null shape on the frame web's erosion, and takes the whole part with it.** Found by the IP-FC-12 sweep: three of 132 boom bulkheads failed to build at U=0.75 with a 3 mm panel. The source erodes the OML *with its bores already cut*, and there the longeron bore — which is concentric with the corner arc — grows past the shrinking arc while the panel notch, 4.4 mm deep, is crossed by a 4.5 mm erosion. Not a tangency a nudge would clear: every erosion from 3.0 to 5.0 mm is null there while 6.0 mm succeeds. Fixed by the morphological identity `erosion(A − B, w) == erosion(A, w) − dilation(B, w)`, whose right-hand side is well conditioned in both halves — the outline erodes cleanly and dilating eight separated circles is trivial. **Exact, not an approximation**: where the direct form works the two agree to the last digit, 5770.975162 either way at U=1.0, and every reference number is unchanged. Generalised to any number of subtracted regions as `plane2d.erode_difference` when IP-FC-57 needed the same move on a three-operand difference | IP-FC-12, IP-FC-13 | |
| IP-FC-55 | in progress | **Per-site audit of the `eps` sites IP-FC-50 left, and one removal declined on the evidence.** Each site is a spreadsheet row, so it can be rewritten on a built document and recomputed -- one site at a time, no source edit. Measured at U=1, 3/16 in: the corner's **axial section overlap** (`end_h`, `mid_z0`, `mid_h`) moves the assembled part by −0.0006 mm³ and changes no face; the corner's **relief cut overshoot** leaves +0.0306 mm³ behind without it; the bulkhead's **flange relief overshoot** is volume-neutral but costs 4 extra faces flush. The last two stay, now with a measurement saying why rather than an assumption. **The axial overlap stays too, and that reverses this item's own first plan.** It was written up as the one clear removal — union slop for a kernel we no longer use, which is what IP-FC-49 established. Reading the source settled it the other way: `corner_end` extrudes `bulkhead_thickness + eps` and `corner_middle` starts at `2*bt - eps` and is `2*eps` taller, so **the overlap is stated by the authority, not added by the port**. Removing it is therefore a deliberate divergence, and it costs something real — `EndCutGroove` moves −0.17% and `MidSection` −0.05% against their OpenSCAD references, which would take `EndCutGroove` outside the 0.1% check and turn two cross-kernel comparisons into FreeCAD-measured constants. It buys −4e-8 of the assembled part. **Weakening a check against the authority while the authority still stands is the wrong trade**; the case for removal is real but it is IP-FC-34's, once OpenSCAD is retired and the cross-kernel argument no longer exists. That distinguishes it from `mask_eps`, which diverges because OCCT actively *failed* with the source's value, and from IP-FC-50's cut overshoots, whose removal left every checked volume byte-identical. What remains: `bulkhead_cuts.clean_r`, the one site that is not slop at all but 0.01 mm off a real bore radius, being measured across all 148 valid bulkhead variants | IP-FC-50 | |
| IP-FC-56 | todo | **The bulkhead's sheet carries rows no bulkhead reads.** `bulkhead_section` merges `corner_tree.PARAMS` to reuse `corner_end`, and `FX`, `unit_length`, `greeble_tolerance`, `mid_h` and `mid_z0` come with it. Measured, not assumed: setting FX to 7.0 on a built bulkhead and recomputing leaves the volume unchanged to the last digit, and so does perturbing each of the others. **A bulkhead is independent of bay length (OQ-DES-C3)** — there is no `dp.bulkhead.FX` field, which is that decision written into the data model — so exporting a value for the row would assert the opposite; they are exempted in `corner_common.UNSEEDED` for now instead. **The checker is built** — [`check_unread_rows.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/check_unread_rows.py), 2026-08-14. It perturbs each row up and down, recomputes, and compares volume, face count and bounding box. All three, because IP-FC-55 found an eps whose removal left the volume identical to the last digit and added four faces: a checker watching volume alone would have called that row unread and invited its deletion. **It was wrong twice before it was right, and both failures are worth recording.** It first reported "183 rows, 0 read by nothing" having tested nothing at all — FreeCAD returns a text cell's contents with a literal-marker apostrophe attached, so every alias lookup missed and every row was skipped silently; it now counts skips and fails if it tested nothing. It then perturbed only upward and called 13 corner rows unread, most of them tool dimensions that are generous on purpose — a cut sized to pass clear through material does not care about being made longer. Bidirectional perturbation took that to 7. **Results so far: corner 53 rows, 7 unread; bulkhead 183 rows, 87 unread.** The bulkhead number is not a finding yet and must not be read as one — most of it is `corner_tree`'s own rows (`wedge_*`, `nub_*`, `mouth_*`) describing sections the bulkhead legitimately never builds, and the rest is the same generous-tool insensitivity. **"Unread" here means the part is insensitive to the perturbation, which is necessary evidence for "vestigial" and not sufficient.** **What remains, in order.** (1) Triage those 87 into inherited-and-unbuilt, insensitive-but-read, and genuinely vestigial. (2) Prune what it finds, following the **expression graph and not a name list** — `greeble_radius` and `relief_depth` both reference `greeble_tolerance`, so dropping that row without dropping its dependents breaks the sheet, and dropping its dependents needs them to be unread too. (3) Delete the four inherited entries from `corner_common.UNSEEDED`, which is the check that the prune actually landed: those exemptions exist only to describe this defect, so the list shrinking is the evidence. **Do not simply exclude `corner_tree.PARAMS` from the merge** — the bulkhead genuinely needs most of it to build `corner_end`, which is the whole reason the merge is there. **The checker appeared to die mid-kind on some bulkhead seeds and did not — diagnosed and fixed 2026-08-18.** At `1.0 end_bolt 3/16in` it completed and reported 154 rows with 67 read by nothing; at `1.5 end_anchor 0mm` it exited non-zero having printed nothing at all, twice, after about 40 minutes each. **It was never crashing.** It walked all 154 rows and then refused at `check_kind`'s closing restore assertion — and `freecadcmd` discards the message a `SystemExit` carries, measured directly: a script that raises `SystemExit('...')` prints everything before it, exits 1, and shows nothing of the message. `parameters.table_of` documents the same finding. With the per-kind report printed only after `check_kind` returns, and the header buffered, the result was a non-zero exit with no output that read exactly like a process death. **The lead recorded here — that the seed which died is one where the greeble-to-web corner exists, so the chain IP-FC-73 made conditional was where to look — was wrong, and the way it was excluded is worth keeping.** The solved sketch centers do drift under perturbation, which makes them look responsible; forcing the sketch to re-solve to convergence after every restore drives all four centers to within 2e-15 mm of their build values and leaves the volume miss **exactly** as it was. The rows that move the sketch are simply the rows that move the fillet solids, so the two drift together without one causing the other. `fillets.py` needed no change. **The cause is that the kernel does not rebuild a solid bit-identically, under a tolerance too fine to permit it.** Rebuilding a part from the parameters it was built from, after computing a different shape in between, misses: perturbing all 154 rows both ways at `1.5 end_anchor 0mm`, 142 of the 308 restores miss by more than the 1e-9 mm³ the comparison allowed, and the worst misses 2.602e-4 mm³ on a 17406 mm³ part — **1.5e-8 relative**. Face count and bounding box come back exactly; only volume moves. The tolerance was **absolute**, which is 6e-14 of a bulkhead and far under that floor, and that alone is why only the bulkhead failed: the corner is small enough that 1e-9 mm³ still clears it. An absolute tolerance applied to parts of very different sizes is the whole of the bug. **Four fixes.** `VOLUME_TOL` is now relative to the part's own volume at 1e-6, which is the U³ scaling stated in the form that reads correctly for a volume; the bounding-box tolerance scales linearly with `U` and is floored at U = 1, matching `compare_backends.bbox_tol()` and [OQ-ARCH-12](../architecture/freecad_migration.md); refusals go to stderr and are flushed before the exit, so they can be read at all; and **restoration is checked after every perturbation rather than once at the end**, which is what turns "the sheet did not restore" into a named row — the closing check could only ever fire once every row had been perturbed and put back, naming none of them. **What the loosening gives up is negligible at this scale**: a row contributing under 0.017 mm³ to a bulkhead is a cube 0.26 mm on a side, about 0.14 mm of a single extrusion bead at 0.6 mm wide and 0.2 mm tall — under the printer's own quantum on a part 100·U mm across, so no feature this port builds hides beneath it. Two things back that up rather than resting on it: the volume test was never the sensitive one — IP-FC-55 is precisely a row that moved the volume by nothing and the face count by four, which is why there are three measurements, and face count and bounding box remain exact — and the failure modes are asymmetric, too tight refusing loudly while too loose reports a read row as unread and invites its deletion. **Verified**: `1.5 end_anchor 0mm` now completes, bulkhead 154 rows with 68 read by nothing and corner 55 with 12; and `1.0 end_bolt 3/16in`, which passed under the old tolerance, still reports the identical 67 rows, so no sensitivity was lost. The bulkhead figures above are the pre-IP-FC-73 sheet and are superseded by these. **A single run cannot support a prune, and the number it gives is an upper bound rather than a finding — established 2026-08-18 and it is the reason this row is still open.** The unread list is variant-dependent: the bulkhead's lists at `1.5 end_anchor 0mm` and `1.0 end_bolt 3/16in` are not nested, with `clean_r` and `slot_y` unread only at the first and `rect_w` only at the second. [`sweep_unread_rows.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/sweep_unread_rows.py) answers the question pruning actually asks — which rows reach no geometry at *every* seed — by **narrowing rather than repeating**: the first seed tests every row and each seed after it retests only the rows still standing, so the forty-minute pass happens once and the rest shrink. **On the corner it halved the answer.** One seed reports 12 rows unread; across three seeds spanning U = 1.0 to 4.0 and both end types only 6 survive, with `panel_thickness`, `panel_tolerance`, `slot_x`, `slot_y`, `slot_w` and `relief_depth` all read at some variant the first seed did not reach. Pruning on the single-seed list would have deleted six live rows. So **every unread count previously recorded here is a single-seed figure and none of them is evidence for deletion**, including the corner's historical 7 and the bulkhead's 87 | IP-FC-53, IP-FC-73 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-C3](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-57 | done | **The no-panel boom bulkheads could not be built: `Part::Offset2D` returned a null shape, twice, for two different reasons that look identical from outside.** Both are the last variants standing after IP-FC-54, both are the `0mm` panel where `panel_thickness`, `panel_overlap` and `panel_tolerance` are all zero and the OML's panel notch degenerates, and in both the failing shape is one OCCT had just produced itself. **(a) `dual` — the closing erosion.** The fillet chain's `2r` dilation yields a shape its own `offset(-r)` cannot consume. Fixed by performing that dilation as two dilations of `r`: the same operation, and the same result to every measurable digit — area 7009.984015 mm², 5 faces, 78 edges either way — but only the split one erodes. **(b) `offset_single` — the leading erosion.** `offset(-r)` of `OML − MATERIAL` is null, and that one is a *boolean* result rather than an offset result, so splitting does not apply. Fixed with the IP-FC-54 identity, which is now the shared `plane2d.erode_difference`: the region is `oml_outer − bores − material`, so erode the outline and dilate the two subtracted regions. Every operand offsets without complaint; their difference does not. **What made both diagnosable was the same negative result**: null at *every* distance, down to 0.01 mm, under all three `Join` settings, after `removeSplitter`, after rebuilding the face from its wires, and in half-steps. A shape that is genuinely wrong has a distance that works; uniform failure means the representation, not the geometry — confirmed by reassembling one failing wire from its own edges, which offsets fine and reproduces the identity's answer to 1e-7. Both parts now agree with OpenSCAD, +0.00078% and +0.00068%, with identical bounding boxes, and every previously verified reference number is bit-identical | IP-FC-12, IP-FC-13, IP-FC-54 | |
| IP-FC-58 | done | **Fixed 2026-08-15 by starting the fillet block at the region's true leftmost vertex.** `bolt_flange_fillet` built its block from `bbf_cx`, the fillet center, rather than from `min(bbf_cx; bolt_c)`, the leftmost vertex of the quadrilateral it is trying to produce. Where `bbf_dx > 0` those differ, and there are **two** consequences, one of them misdescribed here until 2026-08-16. First, the fillet was short of material: with the block starting at the fillet center the ray half-plane removes **nothing at all**, so the built region was the rectangle `bbf_cx..bbf_sx` rather than the trapezoid the OpenSCAD polygon produces, missing the triangle `bbf_dx * bbf_dy / 2 * bulkhead_thickness`. Measured on the real tree at `1.5 end_anchor 0mm`, the shape after the ray cut is 190.6137 mm³ with the fix and 184.4649 without, a difference of 6.1488 mm³ = exactly that triangle. **The earlier claim that 'the ray half-plane trims the block back to the same region either way' was wrong** and is retained here only so the correction is visible; it is also why the corpus-wide agreement with OpenSCAD improved rather than staying put. Second, and the reason it *failed* rather than merely being 5.8 mm³ light: a **tangential boolean**. With the block starting at the fillet center, every point of the ray below that center lies outside the block, so the half-plane touches the block at exactly one point, its corner, which is also where the relief stack is centered — leaving a planar face on `x = bbf_cx` that contains the relief cone's own axis. OCCT emitted a 0.000335 mm edge whose length is twice the distance between its own endpoints, and beside it a face of area −0.00038 mm². The octant still measured as one valid solid; the failure surfaced two steps later when the mirror-and-fuse returned an empty shape. One character of expression changes the block's origin to `bbf_bx = min(bbf_cx; bolt_c)`, making the cut transversal. **Verified across the whole swept space: 484 of 484 ported parts build where 483 did before, all 484 agree with OpenSCAD, and the worst deviation in the corpus improved from 0.00375% to 0.00234%** — so the old geometry was slightly wrong everywhere `bbf_dx > 0`, not merely fragile at one point. The two remaining comparison failures are IP-FC-71, unchanged and unrelated. The design relationship that allowed the degeneracy is **not** closed by this and is raised as [OQ-DES-B14](../design/bulkhead.md); the diagrams there are generated by `tools/draw_bolt_flange_fillet.py`. Superseded description follows. **One frame bulkhead variant builds to zero solids.** `1.5 end_anchor 0mm` — a valid swept combination, an end type, so FreeCAD is the backend that builds it — produces no solid at all; `build_part` refuses it loudly rather than writing a bad mesh, which is the guard working, but the part cannot be made. **Found because the frame bulkhead had never been swept exhaustively**: every check on it has been a sample, and `--kinds`, which makes exhaustive coverage of one kind affordable, only existed from 2026-08-14. Scope is now measured rather than feared — **87 of the 88 end-type variants build as one valid solid**, so this is one point and not a class. The `0mm` panel is where `panel_thickness`, `panel_overlap` and `panel_tolerance` are all zero and several tools degenerate (IP-FC-46 is the same corner of the space), and `end_anchor` at U=1.5 is the only combination that tips over. **Diagnosed 2026-08-15, and it is not the local geometry bug this row assumed.** The octant builds *correctly* — one valid solid, 2175.827 mm³ — and so does its mirror; **the first tiling fuse of the two returns an empty shape**, which is the IP-FC-49 failure mode returning at a different point. Findings, each measured: (a) *not a tolerance problem* — fuzzy fuse at 1e-9, 1e-7, 1e-6, 1e-5, 1e-4 and 1e-3 all return empty, and by the IP-FC-57 rule uniform failure across every distance means representation, not geometry; (b) *not repairable as a representation either* — `removeSplitter()`, rebuilding the solid from its own faces, `fix()` and a double refine all still fuse to nothing; (c) *the refine is implicated but is not the root* — the unrefined `SectionCut` **does** fuse with its mirror, but carried through all three tiling stages gives 12443.49 mm³ against the 17406.67 the octant implies, 28% short, so the unrefined octant is defective too; (d) `Shape.check()` names the defects — `SectionCut` has a self-intersecting vertex, its refine a 0.000335 mm edge; (e) *the obvious suspect was tested and rejected* — rewiring `SectionTools` past the unorientable `BulkheadCuts` refine (IP-FC-66) makes it valid and clean, but `SectionCut` is bit-identical and the tiling still collapses. **The single most important finding is that the working variants are not cleaner**: `1.5 end_bolt 0mm` carries a shorter minimum edge and *more* near-coincident vertex pairs than the failing one (IP-FC-67). So this is not one bad point in the space — the whole family is built on degenerate micro-geometry and this variant is where it lands somewhere the boolean cannot survive. **Fixing this variant by nudging a parameter would leave the fragility everywhere and is the wrong move.** The fix belongs in IP-FC-67: find which two constructions land 3e-4 mm apart and remove the degeneracy at its source. The only parameter difference from the working neighbour is `bolt_hole_radius`, 2.75 (anchor) against 2.0 (bolt), which feeds `bolt_boss_r = bolt_hole_radius + bolt_thickness` — 7.25 against 6.5. **Root cause located the same day, in the live FreeCAD session.** The 0.000335 mm edge is `Edge46`, a *Line* whose length is exactly twice the distance between its own endpoints — it folds back on itself — and one of the two faces meeting there, `Face29`, has a **negative area** of −0.00038 mm². Both sit on the plane x = −11.8, where **three independently derived fillet centers coincide**: `ocf_cx = flange_inner_x − flange_fillet_radius`, `bbf_cx = flange_inner_x − flange_fillet_radius`, and `gtw_cx = gtw_start − flange_fillet_radius`, the last collapsing onto the others because `gtw_start = max(flange_inner_x; −bolt_offset)` resolves to `flange_inner_x` (−8.8 against −12). **Separating them identifies the guilty pair exactly**: moving `bbf_cx` alone by 0.01 mm makes the part build as one solid at 17405.210 mm³, exactly 8× its octant, while moving `ocf_cx` or `gtw_cx` by the same amount changes nothing. So the interaction is `bbf_cx` against `ocf_cx` — two fillet centers that are the *same expression* and therefore always coincide, at every U and every panel. **The 0.01 mm nudge is the diagnostic, not the fix** — that is precisely the epsilon habit IP-FC-49 and IP-FC-59 removed. The fix has to make the two constructions either genuinely coincident (one shared feature) or genuinely apart by a dimension that means something | IP-FC-13, IP-FC-67 | |
| IP-FC-59 | done | **OQ-DES-B13, implemented 2026-08-14 — and the fix was not the one this row first proposed.** The symptom was a 0.01 mm `eps` in the bulkhead cleanup tool's radius `clean_r`, which cut that far below the flange face and left a step through the full thickness, eight times per part. Four hypotheses were written down and three were discarded on measurement, including this row's original "terminate the cleanup at the diagonal's true crossing" — that describes a symptom of the real defect, and deleting the `eps` on its own makes the joint **worse**. **The cause was one term in the corner.** Its rectangular extension was dimensioned `panel_overlap + panel_offset - panel_tolerance`, stopping one `panel_tolerance` short of the mating plane at `flat_x`. In the thin band below the flange face where the `corner_radius` circle has curved inboard, that extension is the outermost feature, so the corner's mating face had a `panel_tolerance`-deep notch in it and the bulkhead filled the notch and stood over the corner. **The fix is `rect_w = panel_overlap + panel_offset`**, and it repairs both halves at once because the bulkhead subtracts this same section as its greeble tool. With the face clean the `eps` became inert and was then removed. Applied to the OpenSCAD authority, the FreeCAD port and the port's transcription; **BACKENDS AGREE**, 10 parts, worst 0.00222%. It moves flown geometry by up to 10.3 mm³ on the corner and 2.4 mm³ on the bulkhead, **so it still wants a print check** | IP-FC-13, OQ-DES-B13 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B13](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-60 | done | **The corner/bulkhead interface now has a clearance parameter, `corner_tolerance`, resolving OQ-DES-C5.** Before this the two parts met on planes that were coincident *by construction* — the bulkhead cuts its socket with the same polygon the corner uses for its own boundary — so no parameter existed that could separate them, and the joint's only clearance was the greeble's 0.05 mm. The new parameter moves both seating faces inboard over the corner's full height, each measured **normal to itself**: `flat_x += corner_tolerance` and `flat_offset += corner_tolerance * sqrt(2)`, the root-two because the diagonal runs at 45° and dimensioning both the same way would give them different gaps. **It is carried entirely on the corner** — when the bulkhead re-evaluates the same description it passes 0, so the joint takes the clearance once. In OpenSCAD that is an explicit trailing `0` on the `corner_end` call; in the port it is structural, because `bulkhead_parameters()` does not export the row at all. Every entry point states the row rather than leaning on the module default, so `parameters.py` can check it — it only compares assignments that are written down. **The swept value is 0**, which is what every flown part was built at. Verified: 0 is a true no-op (`ref_corner_full` at 14146.8357350 mm³, the stored reference to the last digit); at a 0.05 mm test the parameter does reach the geometry (−55.6200845 mm³), both backends respond identically (3 corners, worst 0.00198%), and **4 bulkheads render to the same figures as at 0, to the last digit** | IP-FC-59, OQ-DES-C5 | [corner.md §OQ-DES-C5](../design/corner.md) |
| IP-FC-61 | done | **The unvaried parameters are a configuration file now, not literals in Python.** Raised 2026-08-14 on the observation that the new corner tolerance was not read from one — and neither was anything else, because no such file existed. The sweep had two file-based mechanisms and neither fits: `variant_param/*.csv` are **axes**, combined factorially by `flatten_param_space()`, so a column there makes a constant a swept dimension and multiplies the variant count; the cowl JSONs are shape definitions reached through a `parameter_filename` column that only the nose and tail type axes carry. **Membership is decided by measurement, not by category** — resolve the `Parameters` tree for every variant in all three families and keep the numeric fields that come back with exactly one value. Reading the source and picking out what looks like configuration finds the ones that are already tidy and misses the rest: that is how `boom_tolerance` = 0.2 and `cowl_flange_tolerance` = 0.2 were found, neither sitting near the others nor named like a setting at its definition site. Ten parameters qualified, and only two — longeron and greeble — were even reviewable constants beforehand; the rest were bare numbers inside `derived_parameters()`, or in `extrusion_width`'s case the same literal restated **seven times across three files**. [`src/Fuselage/design_constants.json`](../../src/Fuselage/design_constants.json) now holds all ten, keyed by the OpenSCAD parameter names so there is one vocabulary from the settings file to the geometry module, each with its rationale beside the number. Three groups, because each carries a different rule about what a legal value is: `tolerances` (non-negative — none of these joints is an interference fit), `printer` (strictly positive — a zero nozzle silently collapses every feature sized in multiples of it), `geometry` (unconstrained — a negative `boom_key_angle` is a legal rotation). `load_constants()` reads it once at import and **refuses a missing key, an unrecognized key, a missing group, a non-number and an out-of-range value** rather than defaulting — a settings file that substitutes its own value builds every part at a number nobody chose and reports success, which is `check_unseeded`'s failure one layer out. **Deliberately excluded:** the coefficients inside derivation formulas — the 4 in `max(panel_thickness, 4)`, the 3 in `max(3*U, 3)`, the `2*U` in the cowl flange height. Those are scaling rules rather than parameters; the sweep never passes them to geometry as values, it passes what they produce, and turning a rule into data is a separate decision. Verified a pure no-op — 12 parts, both backends, identical to the last digit; exported parameter tables byte-identical; and a changed value reaches the generated part definition, so `--resume` sees it | IP-FC-60 | [corner.md §OQ-DES-C5](../design/corner.md) |
| IP-FC-62 | done | **The printer profile was seven copies of one number, configured in two opposite ways.** Folded into IP-FC-61 rather than given its own file. `PrinterSettings` defaulted to `extrusion_width = 0.4` — the hand drivers' nozzle — and every one of the seven places that built one for the sweep overrode it to `0.6` on the next line: five in `fuselage_variants.py`, one in `fuselage_splode.py`, one in `render_variant.py`. Changing six and missing one would have produced a run built at two nozzle sizes with nothing to say so, since the parts differ only in wall thickness — which a volume comparison passes. `layer_height = 0.2` was never overridden anywhere, so one half of the profile lived in a dataclass default and the other in seven assignments. The defaults now come from `design_constants.json` and **all seven overrides are deleted**; `render_variant.settings()` and the sweep get the same nozzle by construction rather than by two files agreeing to restate it. A `printer` group was used rather than a separate file because the distinction that matters — machine versus design — is carried by the group and its validity rule, and a second file would have needed its own loader for two numbers. Note the exploration cells in `test_fuse.ipynb` still set 0.6 by hand, now redundant but harmless; one cell that used the bare default silently moves from 0.4 to 0.6, which is the nozzle the sweep actually uses | IP-FC-61 | [fuselage_folder_summary.md](../../src/Fuselage/docs/fuselage_folder_summary.md) |
| IP-FC-63 | done | **Every magic number in the parameter derivation is now named and read from config, and the joint analysis is in the repo.** IP-FC-61 moved the ten *resolved* constants — parameters the sweep passes to geometry whose value never varies. It did not touch the coefficients **inside** the derivation formulas, which is where the rest were: 26 more, across `standard_values`, `derived_parameters` and `derived_boom_bulkhead_parameters`. Two hazard patterns were hiding there. **A reciprocal pair** — `panel_offset = 0.25*ceil(4*offset)`, the same design quantity written as itself and its own inverse, so editing one half alone rounds to a grid nobody chose. **Eleven nominal-and-floor pairs** — `max(3*U, 3)`, `max(ceil(2*U)*w, 2*w)`, `max(U*0.5, 0.5)` — two independent literals that had to agree, with nothing saying so. Each now carries one name on both sides. **Naming did not restructure the arithmetic:** every literal was replaced in place, keeping operation order, because floating-point multiplication is not associative and rewriting `max(2*sqrt(U)*w, 2*w)` as `2*w*max(sqrt(U),1)` can move the last bit. The one exception is the quantum, where `q*ceil(x/q)` is bit-identical because both operands are powers of two. The loader also stopped coercing to `float`: `100` stays an int, because several of these reach solid2 and `100.0` in the generated `.scad` would re-render all 576 parts for nothing. **Verified against a clean worktree at 299633c** — the pre-change commit, since an earlier baseline had already been contaminated by IP-FC-61 — capturing all 576 parts before and after: the *only* field that differs anywhere is `corner.tolerance`, absent → 0.0, which is the parameter IP-FC-60 added. Left in code and argued for in the file header: structural zeros, `diameter/2`, the `sqrt(2)` diagonal, and the cowl path, which its own JSON already drives. For a cowling variant it threw `panel_offset is 0 for the bulkhead and 5.5 for the corner`. **Neither number is wrong.** A cowl bulkhead has no panel and no corner socket: the cowl closes that end instead, `bulkhead_validity_check()` refuses any cowling row with a non-zero panel, and the entire corner cut-out in `bulkhead_section` sits inside `if (!is_cowling)`. So on the bulkhead side those names are the *absence of a feature*, forced to 0; on the corner side they are that corner's own panel rebate, which it has regardless of what sits at the end of its bay — `derived_parameters(..., is_bulkhead=False)` never sees the type flags, so the corner half cannot know the station is a cowl and correctly does not care. The four panel-joint names are now exempt **for the COWLING family only**; on every other type they *are* the joint and exempting them would gut the check. Verified: all 16 cowl bulkheads (8 sizes x bolt and anchor) now export; the exemption does not apply when `is_cowling` is false; `corner_radius` and `bulkhead_thickness` mismatches are still refused on a cowl; `greeble_tolerance` stays globally exempt; non-cowling exports unaffected. **Recorded because the investigation went wrong twice:** the failure looks like a panel-axis duplication and is not one — 128 of the 144 cowling combinations never build, and the output is 2 parts per U under `panel_0mm`. | IP-FC-65 | done | **The FreeCAD backend rendered unported parts with OpenSCAD and filed them under the FreeCAD name.** `--backend freecad` fell back per part for any kind, or any *variant* of a ported kind, that had no FreeCAD generator — silently and by design, on the reasoning that a fallback part is still a correct part and that refusing would make the flag unusable until everything was ported. **That reasoning was wrong about what the output tree is for.** The tree is the record of what the port can do; an OpenSCAD mesh sitting in the FreeCAD tree under the FreeCAD name asserts the port built something it cannot build, and nothing downstream — a print job, a comparison, a person reading a directory — can tell without checking for a `.stl.scad` sidecar nobody knows to check for. IP-FC-47 was this same shape: parts that looked built and were not what they claimed. Closed in `solid_render`, which is the single choke point every fallback passes through — reaching it with the FreeCAD backend selected *is* the fallback, by definition — so one guard covers all five sweeps. The part is not produced at all and the refusal is recorded as `UnportedPart` in `RenderQueue.failures`. **`fail_fast` deliberately does not apply to a refusal**: hundreds are expected until IP-FC-12 lands, and raising would stop the FreeCAD sweep producing the documents it *can* produce. `compare_backends` now separates "no generator" from "tried and produced nothing" — without that split, closing the fallback would have buried every real defect among 226 expected ones. `--backend openscad`, the default, is untouched | IP-FC-13 | |

Two nodes each, always the same two: `BulkheadCuts` and `SectionTools` downstream of it. **The "on every variant" claim came from a sample of three that were all `0mm` panels** (`1.5 end_anchor 0mm`, `1.5 end_bolt 0mm`, `1.0 end_anchor 0mm`), which is the same sampling mistake IP-FC-55 records against auditing a *dimension* at one variant. That changes the diagnosis rather than just the count: `0mm` is where `panel_thickness`, `panel_overlap` and `panel_tolerance` are all zero and the OML's panel notch degenerates — the corner of the space IP-FC-46, IP-FC-54 and IP-FC-57 all came out of — so the unorientable result is the degenerate notch reaching the refine, not `Part::Refine` misbehaving in general. **A no-panel bulkhead is a swept, buildable configuration, so this is not academic**, and all 16 still build as one valid solid and agree with OpenSCAD; the invalidity stays latent, as this row already said. Fix and verification are unchanged — refining a *negative* buys nothing the subsequent cut does not redo — and the before/after measurement now has a target list of 16 rather than a corpus. **Superseded description follows.** **`Part::Refine` turns the fused cut tools into an unorientable shape, on every variant.** `BulkheadCuts` is `Part::Refine(CutFuse4)`. `CutFuse4` is a clean, valid single solid — `Shape.check()` passes — and refining it yields `valid=False` with `check()` reporting **"Unorientable shape"**, which then propagates into `SectionTools`. Observed identically on `1.5 end_anchor 0mm`, `1.5 end_bolt 0mm` and `1.0 end_anchor 0mm`, i.e. **including variants that build correctly and agree with OpenSCAD to 0.001%** — so it is latent, not currently fatal, and has been shipping in every FreeCAD bulkhead. Proven *not* to be the cause of IP-FC-58: rewiring `SectionTools` to consume `CutFuse4` directly makes it valid and `check()`-clean, but `SectionCut` downstream comes out bit-identical (2175.83363 mm³, same errors) and the tiling still collapses. Worth fixing on its own terms — an invalid negative is a boolean waiting to misbehave — and the fix is cheap to evaluate, since refining a *negative* buys nothing the subsequent cut does not redo. | IP-FC-67 | done | **The sub-micron substrate is gone, measured across the whole end-type corpus on 2026-08-16, and the residue that remains is 25 times larger and somewhere else.** The degeneracy this row opened on was the bolt-flange fillet's, and it was removed at its source by IP-FC-58 (the block starts at the region's leftmost vertex, making the ray cut transversal) and IP-FC-76 (the fillet center is solved from two `Tangent` constraints rather than substituted from a clamped closed form). All 88 build as one valid solid. Distribution of the shortest edge in `SectionCut`: **44 over 0.2 mm** (a real feature at the layer height), **39 between 0.01 and 0.2 mm**, **5 between 0.001 and 0.01 mm**, none below. **So "at every parameter set" no longer holds, and neither does the reading it supported** — that the variants which build are not cleaner, merely luckier. That was true of the geometry as it stood and is not true of the geometry as it stands; it is retained below because it is what made IP-FC-58 a design question rather than a local fix, which is what produced OQ-DES-B14. **The five variants under 0.01 mm are two sites, and neither is the bolt boss.** (a) `U_0.5 end_anchor` with the `0mm`, `1mm` and `1/32in` panels — one geometry appearing three times, the panel being absent or negligible at all three — carries a 0.00989 mm edge and a 0.0000621 mm² face on the plate's top surface `z = plate_thickness` at x ≈ 0, which is the octant's own mask plane rather than the flange stack; it survives the refine. Those three are also the only faces in the corpus under 0.0001 mm². (b) `U_0.75 end_bolt 1/16in` and `U_0.75 end_anchor 1/16in` carry a 0.00841 mm edge on `x = flat_x = clean_x0 = −4.75`, the corner/bulkhead joint plane, between the *stated* flange top at `y = clean_r = 5.8125` and the *built* outer face 0.0084 mm inboard at 5.80409 — which is exactly the distinction [`bulkhead_cuts.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/bulkhead_cuts.py) already warns against conflating, the joint being where the cleanup stops and the mold line's crossing being where the old overhang ended (IP-FC-59). The refine removes this one: `BulkheadSection`'s shortest edge there is 0.4667 mm. **Neither residue is a fold-back and neither is near a boolean's resolution**, so this closes rather than moving to a new row; if either grows, the scan is the way to see it. **Superseded description follows.** **The bulkhead octant carries sub-micron edges and near-coincident vertices at every parameter set, clustered at the bolt boss.** On `1.5 end_anchor 0mm` the shortest edge in `SectionCut` is **0.000335 mm** and two vertex pairs sit 0.00017 mm and 0.00081 mm apart; on the *working* `1.5 end_bolt 0mm` the shortest is 0.000395 mm with **three** such pairs — so the working variants are not cleaner, they are merely lucky. In both, the degeneracies sit at x = −11.8 in section coordinates, at z = 1.2 (`plate_thickness`) and z = 2.7 (`plate_thickness + flange_chamfer`), i.e. in the flange/plate stack around the bolt boss. This is the substrate IP-FC-58 fails on: features 3e-4 mm apart are four orders below the 0.2 mm layer height, cannot survive a boolean reliably, and represent no design intent. **Identified 2026-08-15, and it is a singularity in a parameter relationship, not a local modeling slip.** The bolt-flange fillet (`bbf`) puts its centre one fillet radius outboard of the flange face and finds the height where that vertical line meets the bolt ring: `bbf_cx = flange_inner_x − flange_fillet_radius`, `bbf_cy = sqrt(max(r_bolt_fillet² − bbf_dx²; 0)) + bolt_c`, where **`bbf_dx = bbf_cx − bolt_c` is the gap between that construction line and the bolt centerline**. As `bbf_dx → 0` the line collapses onto the bolt axis and the fillet block degenerates; the degenerate edge of IP-FC-58 sits at exactly `(bbf_cx, bbf_cy − flange_chamfer, plate_thickness)`, confirmed against both the failing and the working neighbour. **Scanned across all 88 valid end-type variants: 14 sit within 0.5 mm of `bbf_dx = 0`, 28 within 1.0 mm, and 12 of the 18 (type, panel) families cross zero as U varies.** Two variants — `1.5 end_anchor 1/4in` and `1.5 end_bolt 1/4in` — sit at **exactly 0.0000** and build correctly, which is the diagnostic shape of the problem: the *exactly* degenerate case is handled, and it is the near-degenerate neighbourhood that produces slivers. IP-FC-58 at 0.2000 mm is not even the closest to the singularity, merely the one that falls over. **So roughly a third of the frame-bulkhead corpus is being built inside an unstable band, and today only one part fails.** That makes this a design question before it is a code fix: the relationship `flange_inner_x − flange_fillet_radius ≈ −bolt_offset` is unconstrained, nothing in the parameter derivation keeps the flange fillet center away from the bolt axis, and it is not established whether those two features are meant to be independent at all. Raise as an OQ against bulkhead.md before changing construction | IP-FC-12, IP-FC-58 | |

| IP-FC-71 | done | **No geometry moves. Both models put the extent at exactly 120.1375 mm, and the whole disagreement is how each mesh writes that number.** Measured 2026-08-16 by rendering both variants through both backends — possible for the first time because IP-FC-70 gave `render_variant.py` a `--backend` flag, this row's "next step" having had no supported tool until then. **Which extent moved: x and y, all four, symmetrically** — min more negative and max more positive by the same amount, z exact to the digit, on both variants. A part uniformly larger in plan by half a thousandth on every side is not a displaced face; that symmetry says one number is serialized differently. **The number is `unit_width / 2 − (panel_thickness + panel_tolerance)` = 125 − 4.8625 = 120.1375 exactly**, seven significant figures. OpenSCAD writes **ASCII** STL at six significant figures and emits `120.137`; FreeCAD writes **binary** STL and stores `120.137496948`, the nearest float32 to the true value. The raw difference is **0.000497 mm, inside the 0.000500 mm tolerance.** **The reported failure was two stacked precision faults in the checker.** (a) `mesh_stats` rounds the box to four decimal places — a 1e-4 grid, one fifth of `BBOX_TOL` — before `compare_backends` subtracts, landing the two coordinates on different grid points and turning 0.000497 into exactly 0.000500. (b) Subtracting two ~120 mm doubles then gives 0.00050000000000238742, which fails `<= 5.0e-4` **by 2.4e-15 mm**. Fixed by asking `mesh_stats` for the box at full precision (`BBOX_PLACES`); the rounding stays for the `same_geometry` and display callers, which want a stable key rather than a difference. **This also answers the row's own puzzle, and its inference was wrong.** "Both types at the same U and the same panel fail together, and no other variant does" reads as evidence about that combination, and it is not: `U_3.0 3/16in` was rendered through both backends and carries **the same discrepancy to the last digit** — 0.00049694824218704525 mm, byte-identical to U=2.5's, at 145.137 against 145.1375. What differs is only where the rounded subtraction lands: **+2.4e-15 above the tolerance at 120.1375 and −1.2e-14 below it at 145.1375.** So which variants got reported was decided by the last bits of a double subtraction, not by anything about the parts, and the deterministic reproduction across two full runs — correctly observed — was the determinism of floating-point, not of geometry. **What is not fixed is a real limit and is raised as IP-FC-77**: six significant figures at a 120 mm coordinate is a 0.001 mm quantum, twice `BBOX_TOL`, so these parts pass now partly by which way OpenSCAD's write rounded. Had it gone up to 120.138 the raw difference would be 0.000503 and the check would still fail with the two models in exact agreement. **Superseded description follows.** **Two frame bulkheads move an interface dimension by 0.5 µm, the only geometry disagreements in a 461-part comparison.** `U_2.5 imperial 3/16in end_anchor` and `U_2.5 imperial 3/16in end_bolt` both fail on bounding box — 0.000500 mm against the 0.000500 mm `BBOX_TOL` — while their volumes agree to +0.00028% and +0.00001%, far inside tolerance. Volume agreement with a bounding-box shift means the part is the right size and in the wrong place, or has one face displaced, which is exactly what `BBOX_TOL` exists to catch: interface dimensions are strict in both tiers per OQ-DES-B9, because no interface is set by a fillet. **That both types at the same U and the same panel fail together, and no other variant does, says it is that combination rather than the bulkhead type** — the two differ only in `bolt_hole_radius`. Sitting exactly on the tolerance means the number itself is uninformative; the next step is to measure *which* extent moved and in which axis rather than to argue about the threshold. Worth checking against IP-FC-68, since a refine that moves geometry by 0.006 mm³ could displace a face by this order. **Reproduced exactly across two independent full runs** — same two parts, same 0.000500 mm, nothing else in 483 compared parts anywhere near it — so this is deterministic, not boolean noise | IP-FC-13, IP-FC-70 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B9](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-77 | done | **Decided and implemented 2026-08-16 as [OQ-ARCH-12](../architecture/freecad_migration.md): `BBOX_TOL` scales with `U`, the reference is not re-rendered, and the underlying limit expires with the OpenSCAD sweep.** `compare_backends.bbox_tol(u)` returns `5e-4 mm × U`, floored at the historical value below `U` = 1, and `u_of(name)` reads the size off the part filename the way `kind_of` reads the kind — the comparison is handed nothing but names and two rendered trees. An unreadable name raises rather than defaulting to `U` = 1, which would apply too tight a threshold to a large part and report a failure that is not real. The threshold clears the reference's six-figure quantum at every swept size: 5.0× headroom at `U` ≤ 1, 10× at `U` = 2.0, thinnest at 1.2× for `U` = 2.5 where the quantum has just stepped a decade. **Adopted without a corpus re-run, and that is sound rather than a shortcut**: with the floor in place the new threshold is greater than or equal to the old one at every size, so no part that passed can now fail — the change is monotonically loosening. The cost is the other direction, that differences between 5e-4 mm and `5e-4 × U` now pass silently, and from the last full run only IP-FC-71's two bulkheads were anywhere near the old threshold. **Alternative 1, re-rendering the reference as binary STL, was measured and declined** — OpenSCAD 2021.01 writes full float32, bit-identical to FreeCAD's, which would drive the difference to exactly zero, but it invalidates every stored reference tree to improve a check OQ-ARCH-4 has already scheduled for retirement. Verified end to end on the three parts rendered for IP-FC-71: all pass, `BACKENDS AGREE`, worst volume delta 0.00157%. **Superseded description follows, including one claim the decision overturned** — that loosening `BBOX_TOL` "would give up the thing the check exists for". That read the threshold as a fit tolerance; it is 200× tighter than a printed bolt clearance and is a noise floor around an expected zero, so scaling it with the noise gives up nothing the check was actually asserting. **Raised as [OQ-ARCH-12](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) on 2026-08-16 — the alternatives and the recommendation live there, not here.** This row was first written as three alternatives with no recommendation, which is an open question whether or not it is filed as one. The architecture document is where the port's verification strategy is decided, and OQ-ARCH-2, "what replaces the exact verification tier", is the question this one qualifies. The write-up there adds a **fourth alternative this row missed — accept the limit and let it expire with the OpenSCAD reference**, since OQ-ARCH-4 already scheduled that retirement; it is the same reasoning IP-FC-55 used to defer its own removal to IP-FC-34, and it is what makes this not blocking. Recommended there: binary STL for the reference, pending one cheap measurement — confirm this OpenSCAD build's binary writer is float32, then re-render the corpus to confirm no verdict changes. **Implementation waits on that decision.** The statement of the defect follows, and is the input to the question. **Above ~100 mm the bounding-box check compares serialization noise, not geometry.** `BBOX_TOL` is 5e-4 mm absolute and the OpenSCAD reference mesh is an **ASCII STL carrying six significant figures**, so its quantum is 1e-5 mm at a 10 mm coordinate, 1e-4 at 100 mm, and **1e-3 at 120 mm — twice the tolerance the check applies there.** Found by IP-FC-71, where a coordinate whose design value is 120.1375 is written `120.137`, putting the reference 0.0005 mm from the truth before any comparison happens. That is not a FreeCAD-side defect and no amount of care in the port removes it: **the check cannot resolve its own tolerance on the larger sizes**, and whether a part passes depends on which way the reference's sixth digit rounded. Everything at U ≤ 2.0 is unaffected, since those extents fit in six figures exactly. Interfaces are strict in both tiers per OQ-DES-B9 precisely because no interface is set by a fillet, so loosening `BBOX_TOL` to cover this would give up the thing the check exists for. **Three ways out, and the choice is a design decision rather than a bug fix.** (1) Raise the reference's export precision — OpenSCAD can emit binary STL, whose float32 gives about seven figures, which would put the quantum back under the tolerance at every swept size; it changes the authority's output format and every stored reference mesh. (2) Give `BBOX_TOL` a relative component sized to the reference's precision, which keeps the strict absolute limit on the small parts where it bites and states plainly that the large ones are checked less finely. (3) Compare each extent against its **derived design value** rather than mesh against mesh, which is the only option that does not inherit either mesh's precision — and the most work, needing the extents expressed as parameters. **Do not simply widen the tolerance**: that hides the limit rather than recording it, and the volume check would not catch a face moved by 1e-3 mm | IP-FC-71 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B9](../design/bulkhead.md) |

If any handle is open on a subdirectory — **an Explorer window browsing it is enough** — the delete is *pended* rather than performed: the directory entry survives and is still listed by its parent, but every subsequent operation on it fails with `STATUS_DELETE_PENDING`, which Win32 surfaces as "Access is denied". Observed on `freecad/U_0.5/metric/panel_0mm` and `.../U_1.0/metric/panel_0mm`; the tell that separates it from a real permissions problem is that **`Get-Acl` also fails** — an owner can always read an ACL, so failing to open the object for `READ_CONTROL` means the object is dying, not protected. All 11 ported variants at each point were affected, and the run reported them as "not produced by freecad", **which reads as a build failure and is not one.** Settled by re-running once the handle closed: all 22 build and compare clean, so the geometry was never in question and the sole effect was 22 parts silently dropped from a comparison that still printed a verdict. The state clears itself when the handle closes; nothing needs repairing. What needs fixing is the reporting: a part that produced no mesh because its output location was unusable needs the opposite response from one whose geometry would not build, and the two are currently identical in the table. Check the output directory is writable before rendering into it and fail loudly with the real reason; consider `--scratch` defaulting outside the repo (IP-FC-70), which also makes it far less likely anyone is browsing it. **Done 2026-08-18.** `ensure_wiped()` replaces both `shutil.rmtree(..., ignore_errors=True)` calls — the freecad output tree and the openscad reference — and the check it adds is exact rather than heuristic: **the tree is wiped on the way in every run, so anything surviving the wipe is a failure to remove it and never a legitimate state.** Where something does survive, `unusable_dirs()` names the directories that exist but cannot be listed, which is what separates the delete-pending case from an ordinary permissions problem, and the refusal carries the `READ_CONTROL` tell and the fact that nothing needs repairing because the state clears itself when the handle closes. The silent path is gone: a location that cannot hold the parts now stops the run before rendering instead of surfacing 22 variants later as "not produced by freecad". **Verified three ways**: with a real handle held open inside `freecad/U_0.5/metric/panel_0mm` it refuses and names the survivors, and the *same call* succeeds once the handle is closed; an ordinary populated tree is removed and the run continues; and a path that never existed is not an error.
