| IP-FC-73 | done | **Done 2026-08-17. All four rounded corners are solved by one sketch, `FilletTangency`, which carries only the corners the variant has.** It holds the four surfaces the corners are cut between — the flange's inner face, the flange's y face, the bolt boss and the greeble web's 45° wall face — and one circle per corner, each with two `Tangent` constraints and two reference dimensions the geometry reads its center back through. The four pair up around the corner: outer = flange face + flange y face, greeble-to-web = flange face + wall, bolt-flange = flange face + boss, web-to-bolt = boss + wall. `BffTangency` and `WtbTangency` merge into it, and **nine more sheet rows leave** — `ocf_cx`, `ocf_cy`, `gtw_start`, `gtw_cx`, `gtw_ex`, `gtw_ey`, `gtw_cy`, `gtw_dx`, `gtw_dy` — for twenty-six across the whole item. **Representation only, and measured as such**: `OuterCornerFillet` 8.344397, `BoltFlangeFillet` 38.156942 and `WebToBoltFillet` 89.952582 mm³ are unchanged to the digit, and the greeble-to-web body matches the construction it replaces to 2e-13 mm³ at three variants spanning both branches. **Four things this turned up, none of them predicted.** (a) **The cost the decision accepted is zero, not at most 0.042 mm³ per variant.** That figure was the fillet's net share of the *positive*, and positives are fused **before** the bolt hole is cut; the clamped body sits inside that hole, so the hole removes it again. Building the octant both ways across all 27 affected variants — `fillet_scope_analysis/sweep_clamped_gtw.py`, the whole population, no sampling — the finished part moves by less than 1e-6 mm³ in every one, so the port still agrees with OpenSCAD everywhere and this conversion did not need the whole-corpus treatment after all. Measuring it the other obvious way, by fusing the body into the finished octant, gives 1.374 mm³ and is equally wrong in the opposite direction: at that stage the fuse fills the hole back in. (b) **The existence predicate is not the one the decision guessed.** Not "does the web emerge from the corner block" but *the flange's inner face is inboard of the bolt center*: `greeble_web.py` builds the web as a strip from the corner at the origin to the bolt center and stops there, so the flange plane crosses it exactly while it lies inside that span. Same inequality the `max` tested, different use — the `max` picked another face to measure from, this decides whether the corner exists. (c) **Merging costs solver precision after a parameter edit.** `solve()` stops on a residual over the whole system, so a system four times the size converges less tightly per constraint: the two bolt-related centers land 1.2e-7 and 5.6e-8 mm from their tangencies after a bare recompute, against 3.5e-11 mm for the separate sketches. A second `solve()` on the same state returns 1.8e-15 mm, which is what identifies the cause as the convergence budget rather than anything geometric — and the *generated* document is unaffected, because the generator makes that call. The looser figure is what a delivered file holds once someone edits a parameter, and it is worst where the geometry is already ill-conditioned: the sample variant has the bolt-flange fillet 0.2 mm from the degeneracy `report_bbf_gap.py` tracks, where the center's y is extremely sensitive to any residual at all. A tenth of a nanometre on a printed part, with every assembled volume unchanged to six figures — so it is recorded rather than treated as a defect, and `check_tangency.py` now carries two tolerances and says which state each is for. Shorter reference lines were tried and do not help; the measurement that first suggested they did came from seeding endpoints at one length while the expression pinned them at another. (d) **A shared sketch broke two of the old checks in ways that made them weaker, and both are fixed.** `wtb_impossible` thickened the flange wall, which also marches `flange_inner_x` out of the bolt-flange fillet's reach — so under one sketch the refusal named the wrong corner and the check passed anyway. Now every seed is evaluated and *all* failures are reported rather than the first; the check asserts the message names its own fillet, and asserts the edit breaks no other corner. The sweep also has a driver per corner, since the two measured off the flange face do not move with `bolt_offset` at all and sweeping it proved nothing about them. `check_tangency.py` gained a fifth property — a corner the variant lacks is omitted, not relocated — and a reported (not asserted) measurement of what a delivered file does when edited past an existence boundary: the body survives, contributing at most 0.055 mm³ inside the octant, because which corners exist is fixed when the document is generated. That limit is stated rather than fixed; it is the same one every topology switch in this port has, including the bolt-flange fillet's quad-to-triangle. (e) **The refusal paths were barely reachable, and walking them deliberately found a real hole.** Three switches decide what the sketch and the bodies look like: `flange_inner_x` against `bolt_c` (the greeble-to-web corner exists; the bolt-flange profile is a quad or a triangle), `bolt_boss_r` against `flange_thickness / 2` (the web-to-bolt fillet is placeable), and the bolt-flange fillet's reach against its offset. **The swept corpus exercises only the first.** It crosses it in 27 of 148 variants and comes within 0.05 mm of it; the boss stays above **4.33 times** the wall half-thickness where the boundary is 1.0, and the bolt-flange reach comes within 0.45 mm and never crosses. So until now every refusal in the module was reachable only through one synthetic edit made from whichever seed was passed — which is how `wtb_impossible` came to be testing the wrong corner without anyone noticing. `check_tangency.py` now carries a **topology table**: 14 spots that walk each boundary from both sides, with the boundaries computed from the parameters rather than written down, so they stay meaningful at any seed. Verified identical at four seeds spanning both branches. **What it found:** the web-to-bolt fillet has a *second* boundary nobody had guarded. Its covering block runs from the bolt center out to where the fillet meets the wall, and that contact point marches back past the bolt center as the boss thins — so between `bolt_boss_r` = 1.000 and 1.114 times the wall half-thickness the tangency is perfectly satisfiable and the body still cannot be built. It failed with OCCT's `Length of box too small` and then "shape is invalid" raised several features downstream, **naming nothing** — the exact failure mode this item exists to remove, surviving inside the item that removes it. The boundary is where `(r + boss_r)² = (r + ft/2)² + (ft/2)²`; `fillets._wtb_seed()` now checks it and refuses by name, with a message about the block rather than about the tangency. The bolt-flange fillet has no equivalent band — it degrades smoothly to 4 mm³ at 0.001 mm from its boundary. **What a fillet *should* be in that band is not settled and is deliberately not asked**: no valid variant is within a factor of four of it, and the OpenSCAD source carries the same construction and the same limit, so refusing clearly is the whole of the port's obligation. Three of the table's spots are marked `unpinned` — a thickened wall moves three quantities at once and which boundary it reaches first genuinely depends on the seed, so they assert only that the outcome is *attributable*, never a fixed answer. Writing one seed's answer down as a rule is the habit this item exists to break. (f) **A seventh property, arrived at from `check_unread_rows.py` and stated where it belongs.** That tool reports `far` as reaching no geometry, which for this sketch means the solved centers do not depend on how long its reference lines are — three of the four features are line *segments* whose endpoints are pinned only so the sketch can reach full constraint, and a `Tangent` against a segment constrains the circle to its infinite line. Left as a survey result that is an incidental observation; the failure it guards against is quiet and the mistake is tempting, since constraining a fillet to a line's *endpoint* rather than the line still reaches full constraint, still solves, and still builds a plausible solid — with an arbitrary modeling convenience now wired into the geometry. `check_tangency.py` stretches the reference lines by 0.25x and 4x and requires every center to hold: measured at 2.3e-14 mm worst across three seeds. **All four sub-systems solve, hold their branch, refuse by name, omit what is absent, and still re-solve after a reload and an edit.** `fillet_intent.py` re-measured: all eight tangency claims hold across 148 variants at worst 3.6e-15 mm, with the greeble-to-web claim now stated against `flange_inner_x` — the old form measured against `gtw_start` and so tested the `max` against itself. **Rescoped 2026-08-17 by [OQ-ARCH-14](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), which is decided: convert all four rounded corners, and put them in ONE cohesive sketch that carries only the fillets active for the parameter set being built** — not four sketches whose inactive elements are overlaid on variants that do not use them. That addition is what makes converting all four coherent rather than a list being finished: *which fillets exist here* is a property of the variant that is currently expressed nowhere, and `greeble_to_web_fillet`'s `max(flange_inner_x; -bolt_offset)` is that question answered badly — it relocates the body onto the bolt centerline, inside the bolt hole, instead of omitting it. **Three things this adds to the row's original scope.** (a) **The two completed conversions are restructured, not left beside the new ones**: `BffTangency` and `WtbTangency` merge into the cohesive sketch. Both are bit-identical today (89.952582 mm³ and the `bulkhead_section` reference at 865.2850670), so re-checking them after the merge is the cheapest available regression test for the merge itself. (b) **A single sketch fails as a unit** — `solve()` and `FullyConstrained` are per object, so four tangency sub-systems in one sketch means one unsatisfiable sub-system reports as one failed sketch and loses which fillet caused it; `check_tangency.py` and the generator's guard must name the sub-system, or the refusal check gets weaker exactly as the number of things it covers goes up. `Constraints.<name>` is also a flat namespace within a sketch, so the existing `bbf_`/`wtb_` prefixes must stay. (c) **What decides "active" must not be the clamp wearing a different hat**: deciding activity from the same `max` comparison moves the defect rather than removing it. The predicate is a stated geometric condition — for the greeble-to-web fillet, whether the web emerges from the corner block at all — and the sketch refusing to solve stays a *check* that the predicate was right, never the mechanism that produces it, which is the branch-guard discipline IP-FC-76 established applied to existence instead of position. **This one conversion can move the flown part, and that is accepted rather than overlooked.** Omitting the inactive body means the 27 variants where the clamp fires lose it — at most 0.042 mm³ each, measured, but a real difference from the OpenSCAD reference that `compare_backends` will report. So it needs the whole-corpus treatment IP-FC-78 gets, not the bit-identical check the other three get. Evidence: the fillet contributes net zero in 33 of 44 measured variants and at most 0.042 mm³ in the rest; two thirds of its outline is a squared-off block sitting inside the greeble web, in every variant, since both areas are exact multiples of `r²`; and the web has no greeble end at all in 5 of the 9 bolt-branch variants measured, with a 0.35–1.33 mm³ sliver in the other 4 that the clamp still ignores. Tools: `fillet_scope_analysis/measure_fillet_share.py`, `sweep_fillet_share.py`, `measure_fillet_context.py`, figures from [`tools/draw_fillet_scope.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_fillet_scope.py). **Original scope follows.** **Stage 1 of OQ-ARCH-11 — express derived features as constrained sketches, keeping CSG for bulk material.** The port currently states no geometric constraint anywhere: across every module the vocabulary is `Coincident`, `Horizontal`, `Vertical`, `PointOnObject`, `DistanceX`, `DistanceY`, all on the single sketch in a 158-object bulkhead. Relationships that a CAD model would state are solved in the spreadsheet and stored as coordinates — `bbf_cy = sqrt(max(r_bolt_fillet^2 - bbf_dx^2; 0)) + bolt_c` is a tangency written out as a quadratic, and its `max(...; 0)` is a clamp that returns a plausible wrong position instead of reporting an unsatisfiable relationship. Convert the features whose position is *derived* from another feature, **starting with the five in `fillets.py`**, since that is where IP-FC-58 came from. **This does not cost the OpenSCAD comparison** — `compare_backends` measures the built solid's volume and bounding box, never a closed-form parameter-to-coordinate map — so every conversion is verified exactly as today. Two things make it more than mechanical: each feature's intent must be established and written down before it can be constrained — for the bolt-flange fillet that is now done, and it turned out to be recoverable from the algebra rather than needing a designer's ruling: the center is `flange_fillet_radius` from the flange inner face and `flange_fillet_radius + bolt_hole_radius + bolt_thickness` from the bolt center, so it is tangent to both to 3.6e-15 mm across all 88 end-type variants and the conversion is two `Tangent` constraints that do not move the part (OQ-DES-B14). **Nothing equivalent is recorded for the other four**, and the same check should be run on each before converting it, and a constraint with two solutions can converge on the wrong branch, which the mesh comparison detects but does not prevent — the first conversion should establish how that is guarded. Exit criterion: every derived feature states its relationship and the whole corpus still agrees with OpenSCAD. **The bolt-flange fillet was converted first, on 2026-08-16, and it answered both of the open method questions — IP-FC-76 has the detail.** Intent proved recoverable from the algebra rather than needing a ruling: the center is tangent to the flange face and to the bolt boss, so the conversion is two `Tangent` constraints and the part does not move. Branch convergence is guarded by keeping the closed form as a test of the solved position rather than as its source, checked over a swept parameter. One limit found there applies to the remaining four, and it is real: the parameter sheet may never read a sketch that reads the sheet. A second supposed limit — that a profile cannot be sketched where it changes topology across the parameter space — was wrong and is withdrawn; the generator emits one document per parameter set and can choose the topology. **Intent is now recorded for all of them, measured 2026-08-16 across all 148 valid bulkhead variants** — the gate this row set, cleared in one pass by [`tools/fillet_intent.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/fillet_intent.py), which is kept because it has to be re-run whenever the geometry moves. **Every one of the four is a circle of radius `flange_fillet_radius` touching two named features, and every claim holds to machine precision — worst residual 3.553e-15 mm.** `outer_corner_fillet`: tangent to the flange inner face and to the flange y face, two perpendicular planes, residual exactly 0 on one of them since it is pure subtraction. `greeble_to_web_fillet`: tangent to the greeble start face and to the 45° wall face. `web_to_bolt_fillet`: tangent to the **bolt boss** and to the 45° wall face — structurally the same pair as the bolt-flange fillet, one circle and one plane. The already-converted `bolt_flange_fillet` is checked alongside them so a regression in it shows up here. **So none of the three needs a designer's ruling**, and each conversion is two `Tangent` constraints that do not move the part, exactly as OQ-DES-B14 turned out to be. **The fifth feature in this file is not a rounded corner, and it is out of this item's scope by decision — [OQ-ARCH-13](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), decided 2026-08-16: leave the construction alone now, make it a real chamfer feature with the `PartDesign` move, tracked as IP-FC-78.** So **this item is four conversions, not five**. **Two are done and the last two are on hold: [OQ-ARCH-14](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), raised 2026-08-16.** Applying OQ-ARCH-13's own test to them — a position that must be *worked out* belongs in this work, one that is *measured* does not — puts `outer_corner_fillet` on the excluded side: its center is two perpendicular faces each minus the radius, with no discriminant, no branch and no configuration that can fail. `greeble_to_web_fillet` is arithmetic too, but its `max(flange_inner_x; -bolt_offset)` selects *which* face it is tangent to, which is a relationship worth stating. Recommended there: convert the greeble-to-web fillet only, or neither if the conditional reference needs machinery the two completed conversions did not establish. **After the two conversions no clamped square root remains in the module**, which is the defect class this work started from. `bulkhead_flange_chamfer` fills the internal corner where the inside of the flange meets the top of the plate, with a 45° face 1.0 mm along each — **material added, not cut**: it is one of eight positives union'd to make the flange. Both it and the four rounded corners fill a corner between two surfaces, so the difference is *not* that one has a relationship and the others do not. The difference is that a rounded corner of a given radius has exactly one position where it touches both surfaces and finding it takes algebra — which is what was done by hand and stored as coordinates, and what this work exists to replace — whereas the chamfer's two ends are simply 1.0 mm from the corner along each surface. There is nothing to solve. That matters because **the work these five belong to describes what it covers two ways in one sentence** — "the features whose position is derived from another feature", and "the five fillets and chamfers in `fillets.py`" — and the chamfer matches the second but not the first. The measurement above is what made that visible. Until it is settled there is no way to say this work on the bulkhead is finished, though it holds up none of the three remaining rounded corners. **Two facts stated 2026-08-16 settle where it should end up, and neither is recoverable from the code.** Its purpose is **strain relief** at the interior corner, running the full interior perimeter of the flange and around to the bolt or anchor — so the feature is defined by the edge it follows, and its size is structural. And **the two-prism construction is a workaround for OpenSCAD**, which cannot designate an edge and chamfer it; the port transcribed that faithfully. So the right end state is a real chamfer feature on that edge, blocked only until edge references survive a rebuild — not the "leave it alone permanently" that OQ-ARCH-13 first recommended, which reasoned from the construction as though the construction were intended. **Recorded because the first write-up of this row also had the geometry backwards** — described as a cut across the flange's top edge — which tracing the built solid corrected. Diagrams from [`tools/draw_flange_chamfer.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_flange_chamfer.py) over a snapshot written by `chamfer_analysis/measure_chamfer_context.py`. **Recommended order: `web_to_bolt_fillet` first**, because it carries the last `sqrt(max(...; 0))` in the module — the clamp OQ-ARCH-11 named as the hazard, which returns a plausible wrong position rather than reporting an unsatisfiable relationship. Worth stating that it is **not** currently near firing: it needs `bolt_boss_r < flange_thickness / 2` and the closest approach in the corpus is 3.6 mm, where the bolt-flange fillet's equivalent reached exactly 0.0000 on two variants. Then `outer_corner_fillet` as the simplest, then `greeble_to_web_fillet`, whose `max(flange_inner_x; -bolt_offset)` is a selection between two faces rather than a clamp and stays legitimate — **that last clause is wrong and the decision overturns it**: it is a clamp, and it fires into the bolt hole | IP-FC-58, OQ-DES-B14, OQ-ARCH-14 (decided) | [OQ-ARCH-14 — decided: convert all four into one cohesive sketch](../architecture/freecad_migration.md#oq-arch-14--do-the-last-two-fillets-need-converting-by-the-test-just-adopted--decided-2026-08-17-convert-all-four-into-one-sketch-carrying-only-the-fillets-active-for-that-variant), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-11](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B14](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-74 | blocked (IP-FC-21, IP-FC-19) | **Stage 2 of OQ-ARCH-11 — bind drawings and assemblies to the constrained features.** IP-FC-21's family drawings and IP-FC-19's assembly joints both need references that survive a rebuild; today they would attach to faces of a 158-node boolean history, which is where topological names are least stable. These are the features that make stage 1 pay, and they should **pull IP-FC-73 forward** for whatever they need first rather than waiting for it to finish — **IP-FC-73 is done as of 2026-08-17, so what stage 1 produced is available now**: the bulkhead's four rounded corners are placed by named reference dimensions on one sketch, `FilletTangency`, rather than by spreadsheet coordinates, and a sketch dimension is a stable name where a boolean's face is not. Nothing else is converted yet, so anything wanting a *surface* still faces the topological-naming problem this row is really about | IP-FC-21, IP-FC-19 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-11](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-75 | blocked (IP-FC-13) | **Stage 3 of OQ-ARCH-11 — convert to `PartDesign::`, the target state.** OQ-ARCH-11 records that this is what a FreeCAD model of these parts would look like had they been authored in FreeCAD rather than translated into it. **Gated on OQ-ARCH-4 firing, not on preference**: that decision already retires OpenSCAD once IP-FC-13 demonstrates whole-corpus equivalence, at which point a frozen FreeCAD corpus becomes the reference and `compare_backends` retargets to it unchanged, since it compares meshes. Conversion is then verifiable body by body against frozen geometry, and IP-FC-73 will already have produced the constrained sketches PartDesign features consume. **The single-solid concern was measured on 2026-08-15 and is not a blocker.** PartDesign's idiomatic construction for this symmetry is one base feature plus a **`PartDesign::MultiTransform`** holding an ordered list of `Mirrored` transformations — *not* `Mirrored` features chained into one another, which fails, because a transformation takes the original *features* as its `Originals` rather than the accumulated solid. Built and measured: octant pad 4875.0 mm³, MultiTransform with three mirrors → **one valid solid at 39000.0 mm³, exactly 8×**, bounding box symmetric. The 45° mirror needs a `PartDesign::Plane` datum, which is itself parametric and can be expression-bound like anything else. The single-solid rule is the `Body.AllowCompound` property, not a hard law. **The real hazard is its failure mode, and it is the shape this project keeps getting bitten by**: with the default `AllowCompound = False`, a construction whose mirrored copies do *not* touch silently keeps only the connected part and discards the rest — measured at **ratio 2.0000 instead of 8.0000, reporting `Up-to-date` with no error**. With `AllowCompound = True` the same case yields 4 disjoint solids at the correct 8.0000. So the conversion must assert the tiling ratio rather than trust the state, exactly as `bulkhead_full.py` already asserts 8× the octant. Favorably, the input PartDesign wants is what IP-FC-49 already established — exact touching with **zero** overlap; the 0.01 mm overlap it removed measures 7.9963 here, not 8 | IP-FC-13, IP-FC-12, IP-FC-73 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-11](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-4](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-78 | blocked (IP-FC-75) | **Make the flange chamfer a real chamfer feature, replacing the two prisms that stand in for one — [OQ-ARCH-13](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), decided 2026-08-16.** `bulkhead_flange_chamfer` is built as two pentagonal prisms, each a box with a second box rotated 45° subtracted from it, placed by nine spreadsheet rows in a rotated frame of its own. **That is not a design — OpenSCAD has no way to designate an edge and chamfer it, so the shape had to be built explicitly, in as many pieces as the corner has runs, and the port transcribed the workaround faithfully.** The feature itself is strain relief along the interior corner between the flange and the web, running the full interior perimeter of the flange and continuing around the bolt or anchor, so it is **defined by the edge it follows** and its size is a structural quantity. Applying a chamfer to that edge replaces all nine rows with the one parameter that means "how much relief", and makes the feature track the corner instead of having to be re-derived by hand whenever the flange outline moves — today nothing would report it if that were missed. **Gated on IP-FC-75 rather than on preference**: it needs to name an edge of a shape produced by a boolean, and those names are not stable across a rebuild, which is the same obstacle IP-FC-74 is waiting on; `PartDesign` is both where that becomes tractable and where a chamfer operation is the natural way to say this. **This is the one conversion in the OQ-ARCH-11 work that can move the flown part**, so it does not get the bit-identical check the rounded corners get — it needs a whole-corpus comparison against the reference of the day, and the behavior where two runs meet at a corner must be measured rather than assumed. Until then `fillets.py` records beside the construction that it is a workaround, so it is not mistaken for intent | IP-FC-75, OQ-ARCH-13 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-13](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-76 | done | **OQ-DES-B14 implemented 2026-08-16 — the bolt-flange fillet's center is solved from two `Tangent` constraints instead of computed.** The first conversion of IP-FC-73, and the one that establishes how the rest are done. `bbf_cx` was a subtraction and `bbf_cy` a square root under `max(...; 0)`; what the pair describes is a circle of radius `flange_fillet_radius` touching the flange's inner face and the bolt boss at once, which is now drawn and constrained rather than reconstructed by a reader. `fillets._tangency_sketch()` builds `BffTangency`, fully constrained, and the CSG reads the solved center back through reference dimensions; seven derived rows (`bbf_cx`, `bbf_cy`, `bbf_dx`, `bbf_dy`, `bbf_bx`, `bbf_r`, `bbf_ang`, `bbf_hx`, `bbf_hy`) leave the sheet. **Representation only, and measured as such**: the fillet, the flange positive, the octant, the tiling and the full bulkhead are bit-identical to the arithmetic they replaced at `1.5 end_anchor 0mm`, `bulkhead_section` holds 865.2850670 against its 865.2850690 reference, and a sampled backend comparison agrees with OpenSCAD to 0.0016% worst case — including `U_0.5 imperial 1/32in end_anchor`, one of the 18 variants where the profile is a triangle rather than a quad. **Four things the arithmetic never had to prove are now asserted permanently** in `freecad/check_tangency.py` (named `check_bff_tangency.py` until IP-FC-73 converted a second fillet into it): the sketch reaches full constraint and agrees with the tangencies it states; it stays on the same branch across a swept `bolt_offset` (worst 3.5e-11 mm over ±3.5 mm); an unsatisfiable configuration is refused rather than clamped; and the delivered `.FCStd` still re-solves after a reload and a parameter edit. **Two constraints on how this can be done, both found the hard way and both general to IP-FC-73.** The profile stays in half-planes as a matter of scope, not capability — an earlier version of this row argued it *could not* be sketched, because it degenerates from a quad to a triangle on 18 of 88 valid end-type variants where `bbf_sx = max(flange_inner_x; bolt_c)` resolves to `bolt_c`. **That argument was wrong and is retained here so the correction is visible**: it assumed one sketch must serve the whole parameter space, and nothing requires that, since a document is generated per parameter set. Measured instead of argued, a four-edge profile sketch is exact up to *and including* the exactly-degenerate point and fails only past it — and testing that turned up a defect worth more than the claim it disproved: past the degeneracy the solver returns −1 from all four algorithms while `FullyConstrained` stays True and the extrusion keeps serving the last geometry that solved, 42 mm³ adrift. `corner_tree._sketch()` gated on `FullyConstrained` alone and now checks `solve()` first, which covers the corner's two sketches as well. The second constraint is real and stands: **the sheet may not read a sketch that reads the sheet** — FreeCAD's dependency graph is per object, so that cycle reports "The graph must be a DAG" and then leaves the sketch permanently touched and never recomputed, holding stale values that still look right; solved values therefore flow sketch → geometry only, and the quantities that were sheet rows are now expressions on the objects that need them. Alternative 5 of the same question is implemented alongside: `fuselage_variants.bolt_flange_fillet_gap()` measures the gap and `tools/bbf_analysis/report_bbf_gap.py` reports it across the corpus, flagging the band without rejecting anything — it spans −5.0500 to +4.7500 mm with 28 of 88 inside 1.0 mm of zero. **`BffTangency` no longer exists as a separate object**: IP-FC-73 merged it into `FilletTangency` on 2026-08-17, along with `WtbTangency` and the two remaining corners. Everything above still describes what it does — the two `Tangent` constraints, the reference dimensions, the branch guard and the four permanent checks are unchanged — but its geometry now lives beside the other three corners' in one sketch, and `bbf_cx`/`bbf_cy` are read from there. The one measurement that did not survive the merge is the branch-sweep residual: 3.5e-11 mm became 1.2e-7 mm after a bare recompute, for the reason given in IP-FC-73 | IP-FC-58, IP-FC-73 | [bulkhead.md §OQ-DES-B14](../design/bulkhead.md) |
| IP-FC-79 | done | **The web-to-bolt fillet's center is solved from two `Tangent` constraints instead of computed — the second conversion of IP-FC-73, 2026-08-16, and the one that removes the last clamped square root from `fillets.py`.** `wtb_tan` was `sqrt(max(r_bolt_fillet ^ 2 - wtb_a ^ 2; 0))`, the same shape of expression OQ-ARCH-11 named as the hazard: past the clamp it returns a plausible wrong position rather than reporting that the relationship cannot be met. What those rows describe is a circle of radius `flange_fillet_radius` touching the **bolt boss** and the **greeble flange wall's face** at once, which `fillets._wtb_tangency_sketch()` now draws and constrains as `WtbTangency`. **Intent was established before anything was constrained**, per the rule IP-FC-76 set: both tangencies hold as identities to 3.6e-15 mm across all 148 buildable bulkheads (`tools/fillet_intent.py`). **Fourteen rows leave the sheet** — `wtb_a`, `wtb_tan`, `wtb_cx`, `wtb_cy`, `wtb_sx`, `wtb_dx`, `wtb_dy`, `wtb_r`, `wtb_ang`, `wtb_hx`, `wtb_hy`, `wtb_sum`, `wtb_45x`, `wtb_45y` — and two of them collapse into identities the tangency makes true by construction rather than by algebra, each measured over the same 148 at worst 3.6e-15 mm: `wtb_r` is exactly `r_bolt_fillet`, since the center sits one fillet radius outside a boss of `bolt_boss_r`; and `wtb_sx`, previously reached through the clamped root, is `wtb_cx + flange_fillet_radius / sqrt(2)`, the x of the point where the fillet meets the wall. **Representation only, and measured as such**: `WebToBoltFillet` is bit-identical at 89.952582 mm³, and `bulkhead_positive` (+0.00028%), `bulkhead_section` (865.2850670, −0.00000%) and `bulkhead_full` (6922.2805355, +0.00011%, exact tiling) all hold their references to the last digit, with the octant gaining exactly one node and one sketch. **The permanent check was generalized rather than copied**: `check_bff_tangency.py` became `check_tangency.py`, which runs the same four assertions per converted fillet from a `Case` table, so the two conversions left in IP-FC-73 add a table entry instead of a fourth near-identical script. Both sketches pass: fully constrained, agreeing with the tangencies they state, refusing an unsatisfiable configuration, and still re-solving after a reload and a parameter edit. The new sketch holds its branch to **1.1e-14 mm** over a ±3.5 mm sweep of `bolt_offset`, against 3.5e-11 mm for the first. **`WtbTangency` no longer exists as a separate object** — IP-FC-73 merged it into `FilletTangency` on 2026-08-17 with the other three corners. What it states is unchanged; where it states it is not. Two things this row asserted did not survive the merge intact and are corrected there: the branch-sweep residual, which loosens to 5.6e-8 mm after a bare recompute because one solver now covers four sub-systems, and `wtb_impossible`, which thickened the flange wall and so also pushed the flange face out of the **bolt-flange** fillet's reach — harmless while the two had their own sketches, and enough to make the refusal name the wrong corner once they shared one | IP-FC-73, IP-FC-76 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-11](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-17 | done (residue in IP-FC-115, IP-FC-116, IP-FC-117) | **Both cowls build as shelled solids as of 2026-09-04.** The constraint OQ-DES-CW6 attached still holds and is now enforced by construction rather than by care: the interior is *additive*, so `nose_cowl_shell` and `tail_shell` are **two more part kinds beside** `nose_cowl` and `tail` and never replacements, the UC-1 print export keeps coming from the un-shelled notched blank, and the shelled kinds are **FreeCAD-only** -- asking for one on the OpenSCAD backend raises rather than quietly rendering the blank. The rib is not modelled as a feature: eroding the notched section reproduces it at exactly `2*n_p*w + t_cut` = 1.3 mm, and that is measured rather than assumed. **What landed:** [`cowl_interior.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/cowl_interior.py), the algorithm; [`cowl_nose_cowl_shell.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/cowl_nose_cowl_shell.py) and [`cowl_tail_shell.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/cowl_tail_shell.py), the two entry points, which differ only in which blank they ask `cowl_tree` for -- the construction functions are shared, so a fix on one cowl is a fix on both; [`check_cowl_interior.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/check_cowl_interior.py), the acceptance tests of [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md) section 6; and the kinds wired through `part_kinds.py`, `build_part.py` and `fuselage_variants.py`. **Measured at `U` = 1:** the nose wall is 9714.2617 mm3 in one valid solid of 503 faces, worst wall error 0.0374 mm over 9 stations; the tail is 23685.2264 mm3 in one valid solid of 2480 faces, worst wall error 0.0480 mm over 16 stations; the tolerance is 0.05 mm and every station count is reached by refinement, not chosen. Both are rendered to `variant_output_cowl/U_1.0/` with STL, preview and `.FCStd`, and the four print kinds beside them are untouched. **Five things the algorithm document did not say and the build required** are recorded as section 9 of [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md); the two load-bearing ones are that the morphological identity is evaluated in **3-D** -- the ribs are dilated as solids and cut from the smooth interior, rather than each section being eroded with its notches already in it, because a surface fitted through notched contours runs a smooth patch across the crease each rib makes -- and that **fitting spacing and checking spacing are two different numbers**, because what the tolerance is on is the printed perimeter width and not the surface agreeing with itself. **Two defects that passed every check are written up below**, and neither was visible in the part | IP-FC-12, IP-FC-16, IP-FC-14, IP-FC-42 | [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md), [cowl.md §6.4](../design/cowl.md) |
