| IP-FC-20 | blocked (IP-FC-18) | UC-8 tier 1 — mass properties: densities per component, mass, CG, inertia tensor | IP-FC-18 | [freecad_migration.md §UC-8 is a ladder](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-21 | in progress | UC-7a — part drawings as a **family drawing**: lettered dimension callouts plus a per-variant value table. Interface dimensions are the required floor ([dimension_scheme.md](../design/dimension_scheme.md), IP-FC-36). **The headless question is answered — TechDraw drives from `freecadcmd`, and [`spike_techdraw.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/spike_techdraw.py) is the evidence, 2026-08-21.** It was an unasked gate of exactly IP-FC-35's shape: TechDraw is the workbench in this project most obviously written for a GUI, since dimensions are normally placed by dragging them, and a sweep cannot contain a step that needs a window. A page takes a real template, a view projects, and both come back **as coordinates** — `getVisibleEdges()` returns `Part.Edge` objects in view space, which is what a placer needs in order to avoid the geometry it is annotating. **`TechDraw.writeDXFPage` is the deliverable and the format was not chosen, it fell out**: it carries the `DIMENSION` entities and the placement, and two builds of the same page exported **byte-identical** files, which is [dimension_scheme.md §5.5](../design/dimension_scheme.md)'s determinism requirement met by the artifact itself — a drawing set that can be reviewed by `diff`. `TechDrawGui` refuses to import in a console application, so the GUI page exporters are unavailable and `viewPartAsSvg` returns a bare view fragment with no template and no dimensions; the `.FCStd` is **not** the reviewable artifact, since it differs run to run. **How a dimension binds to parameters rather than to projected topology, which is what the note below demands.** `makeCosmeticVertex3d` accepts a point in *model* coordinates and the view projects and scales it, so a dimension can reference two points computed by arithmetic on the parameters — never asking the projection what its edges are called. Verified end to end: two points 20 mm apart in 3D, dimensioned on a 2:1 view, report `20.00 mm`, so the view scale does not leak into the value. **The trap is the index.** Cosmetic vertices are appended *after* the projected ones, so a reference is `Vertex(n_geometric + i)` and `n_geometric` moves with `U` — IP-FC-5's face-count finding wearing a different hat. A literal `Vertex4` is the same defect the whole binding rule exists to prevent; the count must be read immediately before the vertices are made. **Two things the drawing needs that TechDraw will not supply.** It emitted a `DistanceX` across a vertical edge as a confident `0.00 mm` and raised nothing, so §5's H5 — never place a structurally-zero dimension — has to be enforced by the placer; and the drawing's *appearance* is not in the document at all. The template resolves through `App.getResourceDir()` to a file inside the FreeCAD installation, and TechDraw's `Labels`, `Dimensions` and `General` preference groups are **empty on this machine**, so font and text size come from a compiled-in default a user preference can silently change. **Both have to become project data before the first drawing is generated**, alongside the dimension scheme, the way `design_constants.json` carries the tolerances. If the font is vendored its license is a question for IP-FC-6 — `osifont` is LGPL-3 with a font exception. **Annotation extent was filed as blocking (OQ-ARCH-18) and is not — withdrawn 2026-08-22.** `getArrowPositions()` does return `Vector (0, 0, 0)` for both arrowheads headless and nothing reports the text's rendered width, but the question was posed against `20.00 mm`, and OQ-ARCH-7 put values in the table and a **single lettered callout** on the view. Remeasured on capitals at 3.5 mm: the worst cross-font spread is 1.270 mm rather than 6.139 mm, and every callout is the same length, so bounding each by the widest letter in the widest candidate font (`W`, 3.461 mm) is a uniform over-estimate — it shifts the layout without distorting it. Arrowhead extent is a fixed multiple of the text height. **What the withdrawn question leaves behind as work rather than as a decision:** pin the font, its size and the template as project data, and size the value table's columns to their widest cell, which is a grid problem and not §5.2's collision problem. Exact advance widths are available if wanted — `fontTools 4.61.1` imports inside `freecadcmd` and TechDraw ships `osifont` and two Y14.5 faces . **The appearance is pinned and the placer is built, 2026-08-22.** [`drawing_standard.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/drawing_standard.py) is the sheet, the font and the extent bound as project data — ANSI A landscape, `osifont` at the ISO 3098 3.5 mm height, and a **23-letter callout alphabet with I, O and Q omitted** because they read as 1, 0 and 0. The font is **not vendored**: it is referenced in the install and its metrics are *asserted*, which closes the same hole without taking IP-FC-6's licensing decision — `require_font` recomputes the recorded advances from the file actually found and refuses to build if they disagree. Verified against a substituted face (DejaVu Sans is named as wrong on both the callout and the digit bound), a missing file, and a missing glyph. **A finding that contradicts the natural assumption: osifont's digits are not tabular** — `1` is 700 units and `8` is 1056, a 50 % spread — so a value-table column cannot be sized by counting characters, and `digits_width_mm` bounds each digit position by the widest digit instead. [`dimension_placement.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/dimension_placement.py) is §5's solver, and it takes plain 2D coordinates rather than a `DrawViewPart`, so it is testable without building a part. §5.4's two tunables are the ISO 129-1 spacings expressed in text heights — `g = 3·h`, `s = 2·h`, giving the conventional 10.5 and 7.0 mm at 3.5 mm text — which makes *"both scale with text height, not with `U`"* true by construction rather than by discipline. **The independent checker earned its keep on its first input, which is the strongest evidence for §5.6's rule that it be independent.** The placer's original lane rule let a small dimension and a larger one containing it share lane 0, because their *text* did not collide; that draws two collinear dimension lines and puts a witness line through one of them, and the checker named it as H4. A placer certifying its own output would have reported success. **Two defects came out of that one failure.** Lane sharing now requires **disjoint spans**, not merely non-colliding text. And `_segments_cross` was reporting every witness line as crossing its own dimension line: the usual `(d1 > 0) != (d2 > 0)` form reads an exactly-zero orientation as negative, and a witness *ending on* a dimension line is precisely the zero case — it now requires strictly opposite signs, and is unit-tested on seven cases including the touch, the through-crossing and the collinear overlap. **The nesting sort is on the span in view coordinates, not on the model value.** For a linear dimension the two are the same number scaled, so on well-formed input the orderings match — but the span is what nesting depends on, and sorting on it makes H4 structural instead of contingent on the caller's value agreeing with the geometry. [`check_dimension_placement.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/check_dimension_placement.py) runs the pair over cases chosen for difficulty per §5.7: nested-from-a-datum, interleaved spans, four equal magnitudes where every tie falls to the letter, and a dense twelve-dimension case that places in four lanes. All clean; all three refusals refuse; placement is unchanged under input reversal, which is §5.5. **What remains:** `drawing.py` to bind the solver to a real `DrawViewPart` through the cosmetic-vertex mechanism above, the value table, and the partition of the 576 variants into topology families per §5.1 . **`place` now refuses rather than returns a violating layout.** H5 and the repeated-letter rule were enforced on the way in and H2 holds by construction from the lane rule, but H3 and H4 are properties of the finished layout and were never tested — so a witness line through a dimension line would have been returned without complaint, which is the exact case §5.6 exists for. Both are now regressions: four mutually interleaved spans (two sides cannot separate four of them, so no lane order avoids H4) and an edge lying where the only available callout position falls. **Blocked on [OQ-DES-D1](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) for the sheet, not for the solver.** The topology partition was computed and gives **18 families**, which is the number the family drawing was meant to produce — but the corner's panelled family is **384 rows** against a sheet holding about 25, and twelve of the eighteen overflow. Measured while filing it: across every part family, **the entire coupled surface of the sweep is two fields** — `panel.offset` wherever there is a panel, and `corner.length` for the corner — with everything else following exactly one size axis, and the two cowling bulkheads coupled to nothing. So factoring the table by axis turns 384 rows into 22 plus two small matrices. That is the recommendation; the choice is the user's. **OQ-DES-D1 decided 2026-08-22: factor the table by axis, and add a second product.** The family table becomes one short table per size axis — the corner's 384 rows collapse to 22, plus an 8 × 6 matrix for `corner.length` and an 8 × 8 for `panel.offset`, which the separability measurement showed are the whole coupled surface of the sweep. Alternative 3 was rejected rather than deferred. **The placer now carries both extent models**: `Dimension` takes an optional rendered text, `None` meaning a family sheet where the uniform callout bound applies, a string meaning a single-variant sheet where the width is measured exactly from the pinned font via the new `drawing_standard.text_width_mm`. That is not a refinement — `112.50 mm` is 5.3× the width of `W`, so a uniform bound over value strings is the case OQ-ARCH-18's alternative 1 was rejected for. Measured, not assumed: the dense twelve-dimension case places in four lanes as both products, 2.66 mm wide as a family sheet and 5.75–9.22 mm as a single-variant sheet across the `U` range, so widening the text cost no lane. **The partition and the factored table are built and measured, 2026-08-22 -- and three of the numbers OQ-DES-D1 was decided on were estimates that turned out wrong, all three in the decision's favor.** [`drawing_families.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/drawing_families.py) enumerates every variant the five sweeps would actually generate, partitions them by the features a resolved part has, and asks of each dimension which size axes it is a function of. It runs in the project virtualenv and writes JSON, the same boundary hop `export_parameters.py` makes and for the same reason. **13 family sheets, not 18.** The partition is taken from features, never from the names on a type axis, and two distinctions that read as separate parts are not: `end_bolt` and `end_anchor` reach the same OpenSCAD module with the same arguments and differ in exactly one number -- `bolt_hole_radius`, 1.95 mm against 1.50 mm at U = 0.5 -- and `offset_single` and `dual` differ only in where the single boom sits, because the geometry has no mirrored second boom at all. Under section 1 each pair is one part in two sizes, so the type axis becomes a table column. The remaining three sheets are the cowling types, which have no panelled form for `bulkhead_validity_check` to allow. **A finding that falls out of the same code: `BoltParameters.is_anchor` is never assigned.** `derived_parameters` reads `is_anchor` into a local, uses it on one line and leaves the object's field at its `False` default, so anything partitioning on it would have found one family where there are two -- it happens not to matter here only because the anchor is not topology either. **Five coupled fields, not two, and it is the same finding wearing a hat**: demoting a type axis from a sheet to a column is exactly what makes `bolt_hole_radius`, `boom_y_position` and `boom_z_position` follow `U` x type. The original two, `panel_offset` and `unit_length`, are unchanged. **19 rows to a sheet, not "about 25", because the sheet was measured.** The template pin in `drawing_standard.py` now carries the frame and the title block as project data alongside the font -- read out of `ANSIA_Landscape.svg` by element id, and `verify_template` re-derives them and refuses on disagreement, which an ANSI B substitution fails on all four counts. The column a table gets is the frame top down to the title block, 139.0 mm, at the 7.0 mm row pitch the drawing standard already uses between dimension lines. **Every family fits, and what closes the gap is that tables sharing a leading axis are one block rather than several** -- a `U` table and a `U` x panel matrix put the same eight values of `U` down the left, so printed apart that column is printed twice. Merged, a coupled field is a band of columns inside the same table, which is what alternative 2 describes read carefully. **Five families then land at 19 of 19 with nothing spare**, which is a fit in the arithmetic and not on paper: one more dimension or one more panel stock puts them on a second sheet. **Section 3's completeness test does not bite as written, and that is the one finding that costs work.** It says the test rides on `design_constants.json`, because each tolerance entry's `why` carries the governing expression. Most do not: `panel_tolerance`'s `why` yields `corner_radius` and itself, where section 2's register row for the same joint consumes `panel_thickness`, `panel_overlap` and `panel_offset` as well, so read from the constants file alone the test passes on a drawing missing most of the panel joint. The two are now read together and cross-checked -- the constants file is the authority on which joints exist, since `load_constants` validates its membership on every sweep run, and the register on what each consumes -- and a tolerance in the group with no register row fails. That also surfaces `bolt_radius`: the register's name for what every mapping calls `bolt_hole_radius`, so joint 10's bore was being dropped from the completeness test by a name that does not match. **Structural zeros are separated rather than dropped**, per section 2: seven interface dimensions are zero across a whole family and are omitted from its sheet rather than printed as `0.00`, and the report names them, because `corner_tolerance` is among them and OQ-DES-C5's residual is that its zero may be a bonded joint rather than an absent one. **`DECIMAL_PLACES` is pinned too**, and it is not cosmetic: the separability question is asked at the precision the sheet prints, so the same constant decides the shape of the table and the text of an annotation. Measured at two places, no field varies only below it. Output is byte-identical across runs. **Remaining here:** `drawing.py`, binding the solver to a real `DrawViewPart` through the cosmetic-vertex mechanism above. **Blocked on [OQ-DES-D2](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) for what `drawing.py` draws, not for how it draws it.** Section 3 obliges the sheet to carry every dimension a joint expression consumes, and the parts were measured against that on 2026-08-22: each planar face reduced to its axis-aligned position, each cylindrical and conical face to its radius, every interface parameter tested against every face separation, face-to-datum distance and radius. **6 of 22 exist as a distance on their own part, and one of the six is a value-matching coincidence** -- `cowl_n_perimeters` is a count of extrusions that happened to equal a 1.0 mm radius. The corner is 1 of 10, and the near misses are exact: `panel_thickness` 4.7625 against a 4.8625 mm face separation, `panel_offset` 2.5 against 2.4, `longeron_radius` 2.0 against a 2.05 mm bore. Every one is off by precisely the clearance, because section 2's own principle puts the whole fit clearance inside the mating face. **The first reading of that measurement was wrong and is worth recording as such.** It was taken to mean the drawing should carry the measurable faces and give up the parameters -- which is right for an inspector and exactly backwards for the reader this drawing set has: the drawing user is **integrating with the structure**, and what they came for is the longeron size and its tolerance, the panel size and its tolerance, the fastener sizes. Those are the nominals. A scheme that hides them behind the as-built feature answers nobody. The question is therefore not which of the two readings to carry but how one annotation carries both, and the reader is now written down in the design document as section 0 rather than left implicit. **The alternatives are drawn rather than argued**, by [`draw_dimension_alternatives.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_dimension_alternatives.py), which annotates the corner mid-bay section four ways -- traced from the built solid by `dimension_alternatives/measure_sections.py`, not computed -- and scores each against what the reader asked for. Feature dimensions alone state none of the three hardware sizes and not by arithmetic either, since the clearance is the missing term and never appears; nominals to reference geometry state all three and leave every dimension disagreeing with the part. **A finding the drawings surfaced that no scheme fixes: the bolt hole is Ø4.00 for a nominal 4 mm bolt, a line-to-line fit**, because joint 10 is the one interface in the register with no clearance parameter -- what the hardware-naming schemes do is make that visible instead of printing a plausible number. **And two of the five questions are not dimensions at all**, so no scheme answers them and they are [OQ-DES-D3](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) rather than part of this one: the cell aperture is a hole nothing mates with, measured at 87.88 mm across and 6874.95 mm2, and volume is a property of the solid -- 14 146.89 mm3 for the corner, 6 922.28 for the frame bulkhead, 7 197.47 for the boom bulkhead. Mass needs a material density, and the project records none anywhere. The solver, the partition, the factoring and the sheet pin are all unaffected by either question; what is blocked is the one thing that needs to know which two points a dimension runs between, and what the annotation on it says. **OQ-DES-D2 decided 2026-08-22: the dimension gives the feature as built and a callout beside it names the hardware.** So the corner's bore is annotated `Ø4.10 BORE / FOR Ø4.00 LONGERON / 0.10 DIA CLEARANCE` -- one annotation carrying both readings, and the two numbers cannot drift apart because both are generated from the same expression. Moving the hardware into a schedule was the runner-up and lost on sheet space: five of the thirteen family sheets already measure 19 of 19 available rows. **The risk this takes on is measurable and has not been measured** -- section 5.2's hard constraints are about the rectangle an annotation occupies, and a three-line callout is several times a bare dimension's extent, so whether a whole part's callouts place without collision is the first thing `drawing.py` has to establish. `text_width_mm` already measures arbitrary strings from the pinned font, so nothing new is needed to find out. If they do not place, the schedule becomes the answer and the sheet size becomes the question. **Unblocked; what remains is `drawing.py` itself**: bind the solver to a real `DrawViewPart` through the cosmetic-vertex mechanism above, annotate per OQ-DES-D2, and check the extents. **OQ-DES-D2's residual risk is measured and it is discharged, 2026-08-22.** The decision recorded that a three-line callout is several times a bare dimension's extent and that whether a whole part's worth of them places had not been tried. It has now: [`dimension_placement.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/dimension_placement.py) gained a `Note` -- an anchor point and one or more lines of text -- and the corner's four notes plus six dimensions **place clean in two lanes**, on a family sheet and on a single-variant sheet. The narrowest view region they place in at all is **84.5 mm** for the family product and **100.0 mm** for the single-variant one, found by search because the placer is not invertible. **Notes take the innermost band and the dimension lanes start outside it**, which is a structural argument of the same kind as nesting: a leader that stays between the geometry and the first lane cannot cross a dimension line. The cost is that each side's dimensions stand off by that side's note band, and H1 is what says when the sheet cannot afford it. **Three findings came out of building it, and two are defects the measurement would have hidden.** The note's *side* cannot be fixed by its anchor: with each of the four corner notes on the side it faces the sheet is refused by H1, and with the same four moved above and below it places -- so the side is searched, preference first, and a solver that fixed it would have called that drawing impossible. **Dimension text never left the midpoint of its own line**, which is invisible while a callout is 2.7 mm of letter and two lanes are 7.0 mm apart, and collides on a single-variant sheet where the text is `20.00`; staggering it along the line is section 5.3 item 4 being *used* rather than assumed away, and it is what every drafted stack of dimensions does. And **the determinism test earned its keep**: because the side comes out of a search whose ties break on position, reversing the input moved a note to a different **side of the sheet** -- a much larger movement than the lane shuffle section 5.5 was written against. Fixed by sorting the notes into a canonical order before anything reads them. **Blocked on [OQ-DES-D5](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), which is a finding rather than a question that was waiting to be asked: the factored family table fits the sheet down the page and does not fit across it.** `table_rows_available` was the only budget the factoring was ever checked against, and every family passed it, five exactly. A table also has a width. Carrying every field the part is driven by -- which is what section 4's *"the variant table carries the value"* asks for -- **12 of 13 families exceed the width beside the view and 3 of 13 exceed the whole 239.5 mm frame**, the worst at 302.5 mm; carrying section 3's interface floor, all 13 fit at 112.4 mm. **Width and rows fail for one reason and it is the reason the rows work**: a coupled field is a *band of columns*, so `panel_offset` on U x panel is eight columns, which is exactly what spends the panel axis across the page instead of down it. The row count was derived knowing that; the column count never was. **A first attempt at deriving the width budget was wrong and is worth recording as such**: pinning the table to the title block's 146.7 mm looks derived and is a coincidence of one paper size -- the ASME title block is *the same* 146.7 mm on ANSI B, where the frame is 391.8, so the rule would give a B sheet no more table than an A sheet. The table and the view spend one budget, so the honest form is the measured view width and what is left: **151.5 mm**. **Turning the table round was measured rather than argued**, since it is the obvious response: it fixes the width outright at 123.6 mm and puts 6 of 13 families past 19 rows, the worst at 30. Neither orientation holds every field on ANSI A; both hold section 3's floor. **ANSI B holds everything by 1.3 mm**, which is the same *"fits in the arithmetic and not on paper"* condition section 5.1a already flags. The alternatives are drawn on the frames they have to fit by [`draw_sheet_alternatives.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_sheet_alternatives.py), with the widest family's reach marked on every sheet so that drawing one family does not flatter the alternative that family is comfortable on. **What is not blocked:** the placer, the note model, the partition and the factoring are all independent of the answer. What waits on it is the table `drawing.py` prints and how much of the frame the view is left with. **OQ-DES-D5 was rejected as filed on 2026-08-22 and the rejection was right.** The five alternatives varied *what the table carries* and *what the paper is* while holding the layout fixed, and the layout had never been compacted at all: the objection was that no layout dedicating that much of a drawing to table values is acceptable, with three questions attached -- how many values, are units repeated, is the packing non-rectangular. **All three had measurable answers and all three were unflattering.** The largest table in the set prints **68 numbers**; nine of the thirteen families print 32 or fewer; the nose and tail print none. **No unit is repeated and none is printed at all** -- a cell is a bare number, and the only unit anywhere on the sheet is inside a panel stock name used as a band heading. **There was no packing**: blocks were stacked in one column, so a 10-row block under a 9-row block cost 19 rows. **Compaction alone takes the worst family from 33.3 % of the frame to 11.1 %, printing the same values** -- per-column decimals (`DECIMAL_PLACES` is a bound on precision and was being read as an instruction to print that many places regardless), short band headings, a 1.0 mm gutter instead of one text height (H2's rule is about annotations floating on a view, and a ruled table has a rule between its columns), a 1.5 h row pitch instead of 2 h, and blocks abreast. Twelve of the thirteen families land at 3.4 % or less. **The second and larger correction is that the view's share is a packing result and not an area subtraction.** A view is a projection with a bounding box, so what it gets is the largest *rectangle* left once the title block and the table are placed, and only two placements leave one -- a band across the bottom or a column up the side. Measured that way the compacted layout gives the view **38.4 %**, not the 90 % the area arithmetic implied, because the compacted table is **too wide to sit beside the stock title block** and has to stack above it. **The stock ANSI A title block is the binding constraint and it binds twice**: its 48.1 mm depth caps the view at **74.3 % with no table on the sheet at all**, so the 75 % requirement is unreachable on this template whatever the table does, and its 146.7 mm width is what forces the column placement. It is 7 051 mm2 -- **63 % of everything that is not the view**, larger than every family's table put together, and the one rectangle on the sheet nobody here chose. With a 120 x 40 block at a 1.3 h row pitch the table sits beside it in a 45.5 mm band and the view reaches **75.7 %**; a 100 x 32 block gives the same number, because past the point where the block is shallower than the table the band's depth *is* the table. The recommendation is therefore our own title block, which also fixes something worse than a layout problem -- **the sheet states its units nowhere**, and the stock template has no field for them. **OQ-DES-D5 decided 2026-08-22: compact the table, and draw our own title block.** The compaction is landed in [`drawing_standard.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/drawing_standard.py) as project data alongside the font and the template pins -- `TABLE_ROW_PITCH_HEIGHTS` **1.3**, `TABLE_COLUMN_GUTTER_MM` **1.0** (a millimeter, not a multiple of the text height, which is the correction: H2 governs annotations floating on a view with nothing between them, and a ruled table has a rule), `format_column` for per-column decimals, and `VIEW_SHARE` **0.75** as the requirement the rest serve. **`best_view` and `title_block_envelope` are the sheet's packing**, and they live there rather than in a checker because the view's share is a property of the sheet: a view is a projection with a bounding box, so what it gets is the largest *rectangle* left once the block and the table are placed, and only a band across the bottom or a column up one side leave one. **The decision's real output is an envelope rather than a size** -- for the view to reach 75 % the title block fits inside **143.4 x 46.8 mm**, and any block inside it gives the same share, because past the point where the block is shallower than the table the band's depth *is* the table. That is why 120 x 40 and 100 x 32 both measure 75.7 %. The stock ASME block misses the envelope by **3.2 mm of width and 1.3 mm of depth**, which is how close the sheet came to working untouched. **The requirement is now a checked assertion rather than a figure in a document**: `check_dimension_placement.py` builds the decided sheet and fails if the view is under `VIEW_SHARE`. **A borrowing the decision exposed:** `NOTE_LINE_PITCH_HEIGHTS` was `TABLE_ROW_PITCH_HEIGHTS` on the reasoning that a note is a short column of text lines -- so moving the table's pitch from 2.0 to 1.3 would have silently tightened every leader note's line spacing. It is now **1.4** in its own right, which is ISO 3098's minimum line spacing for type B lettering, the same standard the 3.5 mm text height comes from. **Unblocked. What remains is `drawing.py`**, plus IP-FC-86 for the template it draws on. **`drawing.py` exists and a real sheet builds, 2026-08-22.** [`drawing.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/drawing.py) builds the part, puts it on a page with the project's template, projects a view, places the annotations and exports DXF; [`check_drawing.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/check_drawing.py) does it on the 1U 3/16 in corner and asks the questions only a finished page can be asked. **The binding rule holds on a real part**: three dimensions bound through `makeCosmeticVertex3d` to points computed from the parameters report **4.8625, 10.0000 and 10.0000 mm** against parameter values of exactly those -- so nothing is bound to a projected edge name, and the 2:1 view scale does not leak into the value, which is the failure [`spike_techdraw.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/spike_techdraw.py) went looking for. The vertex count is read immediately before the vertices are made, never cached. **OQ-DES-D2's callouts are on the page as `DrawViewAnnotation` plus `DrawLeaderLine`**, seven lines across three notes, and the exported DXF carries the callout text -- so the hardware a feature is cut for survives into the deliverable rather than living only in the model. **The scale is searched rather than chosen**: the largest of ISO 5455's preferred scales whose annotations all place is the one used, which serves OQ-DES-D5's requirement from the other end -- the view is as large as the sheet can carry rather than as large as a constant says. The corner comes out at **2:1**. A part that places at no preferred scale is a refusal naming what failed at each, which is section 5.6's contract. **The view region is 239.5 x 140.4 mm, exactly 75.0 % of the frame**, and the independent checker re-derives the hard constraints from the finished page and passes. Two builds of the same variant export **byte-identical** DXF, so section 5.5's determinism survives the whole pipeline and not just the solver. **A floating-point lesson worth keeping:** the view share compared as `< VIEW_SHARE` failed at 0.7499999999999999, because the band's depth is *defined* as one minus the share -- the check was comparing a number with itself through two operations. **What remains:** the value table on the sheet, the annotation sets for the bulkhead and boom bulkhead (the corner's is written; the other two kinds have a view direction and no dimension set yet), and the single-variant product, whose views carry values rather than letters and which section 5.1 says places per sheet rather than per family. **The annotation set and the value table are now one thing, and section 3's completeness test runs.** [`sheet_annotations.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/sheet_annotations.py) is split out of `drawing.py` because both sides of the boundary need it -- `drawing.py` imports FreeCAD and the table is computed in the virtualenv by `drawing_families.py` -- and it declares a **`Quantity`**: a number the sheet states, the interface parameters it *carries*, and whether it is constant across the family. That record is what makes OQ-DES-D5's recommendation true rather than aspirational: the letter is issued once, to the annotation and to the table column, so a column the reader cannot address cannot exist. **Section 3's test is executable from it**: the union of what a sheet's quantities carry, against the interface set, names exactly which parameters the drawing does not state -- reading the *pair*, dimension and callout, which is what OQ-DES-D2's resolution made possible. Run on the corner it named five, and all five are now stated: joint 1's lead-in (`extrusion_width`), joint 2's socket (`greeble_tolerance`, from `greeble_radius_of` -- bore plus greeble wall plus clearance, with the post the same without it), and joint 4's extension (`panel_overlap`, `panel_offset`). **`corner_tolerance` is excluded rather than demanded**, because section 2 says a parameter that is zero across a whole family is the absence of the joint. **The extension was measured, not assumed**: the corner has a planar face normal to X at **-7.2625 mm** at U = 1 with 3/16 in panel, and `panel_overlap + panel_offset` is 7.2625, so the expression is the distance rather than something a clearance has been folded into. The slot mouth at `-(panel_offset - panel_tolerance)` makes the slot `panel_overlap + panel_tolerance` deep, which is the panel's entry plus its fit. **A family sheet's notes now carry letters and a single-variant sheet's carry numbers**, which is the only thing section 5.1's two products differ by in this code: the corner's bore note reads `OB BORE / FOR OC LONGERON / 0.10 DIA CLEARANCE / 0.60 LEAD-IN` on a family sheet, with the two constants written as themselves because a letter pointing at a column that repeats 0.10 nine times is worse than the number. **And the table that section 3 now obliges does not fit.** The unpanelled corner is 7 columns, **54.1 mm x 9 rows** into a band of 108.5 x 10 -- comfortable. The panelled corner is 17 columns, **138.9 mm, over by 30.3 mm**, and the cause is the one OQ-DES-D5 already identified: `panel_extension` follows `U` x panel, so it prints as a **band of eight columns**, 8 of the 14 in the `U` block. No framing of joint 4 avoids it -- dimensioning `panel_overlap` and `panel_offset` separately was the 112.4 mm measurement that started OQ-DES-D5. **This is the condition OQ-DES-D5's recorded reserve was written for** -- *make panel stock a sheet axis for the corner, which is the only family with a wide band; it costs sheets, which are cheap, rather than legibility, which is not* -- and taking it turns the corner's panelled family into 8 sheets, so the set goes from 13 family sheets to 20. That is a change to the shape of the deliverable and is put to the reader rather than taken. `check_table_width.py` fails on it, so the gap cannot be forgotten. **The blocker above dissolved on 2026-08-27, and it dissolved because two type choices were wrong rather than because anything about the drawing changed.** Asked whether the font sizes and text margins were reasonable, they were not. **The value table's row pitch was 1.3 text heights, under ISO 3098's 1.4 h minimum for type B lettering** -- set to make the band arithmetic work, while the same 1.4 was cited in `dimension_placement.py` as the standard's floor for note line spacing. Two files, one standard, one obeying it. And the squeeze that forced it came from a choice nobody had made: **the table was setting at the view's 3.5 mm**, because there was one text-height constant and the table inherited it. A value table sets one size smaller than the annotations around it, which is what every title block and parts list does. `TABLE_TEXT_HEIGHT_MM` is now **2.5 mm**, the next size down in ISO 3098's preferred series, and `TABLE_ROW_PITCH_HEIGHTS` is **1.4**. **The corrected sheet is smaller on both axes than the one that broke it**: the panelled corner's table goes from 138.9 x 45.5 mm to **104.0 x 35.0**, into a band of 108.5 x 46.8 -- so it fits with 4.5 mm and 3 rows to spare, the row budget goes from 10 to **13**, and **OQ-DES-D5's reserve does not need to be called**. The drawing set stays at 13 family sheets. **A second defect the same question turned up, in the title block**: an 8.5 mm row held a 2.5 mm caption over a 3.5 mm value at a 1.6 mm pad, which puts the value's cap height **0.7 mm above the caption's baseline** -- they overlapped, on every field of every sheet. Nothing would have reported it, because a title block is drawn by the template rather than placed by the solver, so no placement check ever sees inside a cell. Rows are now 10.0 + 9.0 x 4 and `make_sheet_template.check_rows` refuses a row that cannot hold a caption above a value at the standard's line spacing -- verified by feeding it the old rows, which it names one by one. **A debt this leaves written down rather than assumed**: the 1.0 mm column gutter is 0.4 of a text height and is only defensible because a rule is drawn between the columns. The table renderer does not exist yet, and a table drawn without rules at that gutter is the failure H2 describes. **The renderer landed on 2026-08-27 and the debt above is paid.** [`sheet_table.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/sheet_table.py) lays a family's blocks out as positioned cells and drawn rules -- in the table's own frame, origin top left and y downward, so the page's y-flip happens once in `drawing.py` rather than inside the layout arithmetic. It sits beside `sheet_annotations.py` on the FreeCAD-free side of the boundary for the same reason: the width has to be measurable from the checker, and the checker now measures it **by laying the table out** rather than by re-deriving the sum. Two sums of one intent are two tables, and the one that passes the check is not necessarily the one on the sheet -- which had already happened in miniature, because the renderer uppercases before it measures and the check was measuring `1/16in` where the sheet prints `1/16IN`. **The rules are real geometry, and that is forced rather than chosen.** Measured 2026-08-27: headless FreeCAD 1.1 offers `writeDXFPage` and `writeDXFView` and no SVG or PDF writer at all, so the DXF is not one export among several but the only one a checker can read -- and `writeDXFPage` **drops a `DrawViewSymbol` entirely**, so the obvious SVG table would render on the page and vanish from the deliverable. A projected compound of line segments exports as DXF LINE entities, so that is what a rule is. **`check_drawing` reads the rules back out of the exported file** and compares their coordinates with the layout, because a `DrawViewPart` centers its projection on its own shape and a table 20 mm from where the layout put it renders perfectly well on top of the title block. **A defect the same probe exposed, in work already called done**: `writeDXFPage` writes exactly **one TEXT entity per annotation** however many lines it holds, so every corner sheet was exporting the first line of each OQ-DES-D2 note and silently dropping the rest -- three of the bore note's four lines, two of three on the socket and the pocket. `check_drawing` passed it because it looked for `BORE`, which is on the first line. Notes are now one annotation per line, positioned from the placer's own box rather than by TechDraw's line stacking, and the check demands **every line of every note** in the export: 11 of 11, where it had been 4 of 11. **`sheet_table.check_layout` is section 5.2's H1 and H2 asked of a table** -- text inside the box, no two cells touching, and a rule in every gutter narrower than H2's clearance, which is the standing condition on OQ-DES-D5's 1.0 mm gutter rather than a note about it. It is exercised against three tables broken on purpose, because the swept corpus reaches none of these cases: a laid-out table is correct by construction, so a check that had never refused anything would be a check whose thresholds were untested. Measured: the panelled corner's table is **105.8 x 35.0 mm, 126 cells and 26 rules**, into a 108.5 x 46.8 band, and the unpanelled one is 40.3 x 31.5. A family also has a **key** now (`corner-corner-4d98b4`), because a sheet is drawn per family and the parameter file only says which variant it is -- the corner's two families differ by a topology question the FreeCAD side cannot reach. **Drawing the *other* corner family found a third defect, and it was total.** The annotation set emitted `panel_pocket` unconditionally, so on the corner with no panel the sheet asked for a dimension of 0.00 -- and section 5.2's H5 refused it at every one of the ten preferred scales, correctly, because a dimensioned zero asserts an inspectable coincident fit and there is nothing there to inspect. **The unpanelled family's sheet could not be drawn at all**, and nothing had noticed because the check built the panelled variant. Section 2 already had the rule -- a parameter that is zero across a whole family is the absence of the joint -- and `drawing_families` was applying it to the *table* while `sheet_annotations` was not applying it to the *view*, which is the same disagreement between the two halves of a sheet that OQ-DES-D5 was decided to prevent. The panel quantities, the pocket dimension and the pocket note now depend on the panel being there; `panel_extension` stays, because `panel_overlap` is nonzero whether or not a panel is fitted. Both families now build: the unpanelled sheet is 2 dimensions, 3 notes and a 40.3 x 31.5 mm table, the panelled one 3, 4 and 105.8 x 35.0. `check_drawing` also picks the family from the **variant's** panel thickness rather than taking whichever family is panelled, and reports a refusal as a failure with the refusal's own words rather than as a traceback. **The bulkhead's annotation set landed on 2026-08-27, taking the drawn set from 2 families to 7 of 13.** Every value in it was measured on the built solid before it was written: at 1U with 3/16 in panel and the `end_bolt` type the part has planar faces at **+/-45.1375**, cylinders of **R2.05** at (+/-40, +/-40), **R3.25** and **R4.45** about the same axes and **R2.00** at (+/-32, +/-32) -- the panel seating face, the longeron axis, the bore, the greeble post, its nub and the bolt. Reading the parameter list instead would have got one of them wrong: section 2's register describes `bolt_offset` as "on the diagonal", and the part places it **8.00 in each axis**, 11.31 along the diagonal. **The first draft's table did not fit and the fix was drafting rather than compaction.** Dimensioning the outer face at its own coordinate, `unit_width/2 - panel_thickness - panel_tolerance`, makes it a function of both size axes, so it printed as a **band of eight columns** of three-digit numbers and the panelled end family's table came to **167.0 mm** against a 108.5 mm band. Located instead by a datum and a setback -- the mold line on `U` alone, the pocket on `panel` alone -- it is two ordinary columns and the table is 121.8 mm. That is also how the corner already states the same joint, with `corner_radius` and `panel_pocket`, and two parts meeting at one joint should not describe it two different ways. **The remaining overflow was the headings, not the data**: a band whose second axis is the bulkhead type heads its columns `cowling_anchor` and `cowling_bolt`, fourteen characters over four-character numbers, so the heading set the column width. Dropping the prefix the whole band shares -- the same argument OQ-DES-D5 made for stating a panel stock's unit once above its band -- took the cowling family from **122.9 mm to 81.1**. All seven families now fit, worst at 105.8. **A drawing that would have been plausibly wrong is now refused.** `bulkhead_full` implements the plain end type and takes no type flag, so handing it an interconnect variant does not fail -- it returns an end bulkhead. Measured: at 1U with the 0 mm panel the interconnect and `end_bolt` variants have **identical parameter values**, differing only in the `is_interconnect` boolean, which is not a parameter and never reaches this backend; and that boolean removes the **bolt flange, its fillet, its web and the bolt hole**. The sheet would have shown four bolt bosses and four bolt holes the part does not have, dimensioned, under the interconnect's title and beside a table of the interconnect's own values -- every number correct and the part wrong. `part_kinds.BUILT_TYPES` records what each backend builds, `drawing.py` refuses a family outside it, `check_drawing` treats that refusal as the pass rather than as a failure, and `drawing_families` names the three families the port cannot draw. **`check_drawing` now takes a kind** and was corner-only before, which is how the corner's unpanelled family went unbuilt long enough to become undrawable; twelve variant-and-kind combinations run clean, including 0 mm panel and 4U at both ends of the scale search. **What is open**: section 3's completeness test reports `cowl_n_perimeters` and `extrusion_width` unstated on all five bulkhead families. That is [OQ-DES-D6](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), and it blocks nothing -- the sheets draw. **It was filed on 2026-08-27 saying neither name reaches any feature, which was measured on 2026-08-28 and is only half true.** `cowl_n_perimeters` is not even a row on the FreeCAD bulkhead's parameter sheet. `extrusion_width` is, inherited from the `corner_tree` merge, and perturbing it moves the part's volume from **6922.281 to 6677.594 mm3**: it reaches the geometry through `longeron_chamfer` into the `max()` that floors `nominal_flat_offset`, which sets how far the **corner seating flat** runs -- register joint 3, not joint 7. The flat spans 0.5250 mm at 1U with 3/16 in panel. So the bulkhead sheet owes a joint 3 annotation on its own merits, and the part genuinely undecided is what a family sheet does with a dimension whose feature is present on 136 of 148 variants and degenerates to 0.0000 mm elsewhere. **OQ-DES-D6 was decided on 2026-08-28 on alternatives 2 and 3, and the drawing set is 13 family sheets no longer -- it is 16.** The rule alternative 3 was missing turned out to be one section 5.1 already had: **a feature absent on some members of a family means the family is mis-partitioned, not that the dimension needs a table convention.** The corner seating flat's span is `max(a, b) - b` of the two branches of `nominal_flat_offset`, so it is **exactly zero when the panel branch wins** and the face is gone rather than narrow -- the corner drops from 54 faces to 52 and the bulkhead from 187 to 179, losing four flats of 3.15 mm2 each. Three families held both kinds of part (24 of the panelled corner's 216, 8 of the panelled end bulkhead's 72, 4 of the panelled interconnect's 36), so their sheets showed an outline a twelfth of their own variants do not have. A dash in the table would not have repaired that, because **the view is wrong too**. The condition is now in the topology signature, computed by `sheet_annotations.corner_seat_span`, which is the same function the bulkhead's annotation dimensions the flat from -- one implementation, because a sheet that dimensions a face its own family signature calls absent is the failure both exist to prevent. **Two implementation errors, both caught by measuring.** Gating the condition on the resolved parameter object rather than on the part's own mapping split the boom bulkhead's four families into eight for nothing -- every kind's flat parameters carry `printer.extrusion_width`, including kinds whose geometry never sees it, and no boom module mentions `longeron_chamfer`. And dimensioning the flat's **span** cost eight table columns, taking the panelled end bulkhead to **158.6 mm** against a 108.5 mm band; the same face located from the longeron axis instead of measured across is `max(a, b)`, which follows `U` alone wherever the flat exists. One column, table **105.8 mm**, all ten families with an annotation set fit. Verified on built solids: the diagonal seating face sits at that offset over root two from the axis -- 1.8738 mm at 1U with 3/16 in panel, 2.0153 at 0.5U with 1 mm panel where the panel branch governs instead. `check_drawing.family_of` reads the seating flag off the family's topology signature, so all three corner families are reachable and each variant lands in its own. **Two open questions came out of the same measurement and are filed rather than left in a summary.** [OQ-DES-D7](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions): section 2's *Governing expression* column means the whole nominal in rows 1, 4, 6 and 7 and only the clearance or its adjustment in rows 2, 3 and 5, and section 3's completeness test reads that column -- so it demands `greeble_thickness` of nobody, demands neither `panel_thickness` nor `unit_width` of either bulkhead, and **demands joint 3 of nobody at all**, since row 3's only surviving identifier is `corner_tolerance`, which is a structural zero. Joint 3 got stated on the bulkhead because the part needs it said; nothing would have reported its absence -- and writing row 3's full expression out would change no demand either, because rows 1 and 4 happen to name every parameter it would name. **Joint 3's coverage is a coincidence of two other rows.** **The register reaches no drawing**, which bounds all of this: mutating the parsed register -- emptying every row, or giving every row every parameter name -- leaves every family's table columns and callout letters byte-identical, because `sheet_annotations` declares the annotation sets by hand and `factor`'s interface argument marks fields rather than filtering them. So no error in that column can produce a wrong drawing; it can only make the completeness test agree with a drawing set for the wrong reasons. [OQ-DES-D8](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions): row 4 says the corner's panel extension is `panel_overlap + panel_offset - panel_tolerance` and the built end face is at `panel_overlap + panel_offset` -- 7.2625 against 7.1625 at 1U with 3/16 in panel, 14.3500 against 14.2500 at 4U with 1/4 in, exactly one clearance out on both. The part is self-consistent: the slot mouth is at `panel_offset - panel_tolerance` and the slot is `panel_overlap + panel_tolerance` deep only if the end face reaches the slot bottom, so the register's shorter extension would open the slot through the end of the part. **And a third, which is upstream of both**: [OQ-DES-D9](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) asks whether section 2 is the interface design or an analysis of the implementation. Section 1 says the register is *a reading of a file the sweep already validates* -- `design_constants.json` -- and measured on 2026-08-28 that file carries a governing expression for **three of the seven joints** and prose for the other four. Rows 6 and 7 quote theirs verbatim; not one term of rows 1 to 5's expressions appears in the corresponding `why`. So rows 2, 3 and 5 are *faithful* readings of a source that has no expression, and **rows 1 and 4 carry expressions that are in no design document at all** -- they were written by reading the parts. For those two the asserted chain (constants file -> register -> test -> drawings) is really geometry -> register -> test -> drawings generated from annotation sets written by measuring the same geometry, so the test compares two readings of one source. That is precisely why it cannot see D8. Which side of D8 is wrong is D9's answer, not D8's. Neither blocks this row: no drawing changes under any answer, because the register reaches no drawing, every name D7 would add is already stated, and both forms of D8's expression name the same parameters | IP-FC-14, IP-FC-36, IP-FC-86 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-7](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D1](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D2](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D3](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D5](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D6](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D7](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D8](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D9](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-84 | todo | **UC-7a companion — single-variant drawing sets, decided in OQ-DES-D1 on 2026-08-22.** A set is keyed on `U` and panel thickness and holds a drawing for every part needed to build a fuselage at that size and panel: 1 corner, 5 bulkheads, 3 boom bulkheads, nose and tail — **11 drawings**. 8 `U` × 9 panel = **72 sets, 792 sheets**. Each drawing is its own file and the files of a set are collocated, so a set can be delivered as a unit. **`FX` is tabled rather than given six corner sheets.** The corner is drawn once at `FX = 1.0` and the `FX`-varying dimensions go in a six-row table — which costs almost nothing here, because with `U` and panel fixed **exactly one dimension moves with `FX`**: `corner.length`, at `100·U·FX` (`unit_length` is 100 mm at 1U, the one standard value `FX` scales). The only other field that changes is `corner.FX` itself, which is the axis parameter and not a dimension; measured at `U` = 0.5, 1.0 and 4.0 with the same result at each. **The bulkhead and boom-bulkhead type axes do not collapse the same way, and the reason given here on 2026-08-22 was wrong.** It was that those axes are topology; measured the same day by [`drawing_families.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/drawing_families.py), two of them are not -- `end_bolt` and `end_anchor` differ in one number, and `offset_single` and `dual` in where the single boom sits. What holds 11 up is that a set is a **build kit** and those are parts a builder installs in different places, so each needs its own sheet whether or not their family sheets merge. Sharing a family sheet and needing separate single-variant sheets are compatible: the family sheet carries a callout and the single-variant sheet carries the number. 11 is the floor rather than a first cut, for that reason and not the one first written. **These are not family drawings and differ in the property that mattered most:** the view carries values, so placement is solved per sheet — 792 solves, not 13 — and the annotation extent is measured per string rather than bounded uniformly. A corner sheet **mixes both models**, ten measured values and one tabled callout, and places clean in four lanes with the tabled overall length falling to the outermost lane on its own, since it has the widest span. The solver already carries both (IP-FC-21). **One requirement this creates that the dimension scheme does not own:** a view drawn at `FX = 1.0` with the length tabled is not to scale for five of six rows — 100 mm as drawn against 300 mm at `FX = 3.0`, `U = 1.0` — and a not-to-scale dimension has to be marked. Which convention marks it falls in the standards-compliance area §6 lists as undecided. What this row owns is the set assembly: enumerating the 72 sets, naming files so a set reads as one, and the collocation | IP-FC-21 | [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D1](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [freecad_migration.md §UC-7](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-85 | todo | **The panel OML becomes a part, decided in OQ-DES-D4 on 2026-08-22.** Panels are to be given their own OML allocation and panel designs made to fit within it, so designs can be interchanged. The envelope is the *space a panel may occupy*, not a manufactured object, and making it a part is what lets a candidate design be checked against it **by containment** rather than by someone comparing it against transcribed numbers -- which is what interchange actually needs. It also keeps the panel itself out of the printed-part pipeline: a panel is cut sheet, and the sweep's assumptions are about prints. **Every boundary is already determined and verified against built faces**, and each is an expression over parameters rather than a measurement: width `unit_width - 2*(corner_radius + panel_offset)` = 75.000 at 1U, exposed span `width - 2*panel_overlap` = 65.475, `panel_overlap` = 4.7625 into each corner, thickness `panel_thickness` = 4.7625 in a `panel_thickness + panel_tolerance` = 4.8625 pocket, inner face at 45.1375 from the airframe axis, outer face on the mold line, and length `unit_length` -- the **full bay**. **The width is confirmed by a clearance rather than a face, which is the stronger check**: at 75.000 the panel edge lands at corner-local -2.5000 against a slot bottom at -2.4000, a gap of exactly `panel_tolerance`, so no other width fits the slot the corner was cut with. **The length is confirmed twice**: the bulkhead's outer profile is inset from the mold line by exactly the panel pocket, and one of its outer flat faces measures 392.850 mm2 = `65.475 x 6`, the exposed span times the bulkhead thickness -- the bulkhead's outer flat face **is** the panel's seating surface, cut back by precisely the panel it seats. A panel is therefore not interrupted at a station; the bulkhead gives way to it. **One envelope serves all four faces of a bay**: two reasons were asserted while OQ-DES-D4 was open for why they might differ and both were wrong -- a cowling station closes an *end* of the airframe and never touches a side face, and the boom runs *parallel* to the fuselage axis (`boom_key_shape` is inside the `linear_extrude`) reaching only 32.20 mm off-axis against a panel inner face at 45.1375, clearing it by 12.94 mm. What differs between faces is the panel *design* fitted into the envelope, which is the interchange the allocation exists for | OQ-DES-D4 | [dimension_scheme.md §2](../design/dimension_scheme.md), [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D4](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-86 | done | **The sheet gets a title block of this project's own, decided in OQ-DES-D5 on 2026-08-22.** The stock ASME block is 146.66 x 48.074 mm = 7 051 mm2, which is **63 % of everything on an ANSI A frame that is not the drawn view** -- larger than every family's value table put together -- and it is the one rectangle on the sheet nobody here chose. It binds twice: its **depth** caps the view at **74.3 % of the frame with no table on the sheet at all**, so OQ-DES-D5's 75 % requirement is unreachable on the stock template whatever the table does; and its **width** leaves 91.9 mm beside it where the compacted table is 95.1, which forces the column placement and drops the view to 38.4 %. **The specification is an envelope, not a size: the block fits inside 143.4 x 46.8 mm**, and any block inside it gives the same 75.7 % because past the point where the block is shallower than the table the band's depth is the table. So the size is free within the envelope and is settled by what the block has to hold. **It also fixes a defect that is not a layout problem: the sheet states its units nowhere.** Cells are bare numbers, annotations are bare numbers, and the stock template has no field for a unit -- a drawing whose numbers are millimeters and does not say so is wrong in a way no compaction reaches. `verify_template` already re-derives the frame and title block by element id and refuses on disagreement, so the new template is pinned the same way the stock one is and a substitution fails loudly. What the block carries beyond the units -- title, drawing number, revision, sheet, scale, default tolerances, approvals -- is conventional and is not a question this plan holds open, but it is what decides the size inside the envelope. **Built 2026-08-22.** The template is generated by [`make_sheet_template.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/make_sheet_template.py) rather than drawn by hand, because its size is a derivation: the generator refuses to write a block outside `title_block_envelope`, so the number in the file cannot drift from the reason for the number. **130 x 46 mm rather than the envelope's 143.4 x 46.8**, deliberately -- the envelope is a limit, and spending all of it would leave the value table 3.4 mm of margin, where at 130 the table has 108.5 mm against the 95.1 the widest family needs. That costs a third of a percent of drawing (75.4 % against 75.7 %) for 13 mm of headroom. **The template is the project's now, not the installation's**, which closes a finding IP-FC-21 recorded and did not act on: it used to resolve through `App.getResourceDir()` into the FreeCAD install, so a generated drawing depended on a file the project does not carry and two machines with different FreeCAD versions would emit different sheets from the same source. **The units statement is fixed text, not an editable field**, and `verify_template` fails the build if the string is missing -- an editable field can be filled in blank, and the whole point is that the statement cannot go missing. `verify_template` also now refuses a template that has lost one of the ten `freecad:editable` fields, which would otherwise print as an empty box, and one whose title block is outside the envelope, which would print a sheet whose drawn view is under the required share and look entirely normal. [`check_sheet_standard.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/check_sheet_standard.py) exercises all five refusals against deliberately broken copies, because a check that has never been seen to fail is not evidence. Verified end to end under `freecadcmd`: TechDraw loads the template, reports the page as 279.4 x 215.9 mm, finds all ten fields, accepts values into them, projects a view and exports DXF. **Two things fell out of building it, both of which the decision made visible rather than caused.** The row budget was **19 and is now 10**: it was the frame's top edge down to the title block, which is the depth of a table in a column *above* the block, and under the band layout the table is beside it, so the budget is the quarter of the frame the view does not get. **Every one of the thirteen families then lands at exactly 10 of 10**, which is the same tight fit section 5.1a already reported at the old budget, and an eleventh row takes the view to 73.3 %. And `drawing_families` was still *summing* its blocks' rows, which is right for blocks stacked in a column and wrong for blocks abreast -- the panelled corner reads 10 rather than 19 once counted the way it is printed. **A checker asserting a requirement against a sheet the build does not use is not asserting it**: `check_dimension_placement` was measuring the view's share against a literal 120 x 40 block written down before the template existed, and reported 75.7 % where the template's own 130 x 46 gives 75.4 %. It reads the pinned block now | OQ-DES-D5 | [dimension_scheme.md §OQ-DES-D5](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [dimension_scheme.md §5.2](../design/dimension_scheme.md) |
| IP-FC-87 | done | **The fundamental design relationships are derived, and the implementation follows from them.** Called for by OQ-DES-D9 on 2026-08-28 and written as [doc/design/design_basis.md](../design/design_basis.md). **Corrected twice on 2026-08-29, both times on the requirements structure rather than on the geometry.** First: originating requirements are separated from the level below them -- L0 comes from outside the design, traces to nothing, and becomes the axiomatic source *once reviewed and accepted*, so it is marked PROPOSED and no tool can check it. Second, and this is the one that mattered: the level below had been called **derived** throughout, and **that word has a specific meaning -- a requirement traceable to NO higher-level requirement**, arising from the design solution itself. Most of the eleven are *decomposed* from a parent, not derived; the document had called all of them derived and then reported as its headline result that every one traced upward, which in the standard sense is a contradiction. **Three are genuinely derived** -- SR-4, SR-5, SR-6, all three decisions about which side of a joint carries its clearance -- and being derived is a review obligation, since nothing above them validates them. `check_derivation.py` now enforces it in both directions: a requirement with no parent that is not flagged derived fails, a flagged one *with* a parent fails, and a derived one without a rationale fails. **11 L1 requirements: 8 decomposed from 9 L0, 3 derived.** A third correction came with them: **SR-2 had said a printed feature is "never below its 1U value", which is false** -- measured at U=0.5 against U=1, eleven derived fields shrink (corner radius 5 against 10, web 1.5 against 3, plate 0.4 against 0.8) and only four floor. The floor is SR-11, an exception with a short list, and its reason is on record for two of the four and not for the rest. **The rest of the work stands unchanged:** `check_derivation.py` derives every numeric parameter of every variant from the chosen inputs -- **560 variants across all five sweeps, every relation deriving the built value** -- and refuses a parameter that is neither a declared choice nor a derived relationship; `freecad/check_derived_geometry.py` predicts where each interface face must sit, how large, and whether it exists at all, then builds and asks, on four variants chosen to reach the topology cases, both kinds, all passing. **What it found:** the corner's panel feature is a rebate `panel_thickness + panel_tolerance` deep and not a slot at twice that -- the register was quoting a cutting primitive, which is an implementation artifact and not a requirement at any level (IP-FC-88); `corner.md` carried a pre-OQ-DES-B13 extension expression, corrected; `panel_offset` satisfies both its requirements on all **528 non-cowling variants** with one quantum less violating one, and the binding requirement is the **assembly motion on 468 of them**; a feature vanishes in two different ways, by merging and by closing up; `longeron_chamfer` chamfers nothing. **Four open questions**, of which OQ-DES-DB4 -- what makes a requirement originating rather than derived -- **blocks acceptance of the L0 list** and is the one to settle first. **Ids superseded by IP-FC-89, recorded here because this row's names no longer resolve:** `SR-n` is now `DES-n`, and the proposed `L0`/`OR-n` set was replaced by the adopted architectural requirements, `AR-` ids in [requirements.md](../architecture/requirements.md). The reconstruction's own L0 list was never accepted, so OQ-DES-DB4 did not in the end block anything; **it and DB5 both closed on 2026-08-29**. | IP-FC-88 | todo | **The panel rebate's cutting primitive is sized from the feature it cuts, so it reads as a design dimension.** In `freecad/corner_tree.py` -- and identically in `scad/fuselage_corner_geometry.scad` -- the rows are `slot_d = panel_thickness * 2 + panel_tolerance * 2`, `slot_w = panel_overlap * 2`, `slot_x = -panel_overlap * 2 - panel_offset + panel_tolerance`. | IP-FC-93 | done | **Three long-standing open questions answered on 2026-08-29, two of them closed and one narrowed.** All three had been filed as *not blocking*, and all three had the same shape: a number or a line whose reason was not on record, where this document had a plausible reconstruction available and refused to adopt it. **Every answer was one the reconstruction would have missed**, which is the point. **OQ-DES-DB1, `panel_offset`'s `min` against `sqrt(2)*corner_radius`** -- alternative 3, keep it and mark it a guard. This document had called that ordering a defect -- *"a clamp the next line can undo"* -- and **that framing was wrong**; clamping after the rounding would return an off-grid offset in exactly the case the guard fires. It still never binds, zero of 528 non-cowling variants, and it is now a recorded guard rather than an unexplained line. They are design choices made on a tradeoff of several factors and objectives, tracing to **no formula and to no higher requirement**, so **airframe size is a discrete series of eight** and a ninth step means choosing again. `4*U` fitting six of the eight rows is a coincidence and must not be adopted: the small sizes are thicker than proportion **because they were chosen to be**. AR-MOD-8 not reaching these two is now a finding rather than a gap. **OQ-DES-DB3, four clearances with no argument for their size** -- answered in substance: `longeron_tolerance` 0.05, `greeble_tolerance` 0.05, `panel_tolerance` 0.1 and `boom_tolerance` 0.2 were **arrived at through fit checks during prototype testing**. That closes the question of whether a formula was hiding behind them -- none was -- and **`boom_tolerance` being four times `longeron_tolerance` is the outcome of two separate fit checks, not a ratio anybody chose**. A fourth source label, **`measured`**, was added for exactly this: a real reason that supports **no extrapolation**, since a new size, stock or printer needs another fit check rather than arithmetic. **DB3's remaining question -- the rule -- was decided on 2026-08-30: ask, one case at a time, and between askings an inference is never promoted.** It can be replaced by a *recorded* or a *measured* reason and by nothing else; there is no third label for a well-argued reconstruction. The evidence is this document's own record over two days: **four cases where a reconstruction was available and in none of them was it right** -- DES-6's two confident readings, both wrong; the four clearances, whose origin was empirical and which no argument would have produced; AP-3, which reframed the question rather than answering it as posed; and DB1, where the reconstruction was not merely incomplete but wrong about the ordering being a defect. Every one of those would have qualified for the third label. **The cost is accepted and stated:** asking does not scale past the person who made the decisions, and if asking ever stops being possible the third label becomes the question worth reopening. **`design_basis.md` now has no open questions**; its section 8 is a queue of things to ask -- `longeron_chamfer`'s reason and name, three of DES-11's seven floors, `panel_overlap`'s one-thickness lap, and **DES-5's allocation**, which carries the most weight and stays marked *inferred* until it is asked rather than until it is argued | IP-FC-87 | [design_basis.md OQ-DES-DB1..DB3](../design/design_basis.md#open-questions), [design_basis.md section 3](../design/design_basis.md#3-what-is-chosen), [design_basis.md section 8](../design/design_basis.md#8-what-could-not-be-derived), [system_requirements.md](../design/system_requirements.md) |

Verified: all four vertices of the source's *bulkhead boundary* polygon satisfy `x + y = flat_offset` to the last digit, the bore branch governs **240 of 264** corner variants against the panel branch's **24**, and where the panel branch governs the flat face is **exactly 0.0000 mm** -- gone, not small. **Three unrelated things carry the name.** The constant, which chamfers nothing; a `// longeron chamfer` comment on a **quadrant masking polygon** at `fuselage_corner_geometry.scad:258`, an implementation artifact; and **a real chamfer** at `fuselage_bulkhead_geometry.scad:125`, the cone flaring the longeron flange boss into the plate, sized by **`flange_chamfer`** at 1.0 mm and not by `longeron_chamfer` at all. **The real one is neither broken nor lost**: `simple_positives.py` builds it as `LongeronChamfer` matching the source term for term. It is **cowling-only** -- `if (is_cowling)` at line 93, established by tracing brace depth to the feature rather than by reading nearby lines, which is how IP-FC-9's reference got it wrong -- and **on one face only**, since `octant_to_full()` mirrors x, y and x-to-y but not z. **Two inferences in `corner.md` were wrong and are corrected in place**, not quietly: it had the boundary clearing *"the longeron bore and its chamfer"*, and separately called the value *"a lead-in for that vertical hole"* at the bore mouth. There is no chamfer at the bore mouth; every appearance of `longeron_chamfer` in the corner source is the local definition or the pass into `corner_middle_shape`. Both readings were plausible -- a vertical printed hole does want a lead-in -- which is the point: **that is the third and fourth reconstruction this project has recorded as confident and wrong**, after DES-6's two, and it is why OQ-DES-DB3 was decided the way it was. **OQ-DES-DB7** asks whether the constant should be renamed to `longeron_standoff`; the recommendation is yes, with an exact test, since the value does not move and every variant and every predicted face must come out unchanged | IP-FC-87, IP-FC-92 | [design_basis.md INT-3](../design/design_basis.md#int-3--corner-seating-faces--bulkhead), [design_basis.md section 8](../design/design_basis.md#8-what-could-not-be-derived), [design_basis.md OQ-DES-DB7](../design/design_basis.md#open-questions), [corner.md](../design/corner.md), [draw_flat_offset.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_flat_offset.py) |

A drawing showing the number without the note is right about the number and silent about the geometry. **Three steps, in order.** *One:* sweep for every dimension whose derivation branches -- `max`, `min`, or a conditional -- because the count decides how many notes a sheet must hold, and `flat_offset` may not be the only one. `check_derivation.py` already evaluates both branches for every variant, so it knows which won and the sweep is a report rather than new analysis. *Two:* decide where the note goes.
