The budget is tight: the drawn view is at **75.4 %** against a 75 % requirement (DRW-9), and an eleventh table row already takes it to 73.3 %. *Three:* emit the note and fail a sheet carrying a branching dimension without one. **Depends on IP-FC-97**, and that is the only real edge in the queue: the note moves every other annotation, so determinism has to be verified first or a diff between runs cannot be attributed | IP-FC-97, IP-FC-86 | [system_requirements.md DES-9](../design/system_requirements.md#4-design-requirements), [design_basis.md INT-3](../design/design_basis.md#int-3--corner-seating-faces--bulkhead), [system_requirements.md OQ-DES-SR2](../design/system_requirements.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-100 | todo | **DES-15 is verified by nothing: compare the two ends of a built bay, aperture excluded.** DES-15, added 2026-08-30 under OQ-DES-SR3, says that at a bay end the features mating with the adjoining bay -- the **OML perimeter**, the **longeron positions and sizes**, and the **fastener positions and sizes** -- are identical across every bay of the same size, so any bay end mates any other. **The bulkhead's interior aperture is explicitly not one of those features.** It is unconstrained, it varies with offsets, and on a boom bulkhead -- and on other planar bulkheads that may yet be defined -- it is neither a standard dimension nor a standard shape. **The exclusion is the part the checker most needs to get right:** a naive whole-face comparison would fail on correct parts, which is how a check ends up being disabled rather than fixed. **What to do:** at each end of a built bay, collect the mating features by kind rather than by comparing the face set wholesale -- perimeter, longeron bores, fastener positions and sizes -- and compare them across bay types of the same size. This is the same kind of face-level assertion `check_derived_geometry.py` already makes, and it belongs beside it. **How to know it worked:** it must pass across the bulkhead types as they stand, including the boom bulkhead whose aperture differs, so the negative control is a deliberately shifted `bolt_offset` on one type -- which must fail -- paired with a deliberately altered aperture -- which must **not** | IP-FC-87 | [system_requirements.md DES-15](../design/system_requirements.md#4-design-requirements), [design_basis.md DES-15](../design/design_basis.md#4-why-each-design-requirement-is-what-it-is), [system_requirements.md OQ-DES-SR3](../design/system_requirements.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-101 | done | **The last three open questions in the requirement set are decided, and a hollow phrase is withdrawn.** All on 2026-08-30. **OQ-DES-SR2: build all three checks, ordered by dependency.** The question's alternative 2 had offered *"does not force a drafting decision under time pressure"* as a benefit of deferring DES-9. **There is no time pressure and there never was** -- the phrase was carried over from AP-3's reasoning, where the concern had been manufacturing a justification rather than admitting a gap, and it does not transfer. Removing the invented urgency leaves one true observation: **DES-9's cost is sheet real estate rather than code**, which is a dependency and not a reason to defer. The order is now set by dependency alone, and **only one edge is real** -- IP-FC-99 after IP-FC-97, because adding a note moves every annotation on the sheet and an unattributable diff is worse than no check. IP-FC-95, 98 and 100 are independent. **OQ-DES-SR3: DES-15, scoped to the mating features.** This document's draft requirement said *"the same end interface"* and was **false**: sameness holds for the OML perimeter, the longerons and the fasteners, and not for the bulkhead's interior aperture. Excluding the aperture is **AR-MOD-1** doing its job -- a minimally constraining interface constrains what must be shared and deliberately nothing else -- which is why DES-15 cites AR-MOD-1 beside AR-MOD-2 and AR-MOD-3: two parents say what must be the same, the third says why the rest must not be. **Coverage is now 13 of 19 architectural requirements with no gaps left**; the six uncited are the four objectives, which the groups refine rather than decompose, AR-MOD-6 for a wing interface that is not a fuselage part, and AR-MOD-7 carried by a given. **50 requirements below 19 architectural.** **Five are verified by nothing and every one now has a work item** -- which is the real change, more than the count: the category *"nothing verifies this and we do not know what would"* is empty | IP-FC-96 | [system_requirements.md OQ-DES-SR2](../design/system_requirements.md#open-questions), [system_requirements.md OQ-DES-SR3](../design/system_requirements.md#open-questions), [system_requirements.md section 9.3](../design/system_requirements.md#93-verification-status), [design_basis.md](../design/design_basis.md) |
| IP-FC-102 | done | **OQ-DES-D7's three prose rows are drawn, so the question can be answered by looking.** The register's *Governing expression* column does two jobs: rows 1, 4, 6 and 7 give the whole nominal, rows 2, 3 and 5 give only the clearance. Deciding whether to require the nominal everywhere means seeing what the nominal *is*, which prose was not delivering. [`tools/draw_register_rows.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_register_rows.py) emits two figures into `doc/design/img/register_rows/`; **diagrams of the definition, not tracings of a built solid**, and each reproduces the measured value the section already quotes -- socket radius **3.3000**, bulkhead outer face **45.1375**. **Row 3 gets no figure** because its geometry is `flat_offset`, already drawn under OQ-DES-DB7, and because it is the row whose full expression would add no parameter name at all. **What drawing them found.** *One:* the length objection and the benefit land on different rows. Alternative 1's stated drawback is that expressions get long; row 2 is four terms in a line and row 5's position is three, while row 3 -- the genuinely long nested `max` -- is the row that would gain nothing. *Two, and this is the finding:* **row 5 is right by accident.** Its prose reads *"the panel's outer surface lands on the mold line at `corner_radius`"*, a sentence about where the face sits -- and the face's POSITION is `unit_width/2 - panel_thickness - panel_tolerance`, which does not use `corner_radius` at all. `corner_radius` is nevertheless owed by this joint, through the face's EXTENT: the exposed span is `unit_width - 2*(corner_radius + panel_offset) - 2*panel_overlap`, and the built face measures 392.8500 mm2, that span times the bulkhead thickness. So the test demands the name, the bulkhead owes the name, **and the reason the register gives is not the reason it is owed** -- the same failure mode this question already names for joint 3's coverage being a coincidence of rows 1 and 4. **Under-naming is detectable by inspection; being right by accident is not**, which makes alternative 4 more expensive than it looked, and is a further argument that a row stating the whole nominal cannot be wrong in this way because the expression is then what the geometry evaluates rather than a sentence about it. **A first draft of this row and of the inserted section claimed `corner_radius` was not consumed by joint 5 at all. That was wrong and was caught before it stood**, by checking the joint's Follows line: the span consumes it. The corrected reading is sharper than the wrong one | IP-FC-94 | [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D7](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), [draw_register_rows.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_register_rows.py), [design_basis.md INT-5](../design/design_basis.md#int-5--panel--bulkhead-flange) |
| IP-FC-103 | superseded (IP-FC-104) | **Superseded 2026-08-30 by IP-FC-104 in its conclusion, not in its method.** The rasterize-from-the-booleans fix stands and the *floor, not the gap* finding stands; the "standoff that chamfers nothing" framing throughout does not. Original entry follows. **The `flat_offset` figures were wrong twice and are rebuilt from the booleans.** Raised 2026-08-30 on review of IP-FC-94's drawings: the depicted geometry did not read as the parameter it claimed to explain, and it did not, because **both errors were in the drawing rather than in the reading**. **Error one: the profile is an octant, not a quarter.** `corner_middle_shape` subtracts a **mirror mask** `[[-far,-far],[far,far],[far,-far]]`, which keeps `y >= x`, and a **third-quadrant mask**. The first figures drew neither, showing a full 0-90 degree quarter with material filling the region the masks remove -- **including the sliver at `y = 0` that `longeron_chamfer` is the whole point of**. The one thing the figure existed to make visible was the one thing it painted over. **Error two: `longeron_chamfer` is a floor, not the gap.** The source comment says it -- *"use longeron_chamfer as a minimum"* -- and the figures labelled the dimension `longeron_chamfer 0.60` on both branches. Measured off the predicate: on the bore branch the standoff is **0.6000, exactly the floor**; on the panel branch at 0.5U with a 1 mm panel it is **1.8000**, three times it, because `flat_offset` is pushed out past what the bore needs. The panel-branch figure carried a dimension that was wrong by a factor of three. **The fix is method, not touch-up.** `draw_flat_offset.py` now **rasterizes the material from a term-for-term transcription of the source's boolean expression** and draws only the annotation from the definition, so the fill cannot disagree with the booleans the way a hand-traced outline can. It also emits the measured standoff on every run, which is what caught error two. Each figure now carries two panels: the octant as built, where the standoff lies on the symmetry line and reads as an artifact of where the cut falls, and the same profile **mirrored about `y = x`** -- the first step of `octant_to_full()` -- where the halves join and it reads as what it is, the wall between the longeron bore and the corner's inboard boundary. **The prose in `design_basis.md` and `corner.md` repeated both errors and is corrected in place.** **The lesson is the same one OQ-DES-DB3 was decided on, one level down:** a confident hand-derivation from a source that was fully visible was wrong in two independent ways, and neither survived contact with an evaluation of the source itself | IP-FC-94 | [design_basis.md INT-3](../design/design_basis.md#int-3--corner-seating-faces--bulkhead), [draw_flat_offset.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_flat_offset.py), [corner.md](../design/corner.md), [design_basis.md OQ-DES-DB7](../design/design_basis.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-104 | done | **The corner snaps onto the longeron, and `longeron_chamfer` is the chamfer that lets it. Four documented readings said otherwise.** Recorded 2026-08-30. **Measured, not reconstructed** ([`tools/draw_flat_offset.py`](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_flat_offset.py), rasterizing `corner_middle_shape`'s own booleans): the corner wraps **270.0 degrees** of the bore and opens toward the fuselage interior through a mouth whose clear opening is **2.8991 mm against a 4.0000 mm longeron** at U=1, and **1.4849 against 2.0000** at U=0.5. The mouth is narrower than the rod, so the corner is pressed on and the arms spread. **The mouth's two walls are the chamfer**: flat cuts at 90 degrees to each other, so each stands at 45 degrees to the direction of insertion -- the lead-in that spreads the arms -- running from the bore out to the arm tip at `[flat_offset, 0]`. Being in the cross-section, the chamfer runs the **full length** of the corner. `longeron_chamfer` is the **minimum width** of each face: **0.6000** on the bore branch, exactly the parameter, and **1.8000** on the panel branch against the same minimum. Without it the wall runs tangent to the bore and the tip is a knife edge. **The `// longeron chamfer` comment at `fuselage_corner_geometry.scad:258` is on the right line** -- it labels the third-quadrant mask, which is what cuts the mouth; IP-FC-94 called it an implementation artifact on the wrong line and that was wrong. **Four readings preceded this one**, each a reconstruction from the expressions, each written with more confidence than the last: *the smallest chamfer worth printing*; a *standoff* chamfering nothing; a *floor on that standoff*; and the 45 degree bulkhead seating diagonal. Every figure generated along the way **drew the chamfer** while the prose beside it denied there was one, and one reading was written into `corner.md` as a *correction* over wording that had been closer to right. **A fifth reading was drafted and not applied**, because the pattern was finally recognized and the question asked instead -- which is [OQ-DES-DB3](../design/design_basis.md#open-questions)'s decision working as intended, four iterations late. **Corrected in place:** `design_basis.md` INT-3 and section 8 item 3, `corner.md`'s two withdrawn correction blocks, and the figures, which now draw both chamfer faces in orange, mark the mouth, and print its clear opening against the longeron diameter. **OQ-DES-DB7 is withdrawn** -- it asked whether to rename a constant that chamfers nothing, and the constant chamfers something. **Also withdrawn: the 0.5 mm attribution.** This project reported 0.5 mm first, in error; the response that figure drew was then recorded in OQ-DES-DB7 as though it were an independent recollection, which is how a mistake becomes a citation. Nothing in the code is 0.5 -- `longeron_chamfer` is `extrusion_width` = **0.6 mm** at U=1, `flange_chamfer` is **1.0 mm**. **What survives from the superseded rows:** `flat_offset`'s four polygon vertices on one 45 degree line; the 240-to-24 branch split; the flat face being **gone, not small**, when the panel branch governs; the flange chamfer at `fuselage_bulkhead_geometry.scad:125` being cowling-only and on one face; and *floor, not gap* | IP-FC-94, IP-FC-103 | [design_basis.md INT-3](../design/design_basis.md#int-3--corner-seating-faces--bulkhead), [design_basis.md section 8](../design/design_basis.md#8-what-could-not-be-derived), [corner.md](../design/corner.md), [draw_flat_offset.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/draw_flat_offset.py) |

Second, **six literal `{d}` placeholders were sitting in the rendered document**: a patch script applied `.replace('{d}', em_dash)` to its search strings and not to its replacement strings, so the placeholder was written through into the file and survived review. Coverage following which branch of an unrelated `max` wins is the exact failure OQ-DES-D7 was decided against. **Blocked because adding a dimension is a drawing decision** -- OQ-DES-D11 has the four alternatives, including doing it on the panel's own sheet once the panel enters the drawing partition | IP-FC-107 | [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D11](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) |

Row 5 describes a panel seating against a bulkhead, and two bulkhead families have no panel: `bulkhead-end_anchor_end_bolt-257941` and `bulkhead-interconnect-b591ef`. The structural-zero rule drops `panel_thickness`, `panel_tolerance` and `panel_overlap` on them because those are zero -- **and `panel_offset` is not zero**, so it survives and is demanded for a joint the part does not have. The cowling family escapes only because `panel_offset` happens to be zero on it as well. **This is the error OQ-DES-D6 was decided to remove**, arriving through a door its fix does not cover: `CARRIED_BY_TYPES` restricts by *type name*, and having a panel is a topology property that splits a type into two families. | IP-FC-111 | todo | **`panel_extension` is bound to an expression, not to the face it points at, and goes wrong the moment `corner_tolerance` is nonzero.** Found 2026-08-30 by probing rather than reading: the pipeline was run with `corner_tolerance` forced to 0.1 and the profile rasterized from `corner_middle_shape`'s booleans. The corner's end face **is** the bulkhead seating flat -- `check_derived_geometry.py:102` names it that -- and sits at `flat_x = -(panel_overlap + panel_offset) + corner_tolerance`. At 1U with 3/16 in panel: **states 7.2625, material ends at 7.1625**, endpoint 0.1 off the part and in air. **Nothing catches it.** `check_drawing` asks whether a dimension reports the value its parameters say, and 7.2625 *is* `panel_overlap + panel_offset`, so it passes; it never asks whether the endpoints land on the solid. `check_derived_geometry` measures the part correctly and never looks at the sheet. **Fix:** bind the dimension's endpoint and its declared value to `flat_x`, and add `corner_tolerance` to what it carries. **Today the drawn number does not change** -- the two expressions are equal while the clearance is 0 -- so the test is exact: every sheet must come out identical, and the probe with a nonzero tolerance must show the dimension following the face. Not a decision; the face location is not in question | IP-FC-107 | [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D10](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-112 | todo | **The corner's sheet declares no `corner_tolerance`, so a line-to-line fit is invisible and stays invisible if the parameter changes.** Found 2026-08-30. Probed: with `corner_tolerance` forced to 0.1, **the structural-zero exclusion correctly stops applying** -- it is recomputed per family every run and is not baked into the current sweep -- and all three corner families immediately report `unstated=['corner_tolerance']`. So the completeness test would flag it. **But no dimension or table entry would appear**, because no quantity on either sheet carries the parameter. The sheet would be demanded-and-silent rather than wrong, which is the better failure but still a failure. **Fix follows an existing pattern rather than setting one:** the corner already carries `longeron_clearance` and `greeble_clearance` as table constants with no dimension line, which is how a clearance between two parts is stated on a single-part drawing. A `corner_clearance` entry carrying `corner_tolerance`, `constant=True`, sits beside them. **No sheet changes today** -- the structural-zero filter drops the column while the value is 0 -- and the column appears by itself when it is not. That is the point: the sheet stops depending on the parameter's current value. Bears on [OQ-DES-D12](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions), which is why the filter drops it, but this row does not wait on that answer | IP-FC-107 | [dimension_scheme.md OQ-DES-D12](../design/dimension_scheme.md#open-questions) |
| IP-FC-113 | done | **The sweep could only ever write to one directory, and none of them are in version control.** Found 2026-08-30 while preparing the first full FreeCAD sweep. `OUTPUT_DIR` was a module constant and `_run_all_sweeps()` took no argument, so **every run wrote to `variant_output/` whatever the backend** -- rendering with `--backend freecad` would have overwritten the OpenSCAD output in place, and `.gitignore:257` excludes the whole tree, so there was nothing to recover from. Four trees, 2.3-2.6 GB each, all untracked: `variant_output`, `variant_output_baseline`, `variant_output_original`, `variant_output_ipfc80_20260818`. **Fix: `--output-dir`**, threaded through `main()` and `_run_all_sweeps()`; the resolved destination is **printed at the top of every run** rather than left to be inferred. `rebuild_previews` already took the argument and now receives it from the flag too. **Second layer, belt and braces:** all 6,340 files across the four trees were set read-only, so a misdirected write fails loudly instead of succeeding quietly. **Also found:** `freecad/drawing.py`'s docstring documents `freecadcmd drawing.py --pass --kind corner --params p.json --out sheet.dxf`, and the module has **no `__main__` block and no argument parsing at all** -- that invocation has never worked. `build_sheet` is a library function whose only caller is `check_drawing.py`, which is therefore the only thing that builds a family sheet end to end and exports it. So there is no way to generate the drawing set as such; IP-FC-114 | IP-FC-10, IP-FC-11 | [fuselage_variants.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/fuselage_variants.py) |
| IP-FC-114 | todo | **There is no entry point that produces the drawing set.** Found 2026-08-30. `drawing_families.py` says which 16 family sheets the set needs and which 10 have annotation sets; `freecad/drawing.py` can build one; nothing joins them. The only end-to-end path is `check_drawing.py`, which builds a sheet **for one kind from one variant** and exports `check_drawing_<kind>_<run>.dxf` as evidence for its own assertions. So the artefact a reader would ask for -- the sheets -- exists only as a checker's by-product, one at a time, under a checker's filename. **Needs:** a driver that walks the families with annotation sets, exports each one's parameters, builds the sheet and writes it under the family key. **Note the export is lossy** (IP-FC-33): TechDraw's DXF drops all but the first line of a multi-line annotation, so the format question travels with this work rather than being settled by it | IP-FC-21, IP-FC-113 | [drawing.py](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/drawing.py) |

The reason to want the comparison is that A ends on a cut between two doubly curved NURBS solids 0.6 mm apart, which is the hardest thing this construction asks of OCC and the operation both defects below came out of, whereas B asks for two easier booleans and a fuse. **A is measured and B is not.** On the tail at `U` = 1, A gives 23757.8156 mm3 in one solid with 0.000000 mm3 of rib residue, four consecutive trials identical to four decimals at 409-489 s each. B returned `Bnd_Box is void` -- a null shape reaching `.Volume` -- which is a defect in the comparison harness and **not a result about B**. Fix the harness to name which operand went null at which step, then run both constructions on both cowls and compare wall volume, solid count, rib residue and time. Until that exists, read A's numbers as evidence that A is reproducible and as no evidence at all that it is the better of the two. **Run 2026-09-05, and A stays.** Both cowls were built once, and the blank, the smooth interior and the fused rib tool were saved to BREP so that both constructions were handed the *identical* operands -- one surface fit, not two, which is what makes it a comparison of the assembly rather than of the parts. **On the tail A takes 316 s and B takes 723 s**, and the premise that B asks the kernel for easier work is wrong: B's fuse alone is 165 s, the single most expensive operation in either route, because `blank - smooth` and `blank & ribs` *overlap* -- the shell already contains whatever rib material lies outside the smooth interior -- so it is a union of two intersecting multi-solid sets and not the disjoint one the item assumed. **B also needs a cleanup step that fails.** `removeSplitter` raises `Bnd_Box is void` on the tail's fused wall in 0.8 s, so B either ships a wall carrying 2735 faces against A's 2480 or does not finish; three other spellings of the same union -- fusing the solid list, and fusing one rib solid at a time -- give the identical shape, so it is not how the operands are presented. On the nose B is 46 s against A's 35 s and `removeSplitter` succeeds, and its partition slip is 2.455e-04, above `PARTITION_TOL`, where A's is 9.947e-07. **The two constructions do build the same wall** -- the symmetric difference is empty in both directions and 2003 sampled boundary points of each lie on the other's faces to within 6e-10 mm on both parts -- so there is no quality argument for B either, and nothing in the build changes. **The harness defect that produced nothing last time is fixed**: every intermediate is named, measured and refused if null, which is how the failure was pinned to one operation instead of a bare `Bnd_Box is void`. What this run cost in measurement, and what it found about measuring these solids at all, is IP-FC-120 | IP-FC-17 | [cowl_interior_surface.md §9](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md) |
| IP-FC-116 | todo | **The rib dilation builds a 250-face tool where 15 would do, and every boolean after it carries the face count.** `cowl_interior.dilated_notches` grows each buttress cut by translating the whole solid to 48 evenly spaced directions on a horizontal circle of radius `t` and fusing the 48 copies. The *shape* is right -- a horizontal disc, because perimeter width is measured in the layer plane and a ball would erode the wall by `t` along the normal instead -- but it produces a rib solid of roughly 250 faces where offsetting the notch's section and re-lofting would give about 15, and the tail has 22 of them, so every boolean downstream carries the face count. **The timing this item was filed on is wrong, corrected 2026-09-05.** It said the dilation is more than half the build and quoted 315, 321 and 336 s over three builds at `U` = 1. That timer starts *after* `dilated_notches` returns: it covers fusing the 22 dilated tools together, cutting them out of the smooth interior and checking the residue, none of which is the dilation. Instrumented end to end on one tail build at `U` = 1: **surface fit 194 s, dilation 72 s, rib fuse + cut + residue 337 s, 609 s in all**. So the dilation is **12 %** of the build and the boolean work on its output is **55 %**. The face count is still worth attacking, but for what it does to the 337 s rather than because the dilation itself is slow, and **route (a) below is the one aimed at the part that actually costs**. The 48-gon is also only an approximation of the disc -- its inscribed radius is `t*cos(pi/48)` = 0.99786 `t`, so the dilation is 0.0013 mm short at each facet midpoint, well inside the 0.05 mm tolerance but not exact. Two routes are already scouted: **(a)** cut the tools one at a time with the residue check after each rather than fusing 22 first, measured at 202 s against 256 s in a harness, which buys time but not face count; **(b)** dilate section-wise with `makeOffset2D` and re-loft, which was built and failed only on the 1e-3 mm sliver caps used to join loft spans -- a known and local defect, not a dead end. **A third route -- keep the construction and just use fewer facets -- is closed, measured 2026-09-05.** `RIB_FACETS` = 24 through the full acceptance test lost **1332 mm3** of wall (22348.60 against ~23680) and left two stations reporting **0.1035 mm** where 0.6 is wanted, **while the rib gaps still measured 1.3000 and 1.4000 mm with worst error 0.0000** -- the gap check cannot see this failure, because the ribs it measures are the ones that did form. The cause is connectivity, not accuracy: the copies sit on a circle of radius `t`, so adjacent ones are `2*t*sin(pi/N)` apart, and their union is a solid only while that spacing is smaller than the slab's own in-plane thickness -- below it the "dilated" tool is a comb, still a valid solid, and the wall comes out with material missing where the gaps fell. The floor is `N > pi / asin(w / 2t)`, which for the 0.1 mm axial cut at `t` = 0.6 is 37.6, so **38 minimum and 48 leaves 26 % headroom**; the 0.2 mm diagonals need only 19, which is why N = 24 failed at some stations and not others. Probed on the real cutting tools: **48 accepted in 72 s, 38 accepted in 41 s, 37 refused at 0.1018 mm spacing against 0.1004 mm of thickness, 24 refused at 0.1566, 16 at 0.2341**. `dilated_notches` now derives that floor per tool and raises rather than trusting the constant, and `RIB_FACETS` records that it is a floor and not a tuning knob. Going 48 -> 38 would save 31 s of 609 and spend all of the margin, so **the facet count is not where the time is**. The confound named below is resolved and the premise is now much stronger than it was. It is not the entry point: two builds in one process differ. It is not the refinement: three controlled repeats chose byte-identical station lists, the same 16 to four decimals. It is not anything upstream of the surface: the body, the per-station pipeline, the fitted poles, that surface sectioned and that surface closed as a solid are each bit-identical over four trials. | IP-FC-118 | done | **P4 is a precondition that nothing asserts, and the one check that could see it silently looks away.** P4, stated 2026-09-04 and recorded in [cowl_interior_surface.md](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md) §2: **no notch lies in the layer plane**, because a rib forms only where the slicer's single perimeter can walk into the notch and back out *within* a layer -- a horizontal notch offers no such path, the feature would have to form between layers instead, which thin-wall perimeter slicing cannot do, and the rib comes out malformed. It is also what bounds the rib thickness `2*n_p*w + t_cut / sin θ` ([OQ-DES-CW19](../design/cowl.md)) away from its singularity at θ = 0. **Nothing checks it.** `check_cowl_interior.in_plane_width` returns `None` when the cut face's normal is vertical and `rib_gap` answers with `continue`, so a horizontal notch is dropped from the rib check without comment and the build reports `OK` -- a design-domain violation passing as a silence, which is the exact failure P1's own paragraph in that document warns about (*"a precondition that is merely true today is an accident waiting to be inherited"*). **The plain part needs no decision:** a horizontal cut is outside the design, so raise `PreconditionFailed` and name the notch, the way P1 and P2 already do, instead of skipping it; and report each notch's in-plane width so a shallow one is visible rather than silent. **A limit on *shallow-but-not-horizontal* would be a design decision** -- the design contains nothing shallower than the tail's 30° and no minimum angle is stated anywhere -- so raise it as an open question if one is ever wanted, rather than inventing a threshold here. **Built 2026-09-04.** `cowl_interior.slab_normal` reads the slab's own plane from its largest planar face, and `dilated_notches` -- the one place that sees every cutting tool -- raises `PreconditionFailed` on a tool that lies in the layer plane and on a tool with no planar face at all, which is not a slab and whose thickness has no direction. The threshold `FLAT_TOL` = 1e-9 is documented as a **numerical guard and not a design threshold**, so nobody later reads it as a statement that a 5° cut is acceptable. Each build now reports the shallowest cut plane it saw, which is the number to look at if a rib ever measures wider than expected: the nose reads 90.00° and the tail 30.00°, and a 5° cut would leave a 2.35 mm rib. In the checker, `in_plane_width` raises instead of returning `None` and `rib_gap`'s `continue` is gone, so the silent skip has no path left; P4 joins P1 and P2 in the block of preconditions that must fire, tested against a plate lying in the layer plane. Probed across orientations before the full run: a horizontal slab is refused, a tool with no planar face is refused, and 90/60/30/5° slabs report exactly their own angle. Verified in the full acceptance run the same day: both kinds pass with P4 among the preconditions that fire, and the rib gaps measure 1.3000 mm at every axial notch and 1.4000 mm at the tail's diagonals, worst error 0.0000 against a 0.05 mm tolerance. | IP-FC-17 | [cowl_interior_surface.md §2](../design/cowl_interior_surface.md), [cowl.md §OQ-DES-CW19](../design/cowl.md) |
| IP-FC-119 | done | **`Shape.Volume`, `Face.Area` and `CenterOfMass` are unreliable on this project's B-spline solids, and three conclusions were drawn from them before that was checked.** Found 2026-09-05 while chasing what became [OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md). **Measured on two `tail_shell` builds at `U` = 1.** `Face.Area` reports 47.5811 mm2 and 48.6157 mm2 — a 2.2 % difference — for a face whose boundary wire is identical to **0.0004 mm2** and whose outline matches point for point to **286 nm**; the wire signed areas are 48.2074 and 48.2070. `Shape.Volume` reports 23680.884053 and 23700.694092, a difference of 19.810039 mm3, where the divergence theorem over a 1.8-million-triangle tessellation gives 23737.997001 and 23738.003187 — a difference of **0.006186 mm3**, and both **0.16 to 0.24 % above** what `Shape.Volume` says. `CenterOfMass` comes from the same integration and moved 0.0915 mm on that same identical face. **What to do:** measure by refining a tessellation until the value stops moving, and treat the integrated properties as indicative only. Do not change any *export* deflection to achieve it — measurement and export are separate concerns, and the export setting is chosen for printing. **Then audit the callers**, because several checks compare volumes across shapes: `cowl_interior.shell_solid`'s partition identity, its rib-residue check, and `compare_backends`. Those may well be safe — they difference volumes computed the same way on overlapping geometry, where the error largely cancels, and the measured partition slips are ~1e-6 — but "probably cancels" is what this item exists to replace with a measurement. **Built and audited 2026-09-05.** [`solid_measure.py`](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/solid_measure.py) measures a solid by refining its tessellation and integrating the divergence theorem, and **raises rather than returning a value that is still moving** -- which is the mistake `Shape.Volume` invites, since it always answers. Two constants, and the gap between them is the finding: an **absolute** volume does not settle below **1e-4** and does not even fall monotonically -- 23729.508, 23735.816, 23738.079, then *down* to 23736.472 at 0.00025 mm and 5.7 million triangles -- while a **difference** between two solids tessellated at the same deflection converges cleanly to **1e-5**, because they carry nearly the same discretisation error and it cancels. `converged_difference` returns the tail pair at +0.006186 mm3, 2.606e-07 relative, in 148 s, and returns exactly 0.000000000 for a solid against itself. **The audit found the error does *not* cancel across a partition**, which is the opposite of what was expected: splitting the wall with a plane and summing the pieces slips **1.604e-03** by `Shape.Volume` against 4.4e-05 by tessellation, so 38 of those 38 mm3 are the instrument. `PARTITION_TOL` now records that its real resolution is not the 1e-4 it claims, that it is a gross-failure detector, and that a marginal firing should be blamed on the measurement first. Nothing is failing today -- the slips seen on the cavity cut are ~1e-6 | IP-FC-17 | [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-120 | done (the comparison half is IP-FC-122) | **The same two solids were called identical by three measurements and 112 mm3 apart by a fourth, and picking the right one took most of a day.** Found 2026-09-05 under IP-FC-115, comparing two assemblies of one `tail_shell` wall. They are the same set: the symmetric difference is empty and valid in both directions, 2003 sampled boundary points of each lie on the other's faces to within 6e-10 mm, and two closed surfaces that coincide enclose the same volume. But **their tessellations differ by 112.46 mm3, 4.7e-03**, and refining does not close the gap -- 111.33 at deflection 0.005 mm, 112.46 at 0.001 -- so the usual test for a discretisation artefact, that it collapses under refinement, says the wrong thing here. Both meshes are closed: 0 unpaired edges on one and 3 in 2768775 on the other. The difference is between a wall whose split faces were merged and one whose were not, `removeSplitter` having raised on it. **The nose is the control and it shows the opposite**: two walls with different face counts, 503 against 449, whose meshes agree to 0.032 mm3 -- 3.3e-06 -- while `Shape.Volume` claims they are 91.08 mm3 apart, because merging faces moved that figure by 0.94 % without moving the solid. So the error is not predictable from the partition: it can be enormous or absent, and neither the number nor the convergence tells you which. **The first half is answered, 2026-09-06: the two walls are the same solid, and one of the two meshes is wrong.** Sectioning both at 101 stations -- an instrument that uses neither booleans nor tessellation nor point sampling -- puts them within **0.055 mm2** at every station on sections of about 320 mm2, with the sign alternating, which is shoelace noise on a 2000-point loop, and with matching loop counts throughout. Integrating those sections gives **A 23730.94 and B 23730.81 mm3, 0.13 mm3 apart**, and that integral sits **0.02 % from A's mesh volume and 0.45 % from B's**. So three independent instruments agree the walls are identical and the mesh volume of B is the outlier. **What was ruled out, each by measurement**, so that none of it is repeated: *chordal error* -- both meshes are within 1 to 2 times the 0.001 mm they were built to, measured as the distance from triangle centroids to their own surface, 0.002 mm worst on A and 0.001 on B; *sliver faces* -- the two meshes' largest triangles are 323 and 299 mm2 and their median triangle areas agree to 5 %, so B's tessellation is not degenerate; *orientation* -- both are perfectly wound, 0 directed edges traversed twice and 0 without an opposite, in 5.5 and 5.2 million, so neither volume is a sign error; *different surfaces* -- the mesh areas are 84976.5454 and 84970.1853 mm2, 7.5e-05 apart; *bias from the partition itself* -- a hollow cylinder of the same surface-to-volume ratio, 3.36 against the wall's 3.58, measured against its exact volume converges to 4.2e-06 and its two partitions differ by **6.6e-09** of the part, so the instrument is neither inaccurate nor partition-sensitive in general; and *insufficient refinement* -- the gap is 111.33, 112.46, 112.51 mm3 at deflections 0.005, 0.001 and 0.00025, immovable across a 22-fold rise in triangle count. **What is left unexplained** is how a mesh that is closed, consistently wound, inside its deflection, carrying the right total area and with its vertices on the true surface encloses 0.5 % less volume than the solid it came from. That is a question about OCC's tessellator rather than about this project, and **nothing built depends on the answer**: production builds construction A, whose mesh is the one that agrees with the sections. **The rule to carry forward** is that a mesh volume of a thin shell cannot be trusted alone -- this one was wrong by 0.5 % while passing every check that a mesh can be given -- so a volume that matters is measured twice, by different instruments, or it is not measured. A failed attempt worth not repeating: binning each triangle's own contribution to `integral z n_z dA` by height does not localise the difference, because that quantity is not local -- two triangulations of one surface disagree by thousands of mm3 per band while cancelling to 112 overall, noise 150 times the signal. **Where it lands is `compare_backends`, and that half is now blocked on [OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md).** `build_part` writes every cowl to STL at `COWL_LINEAR_DEFLECTION` = 0.02 mm from whatever solid the tree produced, and nothing checks the result against the solid. Two things were found by reading the comparison rather than by measuring: **`kind_of` will classify a shelled part as the solid one it was hollowed out of** -- it matches known kind names as substrings, the kind table contains no shelled kind, and `..._tail_shell.stl` contains `tail` -- so a shell would be compared at `TOL_FREEFORM` = 6.0e-4 with no warning; and **that tolerance is eight times smaller than the error measured above** on a correct part. Nothing is failing yet, for two separate reasons: production builds construction A, whose faces are merged, and **no shelled STL exists in any output tree** -- checked across all seven `variant_output*` trees on 2026-09-06 -- so the misclassification fires the first time a sweep runs with shells enabled. **But the tolerance is not the defect -- the denominator is.** A mesh's volume error is a surface phenomenon, offset times area, so expressing it as a fraction of the part's *own volume* charges a hollow part for being hollow. Measured 2026-09-06: 6.0e-4 means a **0.006578 mm** surface offset on the tail solid and **0.000168 mm** on its shell, 39 times tighter, 1 % of the 0.02 mm the part is exported at. The same measurement disposes of the nose-against-tail framing: the two shells' A/V ratios are 3.58 and 3.57 and their implied offsets agree to the printed digit, so the class that matters is solid against shell. And the 112.5 mm3 anomaly above is **0.001324 mm** of surface offset, 6.6 % of the export deflection -- small in the unit it belongs in, and alarming only when divided by a shell's volume. [OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) now asks whether the criterion should be **`|Va - Vb| / (A * 100U)`**, dimensionless -- area to turn it into the surface offset the error physically is, and `100U` because that is the length scale the rest of the metric set already uses -- and recommends it at **6.6e-5**, what the present tolerance already grants the solid parts it works on, with the classification fix as its first step, since that is wrong under every alternative and needs no number. It also records that the quantity is dimensionless but not yet `U`-invariant while the export deflection is fixed at 0.02 mm, which is the same drift `compare_backends` already reports across `U`. **What is already settled and should not be re-derived:** `isInside` costs 1.3 s a point on these solids, a thousand times its cost on a box, so membership sampling cannot resolve anything below about 2650 mm3 in a 25-minute run and is not the tool; the sampled surface distance must be taken to the other solid's **faces**, since `distToShape` against a solid returns 0 for any point inside it and is blind to exactly the missing-interior case it would be used to rule out; and a shape hands back the triangulation it already has, so a caller that refines from fine to coarse silently measures the fine mesh twice | IP-FC-115 | [solid_measure.py](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/solid_measure.py), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-121 | done, verification pending | **The cowl export deflection is a fixed 0.02 mm at every `U`, so a part four times the size is meshed four times as finely relative to itself.** Decided and built 2026-09-06 out of [OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md). `build_part.COWL_LINEAR_DEFLECTION` becomes the figure at `U` = 1 and a new `export_deflection(kind, seed)` multiplies it by the part's own `U`, taken from the parameter file, which carries it beside `unit_width` for every swept part; a seed without one falls back to the `U` = 1 figure rather than guessing, the same choice `mesh_stats.u_of_name` makes for a filename with no `U`. **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** the comparison across backends was measuring the export setting as much as the geometry -- `compare_backends` records the tail's volume error falling 2.1e-04 to 1.5e-05 from `U` = 0.5 to 4, which is the deflection becoming a smaller fraction of a bigger part -- and the nondimensional criterion OQ-ARCH-20 proposes is only `U`-invariant once this is true. **Scoped deliberately:** `LINEAR_DEFLECTION` for the prismatic kinds is untouched, because it sits on a float32 STL floor past about 26k facets and was chosen to land within a facet count of the OpenSCAD reference so IP-FC-13 compares geometry and not mesh density; `oml_blank.VOLUME_DEFLECTION` is untouched because it is the acceptance measure and answers to OQ-DES-CW12. `write_mesh` now takes the deflection rather than deriving it from the kind -- the rule was hiding inside a mesher call -- and the build line prints what it meshed at, since it is no longer a constant a reader can assume. **Verified 2026-09-06:** the rule returns 0.02 at `U` = 1, 0.08 at 4, 0.01 at 0.5, 0.02 for a seed with no `U`, and 1e-3 for a bulkhead at `U` = 4; a real export of the tail wall gives 76704 facets at 0.02 mm and 27880 at 0.08. **What is not yet verified, and is the reason this is not simply done:** every FreeCAD cowl STL at `U` != 1 changes, and `geometry_version` hashes `build_part.py` so the version bumps for every FreeCAD kind. Nothing shows the cross-backend comparison still passes at other scales. That needs `compare_backends` over the cowl kinds at several `U`, which is machine time on the working machine and should be asked for rather than assumed | IP-FC-120 | [build_part.py](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/build_part.py), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-122 | done, verification pending | **The cross-backend volume criterion becomes `|Va - Vb| / (A * 100U)`, and the shelled kinds stop being compared as the solids they were hollowed out of.** Built 2026-09-06 from [OQ-ARCH-20](../architecture/freecad_migration.md), decided the same day. `mesh_stats` records **`area`** -- one cross product per triangle on data already in hand -- and gains `volume_offset(a, b, u)`, which returns the volume difference as a fraction of the part: area turns it into the distance the surface would have to move to account for it, and `100U` makes that a pure number comparable between parts of different sizes and between a solid and its shell. `compare_backends` lists `nose_cowl_shell` and `tail_shell` in its kind table -- **without them `kind_of` matched the longest known name inside the filename and answered `nose_cowl` and `tail`**, so a shell would have been judged at the solid's tolerance in silence -- and judges those two kinds at `TOL_OFFSET` = 6.6e-5. **Every other kind keeps the relative-volume rule it has always had, marked in the code as unconverted, and that is deliberate:** converting a kind's own tolerance into the offset metric gives `k = tol_rel * V/(A*100U)`, which makes the new test algebraically identical to the old one -- the volume denominator returns through the threshold and nothing changes but the spelling. A per-kind number has to come from what that geometry achieves. The shells' 6.6e-5 qualifies because it was taken from what the **solid cowls** achieve, a transfer between kinds that only means something because the metric is comparable across them. **What flattening would have cost, measured:** the cowling-anchor bulkhead has A/V = 1.459 per mm, so its present 1.0e-4 relative is **6.9e-7** in the offset metric -- one number for every kind would have loosened it a hundredfold. Also changed: the cowl bounding-box floor is `COWL_EXPORT_DEFLECTION * U` rather than a fixed 0.02 mm, since IP-FC-121 made the export deflection scale and a large part would otherwise be held to a bound its own mesh cannot meet. **A measurement with no `area` recorded fails rather than falling back** to the rule its kind was moved off, which would assert an agreement nobody tested. **Verified 2026-09-06:** `kind_of` answers `tail_shell` and `nose_cowl_shell` for the shells and still answers `tail` and `nose_cowl` for the solids; the offset is 1.324e-05 for the 112.5 mm3 anomaly at `U` = 1 and 3.310e-06 for the same difference at `U` = 4; a measurement without `area` returns `None`; and on a real 36704-triangle STL the area comes out at 14895.7568 mm2 with a part measured against itself at exactly 0. **Not yet verified:** that a real comparison passes at `U` != 1, which needs `compare_backends` over the cowl kinds at several scales -- the same run IP-FC-121 is waiting on | IP-FC-120, IP-FC-121 | [mesh_stats.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/mesh_stats.py), [compare_backends.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/compare_backends.py) |
| IP-FC-123 | done | **The symmetric difference is the right volume metric and the boolean cannot compute it between two builds, so it is estimated by sampling the surface.** Built 2026-09-06. **What the boolean does:** between solids that *share* surfaces it is exact and fast -- a 0.008 mm3 cube planted wholly inside a tail wall came back as 0.008000, and a 0.001 mm3 one as 0.001000, in 2 s a half, on differences that never touch the boundary. Between two solids built *independently* it is unusable: both halves return `isValid()` false, in 9 and 7 solids, totalling 28.874616 mm3 over 1054 s for a pair whose volumes agree to 0.006186 mm3, and a planted cube turns up **in the wrong half with the wrong sign** -- the total fell by the cube's volume when material was removed. `isValid()` on the two halves is the discriminator, true in the working case and false in the failing one, so a caller can tell when it is entitled to report a number. **What replaces it:** `solid_measure.surface_difference`. The difference between two nearly coincident solids is a thin band on their shared surface and its volume is the integral of the gap, so points sampled on one surface -- uniformly by area -- with each one's distance to the other's faces estimate it, with a standard error. It integrates `|d|`, so excursions in and out **add** instead of cancelling, which is the whole objection to differencing two totals. **Each sample is projected onto its own surface first**, and that is not a refinement: a point inside a triangle sits up to the deflection off the true surface, so without it every sample carries a baseline of order the chordal error -- 4.269e-03 mm against a true mean gap of 6.2e-04, which turned an exact 21.41 mm3 into 148.08. **Calibrated 2026-09-06** on a hollow cylinder of the same surface-to-volume ratio as the wall, 3.35 against 3.58, whose answer is closed-form: eight independent seeds on a difference of 62.8331 mm3 gave a mean of 62.4327, **0.62 standard errors out**, with the scatter between seeds 1.6468 against a predicted 1.8138 -- unbiased, and the error bars honest. A first attempt reported three deltas on one seed and got relative errors of +0.031, +0.032, +0.032, identical because it was one draw repeated; **vary the seed**. **What it cannot see, measured:** a 36 mm2 bite worth 21.41 mm3 out of a 34688 mm2 surface was missed entirely by 800 samples and reported as 0.0000 +- 0.0000 -- a localised difference needs about `total area / patch area` samples before one lands on it, and until then the estimate is zero with false precision, so read `max_gap` and not only the estimate. An interior void it cannot see at all, which is exactly the case the boolean does exactly. **The two instruments fail differently and neither replaces the other**. **Applied 2026-09-06 to the two-build pair, and the answer is in [OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md):** keeping each sample's position as well as its gap localises the difference without needing a second instrument -- 65.5 % of the surface reads exactly zero, 5 % of it carries 83 % of the disagreement, and that 5 % sits at the section's corners at radius 62-66 mm, spread along the whole length rather than at the ends or a rib. Sectioning would have been the other route and could not have done it: a difference spread evenly is about 0.016 mm2 a station, under the 0.03 to 0.055 mm2 the shoelace resolves, so it could not have told *spread* from *absent* | IP-FC-119 | [solid_measure.py](../../src/Fuselage/freecad/solid_measure.py), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-124 | done | **The baseline check's identity test was a tolerance, and a tolerance cannot tell two parts apart.** Built 2026-09-06 closing [OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md). `baseline_manifest.verify` decided whether a rebuilt part was the same part by comparing volume and bounding box, and **both can agree while the shape has changed** -- move material from one side to the other and the volume difference is exactly zero. The screening pattern was adopted on the condition that the screen have no possible false negative, which that one fails. `mesh_stats.canonical_hash` meets it: each triangle is rotated so its lowest vertex comes first -- **preserving winding, where sorting the three vertices flips half of them** -- the triangles are sorted, and the result hashed. Equal hashes mean the same set of triangles, so the same geometry exactly; its errors run one way only, saying *different* about parts that agree when a last bit moves, never *same* about parts that differ. `same_geometry` consults it first and falls through to the existing tolerances only when it does not fire, so those tolerances stop being an identity test and remain what they are good at -- judging a difference already known to exist. **That it fires at all was not obvious and is the measurement that justifies it:** one bulkhead rendered into four independent trees has four different *file* hashes -- 24eef96b, c52009f0, f9eb383a, d5558215 -- and **one canonical hash**, because OpenSCAD writes the same geometry in a different facet order every run. A plain file hash would report four false differences. **Verified 2026-09-06:** the hash is unchanged when the facets are shuffled and the vertices within each rotated; it changes when one vertex moves 1 um; it changes when one facet's winding is reversed; equal hashes pass `same_geometry` even with the volume forced 50 % out; and a measurement recorded before the hash existed falls through rather than failing | IP-FC-123 | [mesh_stats.py](../../src/Fuselage/tools/mesh_stats.py), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-19](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |
| IP-FC-36 | done | **Written 2026-08-21: [dimension_scheme.md](../design/dimension_scheme.md).** OQ-ARCH-7 decided the shape of this in 2026-08-07 and named one prerequisite — *"agreement on what belongs to the interface"* — assigning it to `doc/architecture/overview.md`, **which does not exist**. The document supplies that agreement by enumeration instead of waiting for it. **The membership test is mechanical, not aesthetic**: a dimension belongs to the interface set iff another part's geometry depends on it. That turned out to have a convenient property — **the seven `tolerances` entries already enumerate the seven joints**, because each exists precisely because two parts meet somewhere, and each `why` carries the governing expression. So the register is a reading of a file the sweep already validates rather than a new list to maintain beside the code, and **the completeness test rides on it**: the set is complete when, for every tolerance, the drawing carries every dimension that entry's expression consumes. **Three things a drawing must say that a dimension alone does not** are called out: *which part carries the clearance* — joints 2 and 3 are carried entirely on the corner, so a drawing showing both sides at nominal is right about each part and wrong about the joint, and an inspector measuring the bulkhead's post against a clearance would reject a good part; *which zeros are structural* — `panel_tolerance` on a cowling bulkhead, `cowl_flange_tolerance` on every non-cowling type, `plate.tolerance` on `tail_high_open` are the **absence of the joint**, so the dimension must be omitted rather than printed as `0.0`, which would assert a coincident fit that was designed and is inspectable; and *which constraint governs* where more than one can — the corner's `flat_offset` is a `max()` of a longeron term and a panel term, **and which binds changes across the family**, so the variant table carries the number while the note has to carry which term produced it. **Three gaps in the completeness test are stated rather than left to be found**: `plate.tolerance` lives in the per-cowl-type files, so the test must read those too; joint 10 (bolt or insert to bulkhead) has **no clearance parameter at all** and a bolt in a hole and a heat-set insert in a bore are different fits; and `corner_tolerance` is 0 with its own note flagging *"if these faces are bonded, 0 leaves no bond line"* — the same question OQ-DES-CW10 answered for the nose base joint and unanswered here, so **a drawing generated today dimensions a zero-clearance fit that may be a bonded joint**. Internal structure stays reference geometry per OQ-ARCH-7, and the document says why that is not a shortcut: internal dimensions are *derived*, so dimensioning them duplicates a formula that already exists in one place. **Extended the same day with §5, the placement rules**, after review pointed out that knowing *which* dimensions to carry is only half of it: a drawing whose dimensions overlap, sit on an edge, or run off the sheet renders, exports, prints and passes every automated check, and then someone reads 12.5 as 12.6 and makes a part. Five **hard** constraints whose violation fails the build — containment, text-to-text, text-to-geometry, no witness line crossing a *dimension* line (crossing another witness line is conventional and fine), and no structurally-zero dimension placed at all — over five ranked soft costs. **Nesting by magnitude heads the soft list because it is not aesthetic**: smaller dimensions inboard is the arrangement under which witness lines never *need* to cross dimension lines, so it is what makes the hard constraint satisfiable rather than something to fight. **OQ-ARCH-7's lettered-callout-plus-value-table decision does most of the work** — placement is solved once per family against the drawn representative view, not 576 times — **but the family partitions by topology, not size**, because a callout pointing at a feature that is not there is worse than no drawing: §2's structural-zero rule reappearing at the sheet level. Only two tunables in the whole scheme, the lane gap and spacing, both scaling with text height rather than `U` since the reader's eye does not get bigger when the part does. **Placement must be byte-identical between runs** — a drawing set that cannot be diffed cannot be reviewed, and this project already has one nondeterministic generator in OpenSCAD's facet ordering. On failure the build stops and names the dimensions it could not place rather than shrinking text or emitting the overlap; the escape hatch is splitting the view, which is a drafting decision made deliberately. **The checker is independent of the placer**, because a placer that certifies its own output has only proved it is self-consistent | — | [dimension_scheme.md](../design/dimension_scheme.md), [freecad_migration.md §OQ-ARCH-7](../architecture/freecad_migration.md) |

On that view the **panel width** and **panel thickness** are dimensions, and **`panel_tolerance`** and **`corner_tolerance`** are dimension callouts. The bulkhead top view is the right carrier rather than an arbitrary one: it is the only view where all three parts share a plane -- the bulkhead's outer flat face is the panel's seating surface, and the corner's panel extension lands on the same view as a real feature, with both parts carrying a face at 32.7375 from the airframe axis. **The `corner_tolerance` callout will read 0.00 and that is the reason to put it there**: OQ-DES-C5 held the parameter at 0 and recorded the residual -- *if these faces are bonded, 0 leaves no bond line* -- so the callout puts a live question in front of whoever assembles the airframe.
